#5253. Ripple-Backed Evernorth Plunges 40% as Insiders Move to Sell 73.7 Million Shares

Ripple-backed XRP treasury company Evernorth Holdings (XRPN) is under extreme pressure as its shares have declined by over 40% while big investors like Ripple Labs move closer to potentially selling millions of shares.

Earlier this week, Evernorth filed a prospectus with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC), indicating that the XRP-focused company has registered a whopping 73,688,162 Class A shares for potential resale by existing shareholders.

Evernorth's prospectus comes as the XRP-focused company is enduring a brutal stock market debut. XRPN began trading on Nasdaq on Oct. 12.

On Nov. 10, Evernorth's stock price dropped 23.54%. XRPN ended the day at $7.96, compared to $10.41 in the previous trading session. Moreover, about 1.39 million shares were traded, which is a significant jump from about 369,000 shares on Nov. 9.

So far, Evernorth's share price has declined by around 48% from its closing price on Oct. 28 ($15.26). The magnitude of the decline is even greater if we consider the high share price on its debut day ($19.39). XRPN's stock price is down by around 59% from that high.

Some of the biggest names and companies linked to the XRP ecosystem are shareholders who have registered shares for potential sale, as per the prospectus filed with the SEC.

For instance, the biggest shareholder is Arrington XRP Capital Fund, which was a sponsor of the merger between Evernorth and Armada Acquisition Corp. II. It has about 33.82 million shares registered for possible resale.

Similarly, the second-largest shareholder is Ripple Labs, which has about 18.14 million shares registered. Then there are various entities linked to SBI Holdings, a big Japanese financial institution. They have 11.79 million shares registered, as per the prospectus filed with the SEC.

The Larsen Lam Children's Remainder Trust, which is linked to Ripple co-founder Chris Larsen, has about 7.15 million shares registered for possible sale.

Other names mentioned in the filing include Pantera Capital, GSR Markets Switzerland and Kraken parent firm Payward.

It is important to understand that the fact that they've registered shares for sale doesn't mean that these investors have sold their holdings or necessarily plan to sell them.

For example, Ripple, the Arrington-sponsored entity and a Larsen-linked entity are all subject to six-month lock-up agreements following the completion of Evernorth's business combination on Oct. 9.

These lock-up agreements are typically set to expire on April 9, 2027, with some exceptions.

#5252. Bitcoin Crash Coming? Analyst Revisits 2014, 2018, and 2022 Pattern Ahead Of Midterm Elections

Bitcoin (BTC) could face another significant correction as the November 3 U.S. midterm elections approach, with analysts pointing to a historical pattern that has coincided with major BTC price declines during previous election cycles.

With the 2026 midterm elections just days away, market observers are examining Bitcoin’s performance around the 2014, 2018, and 2022 elections to see whether another downturn is approaching.

Although the historical similarities do not establish that elections directly influence cryptocurrency prices, the repeated appearance of major corrections during these periods has raised questions about Bitcoin’s near-term direction.

According to crypto analytics platform Alphractal, Bitcoin was already experiencing bearish conditions before each of the previous three midterm elections. However, selling pressure continued after Election Day, with the cryptocurrency eventually reaching significant cycle lows.

Looking back at the 2014 U.S. midterm elections held on November 4, Bitcoin was already caught in an extended bear market. The cryptocurrency continued declining in the weeks that followed, eventually reaching its cycle bottom in January 2015.

A similar development occurred in 2018. Bitcoin was trading around $6,400 when Americans headed to the polls on November 6. Although the market initially experienced a period of relative stability, BTC subsequently suffered a sharp decline, losing approximately 40% over the following month before establishing its cycle bottom in December.

Meanwhile, during the 2022 election cycle, Bitcoin had already experienced considerable losses by the time the November 8 elections took place. The collapse of cryptocurrency exchange FTX occurred around the same period, triggering another wave of selling that pushed BTC toward $15,500 and marked the bottom of that market cycle.

According to Alphractal, these three occurrences raise an interesting question about whether Bitcoin’s performance has any meaningful relationship with the U.S. electoral calendar.

The analytics platform emphasized that Bitcoin was already in a bear market ahead of all three elections. Consequently, the subsequent declines could have reflected broader cryptocurrency market conditions and the asset’s four-year cycle rather than the elections themselves.

Additionally, popular analyst Ali Martinez highlighted Bitcoin’s historical performance across four U.S. midterm election cycles.

According to Martinez, BTC recorded significant declines around the 2010, 2014, 2018, and 2022 midterm elections, with reported losses of 72%, 65%, 52%, and 27%, respectively.

The analyst considers this repeated downside performance a pattern worth monitoring as another election approaches on November 3.

Furthermore, Martinez pointed to Bitcoin’s fourth-quarter returns during previous midterm election years. His figures show that BTC gained 391% in the fourth quarter of 2010, while the cryptocurrency recorded losses of 16.70% in 2014, 42.16% in 2018, and 14.75% in 2022.

The figures suggest that three of the four election-year fourth quarters ended negatively. However, the 2010 rally stands out as an exception, particularly because Bitcoin was still in its early stages of development.

For the upcoming election, Martinez identified $73,000 as an important price level to watch. He pointed to Bitcoin’s short-term holder cost basis, which represents the average acquisition price of coins held by relatively recent market participants.

According to his assessment, this level could become an important support area if selling pressure increases following the elections. A recovery from that region would potentially strengthen the bullish outlook, while continued weakness could signal further deterioration in market conditions.

Beyond the historical election pattern, technical analyst Crypto Patel has also raised concerns about Bitcoin’s immediate price direction, suggesting another substantial decline is possible.

In a recent market update, the pundit said Bitcoin had already fallen about 12% from his previously identified short-entry area. He explained that his bearish outlook originated from technical chart analysis rather than negative headlines or developments affecting the cryptocurrency industry.

The analyst based his assessment on Smart Money Concepts (SMC), which examines market structure, liquidity movements, and price action to identify potential trading opportunities.

Notably, Patel explained that Bitcoin’s price structure had produced a higher high, leading him to anticipate a subsequent move toward a higher low. He also highlighted the removal of inducement liquidity and a higher-timeframe candle closing below the relevant inducement level as additional bearish signals.

Regarding his latest projections, he identified $66,000 to $62,000 as his potential downside target, provided Bitcoin remains below $81,950.

However, the analyst also established a level that would invalidate his short-term bearish outlook. According to Patel, a higher-timeframe candle closing above $84,870 would prompt him to reconsider his position and return to a bullish stance, with expectations of a move beyond $100,000.

These projections suggest that Bitcoin could experience additional volatility as traders assess the market’s technical structure alongside the approaching U.S. elections.

At press time, BTC was trading at $71,454, down 3.92% over the past 24 hours.

#5251. Solana (SOL), Gram (GRAM), Hyperliquid (HYPE) and Bitcoin (BTC) Price Analysis for November 12: It Is a Bloodbath on the Market

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Solana is currently trading at $83.66. The price is back in the area where its August rally started.

The fall in Solana's price that we are witnessing now is not a normal correction. The price has lost more than 30% of its value since September, when it traded at $125. Usually, corrections stop at around 10% for volatile assets, including cryptocurrencies. Here, the situation is different.

By the end of October, Solana dropped below the black moving average on the chart, which represents the 200-day moving average, suggesting a long-term change of direction. The short-term moving averages crossing below the longer-term ones suggest the bearish trend is getting worse.

The upcoming support is at approximately $80, with a lower support at $72-$78. That range is where SOL spent the whole of July, before breaking out in mid-August. The $80 level is unlikely to hold under such severe pressure, so traders shouldn't be surprised to see SOL trading around $70.

With the spike in trading volume on November 10, we pretty much have confirmation that the downtrend is continuing.

Gram is trading at $1.244 after briefly dipping below $1 on November 9.

Fueled by Pavel Durov's comments on X, Gram received a substantial boost: 24% in three days, with the most recent daily candle showing a 9% surge.

The bounce started thanks to the Telegram wallet updates and the further integration of blockchain technology directly into the messenger, along with speculative buying. Durov states that Telegram users created more than 700,000 Gram wallets in the last 48 hours.

Record-breaking volume on November 10 could reflect concentrated buying, meaning that someone or some group targeted GRAM and put funds into it directly.

The price has already moved above the short green moving average, at around $1.21. Unfortunately, resistance is quite steep: the October-November trailing resistance is at around $1.25. Other moving averages present a chance for bears to play around $1.34 and $1.46.

In late September, GRAM spiked towards $1.73, but failed to hold that momentum and quickly tumbled back below the black moving average.

The current bounce is mixed: both speculative and fundamental factors are fueling it, so its future is somewhat unclear.

Despite positive developments around Hyperliquid, such as listings on South Korean exchanges on November 10 and active buybacks, HYPE is diving. The asset lost around 14% over the week.

Additionally, Hyperliquid saw a bounce to $97 after the October 29 announcement about Hyperliquid-based futures in the US. Unfortunately, it hasn't helped, with HYPE down almost $25 since then.

From a price perspective, the only positive outcome is the breakthrough of the 200 EMA back in August, which might become support at some point, though that has not happened yet.

Currently, Hyperliquid is stuck in the $70-$73 range. It seems unlikely to break out until it hits the 200 EMA at around $68 and bounces off it.

Bitcoin is trading at $73,535, down 16% from its September peak. Unfortunately, the market is brutal right now and clearly shows a serious level of pressure.

Bitcoin spot ETFs have lost around $1.6 billion since the beginning of November, though the dynamic is improving. According to Bloomberg's Eric Balchunas, only stock funds saw the biggest inflows, which suggests a continuing trend of outflows from digital gold in favor of more traditional assets.

A better time to buy Bitcoin could be tied to an overall market recovery, and therefore to a better market catalyst, which doesn't exist at this point.

The Anthropic IPO, growing demand for memory chips, and the rapid improvement of AI push investors away from the cryptocurrency industry, which has been in pain for the last two years, lacking catalysts, safety and use cases.

SpaceX shares rose 0.40% in premarket trading on Friday to $140.50, after closing Thursday at $139.94, as investors look ahead to the company’s most ambitious Starship test yet. Flight 15 is set to launch at 22:45 GMT on Friday, or 4:15 am IST on Saturday, from Starbase in Texas. SpaceX plans to catch the Super Heavy booster with its launch tower minutes after liftoff, something it has done three times before. For the first time, it will also try to catch the Starship upper stage about 22 hours later, after the spacecraft circles Earth roughly 14 times and releases 26 Starlink V3 satellites. The catch will only go ahead if the ship passes safety checks, otherwise it will splash down in the Pacific. As of Friday morning, the FAA had not yet issued a launch licence, Reuters reported. Wall Street’s average price target stands at $246.30, though some investors prefer to wait for a pullback given SpaceX’s heavy spending.

#5249. Bitcoin Price Slides Toward $73K as Nvidia Plunges 9% and MSCI Drops Saylor’s Strategy

BTC slips as Nvidia sinks 9% after hours and MSCI drops Strategy. ETFs lost $1.88B in November, 94% of it from IBIT.

In Bitcoin news, the coin fell from $74,811 to $73,480 within two hours of the U.S. close. Bitcoin slipped overnight after two shocks hit U.S. markets after Wednesday’s close. Nvidia shares fell about 9% in after-hours trading on a new report about CEO Jensen Huang. Index provider MSCI also confirmed it will remove Strategy, the largest corporate Bitcoin holder, from its global indexes.

It trades at $73,588 on Binance on Thursday morning, down 0.4% in 24 hours and 5.8% in a week.

The funds are not helping. U.S. spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) lost $299.8 million on Wednesday, their fifth straight day of outflows. Nasdaq futures are down 0.9%.

Bitcoin ETFs Shed $300 Million, Ether Funds Lose $145 Million

According to SoSoValue, U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $300 million in net outflows on November 11, while Morgan Stanley's MSBT led individual fund inflows with $8.96 million. Spot Ethereum ETFs saw $145 million in… pic.twitter.com/5sn88B2JQX

— Wu Blockchain (@WuBlockchain) November 12, 2026

So what happened, and how much of the selling comes from the ETFs?

The first hit came from Nvidia, the world’s most valuable chipmaker. The New York Times reported on Wednesday evening that Huang had a second relationship with a senior employee who reports to him. The Wall Street Journal reported that Nvidia’s board is preparing to remove him as CEO.

Nvidia had closed the day up 2.9% at $173.73. Within two hours, it traded at $157.99, down 9.1%. It is still down 8.6% before Thursday’s open. Nvidia has lost about 15% since last Friday. It first fell on Monday, after its board opened a separate probe into reports that Huang was the target of a foreign intelligence effort.

Bitcoin has traded closely with tech stocks this year, and it fell with them. Nasdaq 100 futures dropped 1.1% in the same two hours.

The second hit is closer to crypto. MSCI, whose indexes guide trillions of dollars in index funds, will delete Strategy from its global indexes after the market close on November 30. It ruled that Strategy looks more like an investment fund holding Bitcoin than an operating business. Japan’s Metaplanet and Upexi are also being removed.

Funds that track those indexes will have to sell their Strategy shares. JPMorgan earlier estimated that selling at about $2.8 billion. Strategy says the funds hold about 13 million shares, 3.1% of its stock. Its shares are down 1.8% before the open.

For Bitcoin news, that matters beyond one stock. Strategy is the steadiest corporate buyer of Bitcoin. Fewer index buyers for its shares could make it harder and more costly to raise money for new purchases.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best Low-Cap Crypto

The ETF selling started before either shock. To see how big the ETF side of this Bitcoin news is, we added up the daily net flows of the U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs for every trading day in November.

The streak is not a record yet. The longest run of outflows this year lasted 13 trading days in May and June, just before Bitcoin fell below $60,000.

The Fed adds to the pressure. October’s hot inflation report on Tuesday pushed the odds of a December rate hike on Polymarket to about 97%. Higher rates make cash and bonds pay more, which tends to pull money out of funds like these.

EXPLORE: Why Is Crypto Crashing Today? Bitcoin Falls Below $73K as Zcash (ZEC) Leads Crypto Losses

BTC trades at $73,588, holding above Tuesday’s low of $72,727, its lowest price since August 20. On the daily chart, where each candle covers one day, it sits below both of its exponential moving averages (EMAs), averages that give recent prices more weight. The 50-day EMA stands at $78,696 and the 200-day EMA at $76,422.

The next Bitcoin news to watch is Thursday’s ETF flow data and how Nvidia trades at the open. A sixth day of outflows and a weak tech session would put $72,727 to the test.

#5248. Kraken Parent Payward Brings 200 UK and EU Stocks Onchain as xStocks

Kraken Parent Payward Brings 200 UK and EU Stocks Onchain as xStocks

Payward, the parent company of crypto exchange Kraken, has tokenized more than 200 UK and European stocks through its xStocks platform, it announced on Wednesday.

The first batch covers some of the most actively traded companies listed in the UK and the European Union, with more to be added on a rolling basis. Like other xStocks, each token is backed 1:1 by the underlying share, which is bought and held by a regulated custodian for as long as the token trades, according to Payward.

Payward's @xStocksFi is expanding beyond US tokenized equities.

In partnership with @GTNglobal, more than 200 of the UK and EU’s largest equities are now tokenized by xStocks, including HSBC, Shell, LVMH and Airbus.

The first international launch, with more markets to follow.…

The tokens are available to trade on Kraken and through other platforms in the xStocks Alliance, and on blockchains where xStocks already trade, including Solana, Ethereum and TON. They are offered in more than 110 markets, but remain unavailable to investors in the UK.

"A portfolio should not have borders or trading hours," said Mark Greenberg, Payward's chief commercial officer. "Tokenizing every other international market is the next step."

The launch takes xStocks past 1,500 tokenized stocks, ETFs and IPOs. The platform holds more than $1.1 billion in assets, has processed over $45 billion in total transaction volume, and has more than 550,000 holders, Payward said.

The new tokens are the first delivered under Payward's partnership with GTN, a brokerage infrastructure firm that handles execution and custody of the underlying shares. When the two partnered in July, Payward said it planned to start with Hong Kong-listed stocks; hundreds of Hong Kong listings will now follow in the coming weeks, the company said.

The expansion also delivers on part of Payward's partnership with the London Stock Exchange, announced in September, under which Payward agreed to tokenize the 100 largest London-listed companies. Subject to regulatory approval, the exchange intends to list xStocks and begin trading them on LSE 24, its planned near-24-hour trading venue, in 2027.

Payward has been drawing interest from traditional exchanges. Nasdaq agreed to invest $100 million in the company at a $21 billion valuation in September, building on a March partnership to bring tokenized stocks to market in 2027, while Deutsche Börse bought a $200 million stake in April.

The company has also been expanding through acquisitions, agreeing to buy derivatives exchange Bitnomial for $550 million and Lithuania's Saldo Bank as part of a push into the EU.

#5247. Kevin O'Leary Reveals What's Really Behind Crypto Market Crash

Renowned investor and Shark Tank personality Kevin O’Leary has shared his thoughts on the current cryptocurrency crash, stating that the crypto industry faces issues that extend far beyond Bitcoin's dropping price.

In particular, Mr. Wonderful, as O'Leary is better known, believes that one of the biggest issues the crypto industry faces is the lack of regulatory clarity in the United States. This prevents big financial firms from adopting blockchain and stablecoin technologies.

According to O'Leary, institutional crypto adoption has slowed to a crawl. Companies are unwilling to invest any capital until there is clarity around regulations.

"It's not just about Bitcoin in the crypto sell-off. The adoption of stablecoins and blockchain within institutions has slowed down. Companies are waiting for clarity on regulations," O'Leary wrote.

The billionaire investor shared that his company already utilizes Circle's USDC for treasury management and international settlements. However, many other companies are afraid to follow suit since their compliance teams aren't sure about the regulatory landscape.

"We use USDC for treasury management and cross-border settlement. But many others won't do it till their compliance guys know the rules," O'Leary added.

The famous investor focused on two major U.S. bills regarding cryptocurrency: the GENIUS Act and the CLARITY Act.

"We got the GENIUS Act going. We need the CLARITY Act for the rest," Kevin O'Leary concluded.

O'Leary has long raised concerns about the crypto space and the reasons why Bitcoin is not seeing more institutional investment yet.

For example, on Oct. 28, the billionaire investor named two main issues affecting Bitcoin. The first was regulatory uncertainty, and the second was the rising threat of quantum computing.

The global space economy is entering a new era and Space Exploration Technologies Corp. (NASDAQ: SPCX) is aiming to be at its center. Despite retreating nearly 50% from its 52-week high of $225.64, the company’s long-term growth narrative remains intact, driven by reusable launch technology, expanding satellite infrastructure, and next-generation space innovation.

As governments and private capital accelerate investments beyond Earth, investors are asking one question: Could this correction be the opportunity before SPCXs next major rally? Here’s our SPCX stock price prediction for 2026-2040.

SPCX stock is building one of the world’s most integrated space infrastructure businesses, combining commercial launch services, satellite connectivity, national security contracts, and next-generation space technologies. As demand for global connectivity and orbital infrastructure accelerates, the company is positioning itself at the forefront of the expanding space economy.

SPCX is building far more than a launch company—it is establishing a vertically integrated space infrastructure ecosystem with multiple long-term revenue engines. From commercial launch services and satellite communications to government missions and next-generation space technologies, the company is positioned to benefit from the accelerating commercialization of space. This diversified business model reduces reliance on any single segment while creating multiple pathways for sustained earnings growth.

The long-term investment thesis now rests on execution. Continued growth in launch cadence, expansion of satellite connectivity services, deeper government and defense partnerships, and disciplined capital deployment could significantly strengthen SpaceX’s competitive position over the coming decade. As global investment in the space economy continues to accelerate, the company’s technology leadership and expanding infrastructure platform have the potential to support a higher long-term valuation than traditional aerospace peers.

3. Can SpaceX stock reclaim its 52-week high?

Yes. If SpaceX continues expanding its launch business, strengthens its satellite ecosystem, and delivers consistent financial growth, SPCX could retest and potentially surpass its previous 52-week high of $225.64 over the coming years.

4. What factors could drive SPCX stock higher?

Key catalysts include higher commercial launch activity, growth in satellite communications, additional government and defense contracts, technological innovation, improving financial performance, and the continued expansion of the global space economy.

5. What are the biggest risks for SpaceX stock?

SpaceX faces risks from execution delays, rising competition, regulatory challenges, macroeconomic uncertainty, and broader equity market volatility. Any slowdown in launch activity or commercial growth could also weigh on investor sentiment.

The no-code platform gives every AI agent its own wallet and on-chain rules, so agents can act on their own without putting their owners’ funds at risk

JuvrixAI, a no-code platform for building AI agents on NEAR Protocol, has started the presale of its utility token, $JUX. The project tackles a central question of the AI agent era: how to let agents act autonomously, including spending money, while their owners stay in control.

AI agents become truly useful when they can finish tasks: finding the data, choosing the tool, paying for the service. But autonomy and payments create a trade-off.

Giving an agent direct access to a wallet or card means full spending power and full risk. Approving every payment manually keeps funds safe but removes the autonomy that makes agents valuable. And since agents can be manipulated through prompt injection, telling an agent to “be careful” is not a reliable safeguard.

Because the contract enforces the rules, a tricked agent still cannot exceed its limits or pay an unapproved recipient.

#5241. Morning Minute: What Anthropic’s $1.4 Trillion IPO Means for Crypto

Morning Minute: What Anthropic’s $1.4 Trillion IPO Means for Crypto

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

💵 What Anthropic’s $1.4 Trillion IPO Means for Crypto

Several key new details for Anthropic’s pending IPO were released on Tuesday. The IPO price range was set at $70 to $78 a share, selling 825 million Class A shares to raise about $64.4 billion (at the top of the range). Pricing is expected November 18, with trading starting November 19 on Nasdaq under ANT. Roughly 20% of the deal is set aside for retail, which is about double what a book this size usually gives individual investors.

Notably on Hyperliquid, ANT is trading at $1.8T with $100M in daily trading volume and $200M in open interest (a big enough market to provide some signal). So perhaps traders are a bit more bullish on ANT price action than Wall Street.

Alongside the IPO sits a $26 billion private placement of non-voting Class N stock, bought at the IPO price by seven of Anthropic’s own compute suppliers. Google and Nvidia take about $10 billion each, AMD $2.5 billion, Broadcom, Micron and Microsoft about $1 billion apiece, TSMC $500 million. Those shares carry no lock-up. Combined with the IPO, Anthropic walks away with roughly $84.6 billion for general corporate purposes, mostly compute.

Regarding financials, Anthropic just reported revenue of $33.2 billion through nine months against $2.5 billion a year earlier and a $1.26 billion operating loss. It reported a $104 billion net loss, almost all of it a non-cash charge on convertible notes. And $900.9 billion in committed spending, with $70.7 billion due inside twelve months.

The liquidity drain thesis has been circulating since April, when CoinDesk laid out the case that mega-IPOs pull speculative money from the same pool that funds crypto. Retail allocations are the mechanism. A dollar earmarked for an IPO ticket is a dollar not in Bitcoin or memecoins.

We already ran this experiment. SpaceX listed June 12 at more than $2 trillion. Bitcoin fell 29% going into it, bottomed at $58,500 on June 30, and then rose 37% off that low. But notably, the Bitcoin bottom was effectively in on June 5 at $59k, a full week ahead of the SPCX IPO.

So the question is not whether an IPO pulls money out. It’s whether that already happened. Bitcoin fell roughly 15% from its early-October high through a $6.3 billion exchange hack, an AI capex selloff and a Democratic sweep.

Two things make this deal smaller than it looks for crypto. The retail slice is roughly $12.9 billion against SpaceX’s $22 billion, about 59% as much. And the $26 billion private placement never touches public market liquidity at all, because it comes from Anthropic’s vendors, who then get paid back in compute contracts.

Thus, if the SpaceX pattern repeats, and with the ~60% factor, we could expect BTC to drop another 5% or so into the Anthropic IPO (notably, that’s right where the 200-week moving average is). And we would expect the effective bottom to be in sometime in the next 1-2 days.

The good news is, the pain is mostly behind us, in that scenario. We will find out soon enough if IPO history repeats itself for crypto…

Until everything comes together, the end of the AI trade and a rapid transition of funds to crypto looks very unlikely.

#5239. Bitcoin Price Crashes to $72,700 as Leveraged Traders Buy the Dip: Is the Bottom In?

The crypto market crash deepens as $1B+ in longs get liquidated. Bitcoin sits mid-range at $72,969; fear at 28 isn’t a bottom yet.

Bitcoin fell below $73,000 on Thursday, November 12, extending a week-long crypto market crash in which traders who bet on a rebound were forced out of their positions again and again.

Bitcoin touched $72,735 on Binance, a hair above Tuesday’s $72,727 low and its weakest price since August 20. It trades at $72,969, down 9.4% from the November 4 close.

BREAKING: $BTC just fell to its lowest level since August.

Over $210 million worth of longs liquidated in the past 24 hours.

The dumping continues… pic.twitter.com/R7NVGivFnD

— Crypto Rover (@cryptorover) November 12, 2026

The altcoins are doing worse. Over seven days, Ethereum has lost 16.4%, Solana 14.7% and XRP 21.3%. CoinGecko values the whole crypto market at $2.42 trillion on Thursday, and Bitcoin makes up 60.5% of it.

Leverage made every drop bigger. More than $1 billion of bets were liquidated on Monday alone, almost all of them longs, the positions that profit only if prices rise. Liquidation means the exchange closes a trade by force once the trader’s collateral runs out, and those forced sales push the price down further.

So is this the flush that ends the crypto market slide, or is there more to come? The data has an answer, and it is not a comforting one.

The sell-off had no single cause. Four pieces of bad news landed one after another:

Each hit forced out more leveraged longs across the crypto market. Thursday’s slide continued the pattern on a smaller scale. We added up every forced closure in Bitcoin, Ether, Solana, XRP and Dogecoin futures on OKX, one of the largest exchanges, over the past 24 hours. Longs lost $27.6 million, against $12.3 million for shorts.

EXPLORE: Bitcoin Price Slides Toward $73K as Nvidia Plunges 9% and MSCI Drops Saylor’s Strategy

Big liquidations usually clear out the crowd. This time, the crowd came back.

In plain terms, traders keep buying every dip with borrowed money. That leaves fuel for another round of forced selling if Bitcoin drops through the next support. Bottoms tend to come when funding turns negative and open interest falls hard, a sign that leveraged buyers have given up.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Next Crypto to Hit 1 Dollar

The Crypto Fear & Greed Index, which scores market mood from 0 (extreme fear) to 100 (extreme greed), reads 28 today. Fear is usually read as a buy signal. The history says it is not enough on its own.

We checked every day since February 2018 when the index sat at 28 or lower, 943 days in all, and where Bitcoin stood later.

This year’s two bottoms came with real panic. When Bitcoin hit $60,000 on February 6, the index read 9. When it bottomed at $57,800 on July 1, it read 11. Today’s 28 is fear, but it is not capitulation, the moment sellers give up all at once.

On the weekly chart, where each candle covers one week, Bitcoin has spent 2026 in a wide range. The low is $57,800 from July 1 and the high is $87,396 from late September. The midpoint is $72,598, almost exactly where Bitcoin trades now.

The price lost its 50-week exponential moving average (EMA), an average that gives recent prices more weight, at $78,219. Its 200-week EMA sits at $69,597. Bitcoin closed below that line for 16 weeks this year, in February and March and again from June to mid-August, and both of the year’s bottoms formed during those stretches.

For the crypto market, three signs would say the bottom is close: the fear index dropping toward the teens, funding turning negative, and open interest falling as longs give up. None has shown up yet. Until they do, the 200-week EMA at $69,597 is the line that separates a pullback inside the 2026 range from a retest of the summer lows.

#5238. Ripple’s Bank-Style Lending on XRPL Nears Launch: What XRP Holders Actually Get

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The LendingProtocolV1_1 amendment on the XRP Ledger (XRPL) has collected 22 of 35 votes (62.86%).

Three more validators have signed on over the past three weeks. Ripple's own nodes have already voted yes, along with the University of Waterloo and UC Berkeley.

To go live, the amendment has to clear the 80% bar (29 votes) and hold it for two straight weeks.

The related SingleAssetVault amendment (XLS-65) has 23 votes (65.7%) and needs six more.

The XLS-65 and XLS-66 standards drop overcollateralization and instant liquidations and let companies take out fixed-term, uncollateralized loans directly on XRPL. The loans work similarly to traditional bank credit, with a fixed term and pre-set interest terms.

Ripple has already tested the protocol on a private XRPL network with nine nodes that matched the hardware of its mainnet nodes.

Holders deposit XRP or stablecoins such as RLUSD into Single Asset Vaults and receive shares. Interest is paid on a cash basis. Depositors receive it only after the borrower has sent the money. Withdrawals from a vault are processed on a first-come, first-served basis.

Borrowers are vetted off-chain by Loan Brokers, who record each disbursement on the ledger. According to the XRPL documentation, the protocol "relies on off-chain underwriting and risk management to assess the creditworthiness of borrowers."

The broker's own "first-loss capital" covers losses first when a client stops paying.

Issuers can also freeze assets or claw them back. According to Ripple, all operations in a vault and in the connected lending protocol stop when an asset issuer freezes the vault.

Until now, XRP holders who wanted passive income had to use third-party bridges and wrapped tokens. After the amendment activates, holders can deposit XRP in a pool on XRPL, with its fraction-of-a-cent fees, and collect part of the interest paid by borrowers. Their income will depend on the terms set by the loan broker and on borrowers' repayments.

If a major borrower defaults and the broker's first-loss capital runs out, depositors bear the losses and their shares drop in value.

This Bitcoin price prediction breaks down the technical structure, key catalysts and potential BTC targets through 2026, 2030 and beyond.

On the upside, Bitcoin needs to reclaim the $75,950 region to improve its short-term technical structure. A stronger recovery above $80,000 would provide a more convincing signal that buyers are regaining control.

For now, $75,000 remains the key level to watch. A recovery supported by stronger spot buying would improve the outlook, while continued derivatives-led volatility could make the support zone harder to defend.

Bitcoin’s price structure in 2026 points toward a transition year, where the market is gradually shifting from consolidation into expansion rather than entering a fresh bearish phase.

The first key trigger remains the $75K–$90K range. A sustained reclaim of this zone would indicate strengthening momentum, allowing BTC to move toward the $100K–$110K region, where the next resistance is likely to emerge. If price stabilizes above this level, it would confirm a shift out of the current range, opening the path toward the $120K–$130K zone in the later part of the year.

At the same time, external uncertainties continue to keep the upside controlled. Periodic spikes in geopolitical tensions, sudden liquidity shifts, and risk-off reactions across global markets are creating intermittent pressure, preventing immediate breakout continuation. This is one of the key reasons why Bitcoin, despite holding strong support, is still struggling to trend decisively.

However, every dip toward lower levels is being absorbed, suggesting that the market is building a base rather than weakening. This kind of structure typically forms before expansion, especially when downside follow-through remains limited. On the downside, failure to hold the $62K support zone could trigger a temporary correction toward the $58K–$60K region. But unless this level breaks decisively, the broader structure remains intact.

Overall, 2026 is shaping up as a rebuilding and controlled expansion phase, where Bitcoin is stabilizing under external pressure while gradually preparing for its next major move.

Bitcoin’s on-chain structure is strengthening as the price recovers toward $85,000, with two signals pointing to a tighter supply environment. Exchange reserves have fallen to 2.68 million BTC, the lowest level shown on the supplied chart since at least September 2023. More importantly, reserves have continued declining across multiple price cycles, suggesting that the reduction in exchange-held supply is not simply a short-term reaction to price movements.

At the same time, short-term holders have rebuilt a $43.8 billion unrealized profit cushion, with STH market capitalization at $333.9 billion versus $290.1 billion in realized capitalization. That puts the cohort roughly 15% above its realized value, reversing the unrealized-loss position seen when Bitcoin traded closer to $60,000. The recovery indicates that recent buyers are back in aggregate profit as BTC approaches $85,000.

Taken together, the data presents a constructive on-chain backdrop: exchange-held supply is shrinking while short-term holder profitability is recovering. The key question for the next phase is whether reserves continue falling as BTC moves higher, or whether a stronger rally encourages previously withdrawn coins to return to exchanges and increases available selling supply.

Alex, CTO of MyDoge Wallet, announced the update, explaining that the team had pushed an interim fix to resolve the reported BTC transaction problems before the broader software release.

Pushed an update to quickly address BTC swap/send issues while using "Classic Wallet" before we release the full v3.1.2 this weekend Simply close / reopen the app and you should see: 3.1.1-652.1 or 3.1.1-652.2 in the "About MyDoge" settings

In October, DogeOS opened a public test network for Ethereum-style trading, lending and other applications using DOGE, seeking to give Dogecoin holders more ways to use their coins beyond payments and price speculation.

In a recent post on X, Dogecoin Foundation director of product Timothy Stebbing shared a Dogecoin ecosystem 2026 update.

Stebbing predicts that the next three to four years for Dogecoin are likely to be shaped more by the development and adoption of smart-contract application layers than by large, pre-emptive changes to Dogecoin’s base protocol. He cited DogeOS as an early and visible example of that direction. If it and similar efforts succeed in attracting sustained developer activity and real usage while continuing to treat Dogecoin as the settlement and value-transfer foundation, Stebbing sees the ecosystem gaining additional utility without compromising the simplicity and reliability of the Layer 1.

However, the proposed mechanism for native proof verification on Dogecoin still requires community discussion and, ultimately, activation. Thus, adoption will depend on whether the resulting applications are useful enough that people choose to use them. None of these uncertainties change the underlying architectural advantage: the Layer 1 can remain a focused means of exchange while the surrounding ecosystem gains the capacity for richer functionality.

#5233. Why Is XRP Bleeding More Than Bitcoin, Ether, and Solana in November Sell-Off? Analyst Cites 3 Brutal Reasons

While Bitcoin, Ethereum, and Solana have also come under bearish pressure during November’s broader crypto market sell-off, XRP is taking a particularly brutal beating, leaving investors wondering why the token is underperforming its biggest rivals. According to prominent analyst EGRAG Crypto, the weakness is no coincidence, pointing to three key factors that could explain XRP’s sharper decline and signal why the token may continue struggling as bearish sentiment grips the market.

Crypto analyst EGRAG Crypto has highlighted XRP’s worsening performance against Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH), and Solana (SOL) amid November’s broader cryptocurrency market drawdown. In a Nov. 10 post on X, the pundit noted the striking difference in losses since November 4, showing that Ripple’s XRP has suffered the steepest decline among the four major cryptocurrencies.

According to EGRAG Crypto’s figures, XRP has plunged 23.4% since November 4, significantly more than ETH’s 18.2% decline and SOL’s 17% drop. BTC has demonstrated greater resilience, losing just 10.4% over the same period.

The figures raise an important question: Why is XRP bleeding considerably more than its major competitors? EGRAG Crypto has pointed to three possible factors behind the token’s disproportionate weakness, offering a closer look at the selling pressure weighing on the Ripple-linked token.

The first factor is leveraged long liquidations, which EGRAG assigns a 30% probability. According to the analyst, forced liquidations of bullish positions, combined with weak buying demand, could be accelerating XRP’s fall. When leveraged traders are forced to close their positions during a downturn, the resulting selling pressure can trigger further losses and deepen an existing market correction.

The second possibility, which carries a 20% probability, is selling by early XRP holders. EGRAG suggests that longtime investors, often referred to as OGs, could be taking profits and rotating their capital into other cryptocurrencies. Such activity could increase selling pressure on XRP, particularly if large holders decide to reduce their exposure amid uncertain market conditions.

Finally, EGRAG assigns the highest probability, 50%, to what the expert describes as market manipulation or a deliberate liquidity hunt. Under this scenario, sharp price movements could be designed to trigger stop-loss orders and force weaker traders out of their positions before a potential recovery. The analyst believes this could explain why XRP is experiencing disproportionately heavy selling compared with other major cryptocurrencies.

Notably, these percentages represent EGRAG Crypto’s personal probability-based assessment rather than independently verified evidence of the causes behind XRP’s decline.

Beyond identifying the possible reasons behind XRP’s sharper decline, EGRAG Crypto has also highlighted several important price levels that could determine the token’s next move. The analyst identified the $1.00-$1.04 range as a critical stabilization zone, suggesting that holding this area could help XRP establish a floor amid the ongoing selling pressure.

On the upside, EGRAG believes bulls must first reclaim the $1.15 level before targeting $1.25. A recovery above these thresholds could signal improving buying momentum and offer some relief to investors after the recent losses.

Sharing a personal assessment, EGRAG Crypto said the ongoing price action appears more consistent with market manipulation designed to frighten newer investors into selling. However, the analyst raised a bigger question about whale activity, questioning whether deep-pocketed XRP holders are using the downturn to accumulate more tokens or adding to the selling pressure by offloading their holdings.

Ultimately, how whales respond to the current market weakness could offer important clues about XRP’s next direction. If large holders are absorbing the available supply, the sell-off could eventually lose momentum. Conversely, continued whale selling could make an already difficult recovery even harder for the sixth-largest cryptocurrency.

#5231. Decrypt Becomes First News Publisher to Offer Perps Trading, Powered by Solana's Phoenix

Decrypt Becomes First News Publisher to Offer Perps Trading, Powered by Solana's Phoenix

What happens when the place you read the news becomes the place you act on it?

The news that moves markets and the tools to trade them have long lived in separate places. Decrypt is bringing them together.

Decrypt is integrating perpetual futures trading, powered by Phoenix, a fully onchain perpetuals exchange on Solana, making it the first media platform to offer perps trading directly alongside its journalism.

Readers will be able to take long or short positions on crypto, stocks and commodities from the same place they follow the news that moves them, without switching apps, connecting external wallets or moving funds between platforms.

"Information influences decisions, shapes opinions and moves markets," said Ilan Hazan, co-founder and COO of Decrypt. "A reader might discover an opportunity through a publication, develop a view and then leave to act on it somewhere else. The publisher monetizes the impression, while someone else monetizes the transaction. It means the publisher captures only a small part of the economic activity that its information and distribution generates."

Rather than treating media and financial products as separate businesses, he said, Decrypt is bringing them together. "We're allowing readers to go from understanding what's happening in the markets to taking a position, without leaving Decrypt.”

"Partnering with Decrypt closes the gap between reading the news and acting on it," said Kabir Sethi, Head of Business Development at Phoenix. "With over 100 Phoenix perpetuals markets built directly into Decrypt, readers can take in a story, form a view, and trade it in the same place. We are excited to help play a part in transforming Decrypt into a true information exchange."

Perpetual futures, or perps, are contracts that let traders bet on an asset's price rising or falling without an expiry date, typically using leverage. Leverage magnifies both gains and losses, and positions can be liquidated quickly when markets move against them.

Phoenix offers markets in crypto, U.S. stocks, gold and pre-IPO companies around the clock, with up to 40x leverage and low fees, according to its website. It is developed by Ellipsis Labs, a team that has built exclusively on Solana and whose CEO, Eugene Chen, has described perps as having "crazy product market fit."

"These are very, very strong, high revenue businesses, probably some of the best in the industry," Chen said of perps exchanges in a podcast interview with Blockworks' Lightspeed earlier this year. Phoenix routes each trade at the moment it lands onchain, splitting it between its order book and market makers "based on what's going to give you the best price," he explained.

Perps trading runs through the Decrypt Money Account, a single self-custodial account on Solana designed to work without seed phrases, gas fees or bridging.

One balance now funds it all: prediction markets on Decrypt Predict, Jupiter-powered token swaps, Earn and, now, perpetual futures. Users can also send funds privately, after Decrypt added confidential transfers through Solana infrastructure firm Helius.

#5229. What's Next For Bitcoin (BTC)? CME FedWatch Shows 100% Odds of a December Fed Rate Hike

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

CME FedWatch puts the implied chance of a Fed rate hike at 100% for the December 9 meeting, according to the November 12 snapshot. This is another source of pressure on Bitcoin, and there is a good chance the market is going to face its full effect.

Bitcoin and the cryptocurrency market in general aren't set in stone and are highly affected by the performance of traditional markets and the global economy. A rate hike directly affects the desire of investors to expose themselves to riskier assets like crypto.

Bitcoin has been going down rapidly even without the additional source of pressure from the Fed. The current BTC price drop appears to be mostly fueled by insufficient compensation for the risk: Bitcoin and crypto in general aren't as attractive as tech or AI stocks, so it is far better to get exposed to the latter ones.

That is the bearish reading here. FedWatch reflects futures pricing, so the hike is already expected rather than guaranteed to become a fresh shock.

Bitcoin is trading at $72,744, below all the moving averages shown on the daily chart. A drop below $70,000 shouldn't necessarily trigger any substantial surge of selling pressure, considering how stretched the decline already looks.

The imbalance of inflows and outflows is still staggering in this reading of the market. The concern is that almost every top-15 crypto asset remains in endurance mode, facing persistent outflows fueled by security concerns, a retreat in investor sentiment and a lack of substantial recovery prospects.

This view draws on Coinglass, CryptoQuant and SosoValue, which track derivatives, on-chain activity and ETF behavior respectively. The interpretation is that all three fields show outflows from digital assets alongside a growing supply of stablecoins.

A full-blown recovery of the cryptocurrency market would need new catalysts and use cases. A potential burst of the AI bubble, with substantial losses for the largest market players, might push them towards digital assets. A transition is possible, but it's not guaranteed by any means.

The confirmation of a market recovery would only come if we witness higher highs and constant inflows into institutional products like ETFs.

#5228. MSFT Stock Price Faces Pressure as Microsoft Outage and $170 Billion AI Spending Raise Concerns

Microsoft stock has another problem beyond its latest global outage. While the disruption to Outlook, Teams and Copilot has put cloud reliability under scrutiny, the company’s aggressive AI infrastructure spending is creating a separate challenge for investors. Microsoft is growing rapidly, but sustaining that momentum requires enormous capital commitments. With MSFT approaching a key technical resistance zone, investors have more than one risk to assess.

Microsoft’s latest global outage has exposed the risks of its growing dependence on cloud-based workplace services. The disruption, which began on November 12, affected Outlook, Exchange Online, Teams, SharePoint and Copilot across multiple regions.

BREAKING: Microsoft's Outlook, Teams and Copilot have been down for over 15 hours.Microsoft says it's not a cyberattack, but servers going down unexpectedly.$MSFT is down -3% in premarket. pic.twitter.com/o4xpAG8e2F

The incident lasted more than 12 hours, disrupting businesses, government agencies and other organizations. Microsoft attributed the problem to server and infrastructure issues and said it had found no indication of malicious activity. Its recovery efforts initially struggled as some restored servers became unresponsive under returning traffic.

The disruption also follows another Microsoft 365 service incident earlier this year, bringing reliability concerns back into focus. For enterprise customers, consistent uptime is central to Microsoft’s competitive position. Repeated failures could encourage large organizations to reconsider their dependence on a single cloud ecosystem.

The immediate financial damage remains unclear, but the reputational risk is harder to measure. Investors will be watching Microsoft’s post-incident explanation and whether the company can demonstrate that its infrastructure is becoming more resilient.

The second concern is the scale of Microsoft’s AI investment. The company is expanding data-center capacity to support Azure, AI workloads and growing demand for computing infrastructure. Microsoft reported $45.2 billion in capital expenditure for fiscal Q1 2027, while management expects calendar-year 2026 spending to reach approximately $170 billion. It also indicated that quarterly capital expenditure could exceed $50 billion in the December quarter.

The investment is supported by strong demand. Azure revenue increased 48% in the September quarter, while Microsoft Cloud revenue rose 30% to $63.9 billion. Commercial remaining performance obligations also climbed 73% to $677 billion.

However, the central question is how quickly that demand translates into returns. Heavy infrastructure spending can pressure free cash flow and operating margins, particularly when new capacity takes time to become productive.

Microsoft’s own filings warn that slower AI adoption, underutilized infrastructure and rising computing costs could affect future returns. The company is therefore facing a demanding balance: invest enough to capture AI growth without allowing capital costs to outrun revenue generation.

Microsoft’s cloud business remains a major strength, but sustaining its current growth rate will not be straightforward. Azure grew 48% in the latest quarter, and management expects growth of 49% to 50% in constant currency for the December quarter. However, Microsoft also acknowledged that customer demand continues to exceed available supply, while some third-party GPU capacity deliveries have been delayed.

Microsoft stock price closed at $539.44 on November 13, gaining 1.08%. The stock is approaching a major supply zone between $550 and $560, where previous advances have encountered selling pressure. A sustained breakout above this region could strengthen the recovery and bring $575 into focus.

On the downside, $515 is the immediate support area, followed by $490–$500. The broader demand zone sits around $375–$390, although that remains well below the current trading range. The moving averages continue to provide support beneath the current price, but the recent rejection near the supply zone suggests buyers have yet to establish control. A breakout above $560 would improve the technical outlook, while a decline below $515 could weaken the recovery.

Microsoft’s long-term growth story remains supported by Azure expansion, enterprise software and AI demand. However, the latest outage has highlighted operational risks, while the company’s infrastructure spending raises questions about capital efficiency and future profitability.

The next phase will depend on whether Microsoft can restore customer confidence, convert AI demand into sustainable revenue and maintain cloud growth without sacrificing margins.

#5227. XRP Risks Losing $1 Amid Aggressive $2.5 Billion Shift

As XRP continues to struggle amid the broad crypto market volatility while failing in multiple attempts to recover, key on-chain metrics are pointing to increased selling pressure among traders.

While momentum remains extremely weak, latest data from crypto analytics platform, Cryptoquant, shows that sellers have dominated both XRP’s spot and futures markets, putting it at risk of losing the long-held $1 mark.

Per the data, XRP’s cumulative volume delta (CVD) across major cryptocurrency exchanges shows that aggressive selling has dominated both its spot and futures market since September 23.

As of November 12, XRP’s Perpetual CVD on Binance has fallen from -$109 million to about -$1.1 billion. It is important to note that the CVD of an asset is a key metric that measures the difference between the volume of aggressive buy orders and aggressive sell orders over time.

While the metric turns negative when more traders are aggressively selling than buying, the -991 million negative balance in the Binance perpetual CVD signals stronger selling pressure.

With the negative balance on Binance exceeding its previous level by more than 10 times, this shows that traders have placed significantly more market sell orders than buy orders, either by opening short positions or closing existing long positions.

Apart from its futures market, further data shows that sellers have also dominated XRP’s spot activity across all major crypto exchanges, including Binance.

Notably, XRP’s All CEX Estimated Spot CVD moved from -$400 million to -$2.9 billion over the same period.

While this marks a $2.5 billion increase in the imbalance favoring sellers, it indicates that XRP spot traders have also been selling XRP more aggressively rather than buying, propelling the asset for further price declines.

#5226. OKX-ICE Tokenized Stock Venue Sets Launch, Turning to Crypto Companies After Big Tech Opts Out

OKX-ICE Tokenized Stock Venue Sets Launch, Turning to Crypto Companies After Big Tech Opts Out

After weeks of rejections from corporate America, OKX and ICE’s tokenized stock venue has found its launch lineup: crypto’s own public companies.

OKXICE said Wednesday that its Tokenized Securities Venue will open Nov. 23, letting eligible U.S. customers trade tokenized securities around the clock in the OKX app.

More than 70 assets are expected to be listed, including securities from Coinbase, Circle, Robinhood, Strategy, BitGo, Securitize and Better.com, plus 59 ETFs from Direxion. Strategy will also list its preferred stocks.

That’s a very different roster from the one the venue started with. As of its Nov. 5 notice, 67 companies, including every member of the “Magnificent Seven” tech giants, had objected to having their shares traded on the platform. Coinbase, Robinhood and Strategy were notably absent from that list, and all three are now among the venue’s launch issuers.

“The SEC has created a real opening for American markets to lead the next era of finance,” said former New York Gov. Andrew Cuomo, who co-chairs OKXICE, a joint venture between OKX and NYSE owner Intercontinental Exchange.

Securitize will provide the tokenization infrastructure. Each token represents an entitlement backed one-for-one by a real U.S. share, carrying dividends, voting rights and other shareholder benefits, according to the announcement.

Trading will run through automated market maker pools built on Uniswap v4 on X Layer, OKX’s Ethereum layer-2 network, and customers will hold their tokens in self-custodial wallets.

Strategy CEO Phong Le called tokenization on the venue “a natural next step” for the company’s preferred stocks, such as STRC.

Access is limited to eligible U.S.-based OKX customers who activate a CeDeFi Wallet, pass identity verification and complete onboarding with OKXICE. The venue operates under the SEC’s innovation exemption, which the agency unveiled in September after the Clarity Act failed to advance in the Senate. Under the exemption, companies can keep third-party tokenized versions of their stock off a given venue by objecting within 30 days.

The launch caps a busy stretch for Wall Street tokenization. DTCC’s new tokenization service, which runs partly on the Canton Network, went live in late October, and Morgan Stanley said it was among the first banks to use it.

#5225. Bitcoin Price Crashes Below $69,000: Is the Worst Still to Come?

Bitcoin news: BTC hit $67,568 as longs were liquidated. Fear is Extreme, but BTC fell further after 15 of 16 past readings.

In Bitcoin news on Friday, November 13, the BTC USD price fell as low as $67,568 on Binance as a wave of forced selling wiped out leveraged bets on a rebound. It was the cryptocurrency’s lowest price since August 19.

Bitcoin trades at $68,772, down 6.7% in 24 hours and 9.1% in a week. Ether lost 5.5% and Solana 3.4% on the day.

BREAKING: Bitcoin falls below $69,000 and ETH nears $1,900 as selling pressure accelerates in crypto.

Crypto markets have now lost -$150 billion in 48 hours. pic.twitter.com/29QW0khxOh

— The Kobeissi Letter (@KobeissiLetter) November 13, 2026

Most of the damage came from liquidations, when an exchange closes a leveraged trade by force once the trader’s collateral runs out. CoinGlass data cited by several outlets put the 24-hour total at about $1.4 billion, close to 90% of it from longs, the bets that prices would rise. Those forced sales pushed Bitcoin through $70,000 in about two hours overnight.

The mood has flipped with it. The Crypto Fear & Greed Index dropped from 28 to 20 on Friday, its first Extreme Fear reading since July.

In Bitcoin news this week, the question is no longer whether the market is scared. It is whether Extreme Fear means the bottom is close, and what the daily and weekly charts say.

The slide started on Thursday afternoon, when Nvidia announced that CEO Jensen Huang had resigned. It picked up speed after midnight UTC, when Bitcoin broke below the $72,727 low from Tuesday and kept falling.

We added up every forced closure in Bitcoin, Ethereum, Solana, XRP and Dogecoin futures on OKX, one of the largest exchanges, over the past 24 hours.

Investors have also kept pulling money from U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs, funds that buy and hold the coin itself, adding to the selling.

EXPLORE: Bitcoin Price Crashes to $72,700 as Leveraged Traders Buy the Dip: Is the Bottom In?

On Thursday, we listed three signs that would point to a bottom: the fear index dropping toward the teens, the funding rate turning negative and open interest, the value of open futures bets, falling as longs give up. Overnight, two of them showed up.

One sign still points the other way. On OKX, 3.19 accounts are long for every one that is short, about the same as before the crash. Many traders are still buying the dip with leverage.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best New Crypto to Hodl

Extreme Fear sounds like a buy signal. History says it usually comes early. We found every time since 2018 that the index first dropped to 20 or lower after at least a month without such a reading. There were 16, and we followed Bitcoin for 90 days after each.

The most recent case fits the pattern. Extreme Fear first appeared on June 3 at $64,014, and Bitcoin still fell another 8.5% before it bottomed at $57,800 on July 1. Then it rallied to $87,396 in September.

For anyone reading this Bitcoin news as a buy signal, Extreme Fear says the bottom is getting closer. It has rarely said the bottom is here. A move to the 2026 lows would match the median path.

On the daily chart, where each candle covers one day, Bitcoin sits far below both of its exponential moving averages (EMAs), averages that give recent prices more weight. The 50-day EMA is at $78,226 and the 200-day EMA at $76,325.

On the weekly chart, where each candle covers one week, the key level is the 200-week EMA at $69,555. Bitcoin trades just below it, and the week closes on Sunday.

That line matters. Bitcoin closed below the 200-week EMA for 16 weeks this year, in February and March and again from June to mid-August, and both of 2026’s bottoms formed during those stretches.

The overnight flush did part of the work a bottom needs: leverage came out and funding turned negative.

For traders following Bitcoin news, the Sunday weekly close against $69,555 is the next test. History says the final low often comes weeks after the first Extreme Fear reading.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Reveals Upcoming Binance Listings

CHZ is testing the lower boundary of a long term descending channel near $0.0100-$0.01.59.

The first upside targets are $0.027 and $0.057, followed by $0.15 and $0.33.

The $0.95 level remains a distant chart target, conditional on a sustained bullish reversal.

The Chiliz price is sitting in a spot that deserves attention, but not blind optimism. On the weekly chart for Binance CHZ/USDT, the token is pressing against the lower boundary of a long running descending channel. That area has acted as a buying zone before. Whether buyers show up again is the question now.

The chart traces a broad downward channel from the 2021 peak, with price repeatedly making lower swings inside its boundaries. The latest move has brought CHZ back toward the channel floor and a marked support zone around $0.0100-$0.01059. A green band on the chart extends to roughly $0.013, highlighting the area being watched.

That’s a location, not a confirmed reversal. The setup may offer an attractive risk reward profile on paper, but the chart alone doesn’t prove that demand will hold. A sustained loss of this floor would weaken the bullish case.

$CHZ Channel Bottom Attracting Buyers 👩‍💻Chiliz is trading near the lower border of the descending channel formation on the weekly timeframe – right where bulls have historically stepped in.The technical setup is increasingly promising for buyers in this zone, and the… pic.twitter.com/LYKwyztfPu

If support holds and momentum turns, the chart marks $0.027 as the first upside target, followed by $0.057. Beyond those levels, resistance markers sit at $0.15 and $0.33, while $0.95 is the distant upper target. Those are chart projections, not guaranteed destinations. CHZ would need to reclaim each area rather than teleport there of enthusiasm.

The descending channel’s upper boundary also matters. A move above it would provide a stronger technical signal than simply bouncing from support.

For now, the Chiliz price story is a test of the floor. Buyers have a clearly defined area to defend, while traders have equally clear levels to monitor overhead. Until price confirms a bounce or breaks support, calling a bottom would be getting ahead of the evidence. The chart is interesting, confirmation is still missing.

#5223. Bitcoin Mining Legend Chun Wang Rebuys ETH at 14% Discount After $18.5 Million Sale

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

F2Pool co-founder Chun Wang, known on X as Satofishi, bought 5,246 ETH worth $10.78 million over the past two days at an average price of $2,054, according to Lookonchain and Arkham data.

On Nov. 5, with ether trading near $2,400, Wang sold 6,200 wrapped staked ETH (wstETH) for $18.5 million in USDC. Wrapped staked ETH is a version of Lido's staked ether (stETH) that can be used in DeFi protocols.

This week, ether traded about 14% lower than on the day of the sale, and Wang started buying it back.

He withdrew 5,246 ETH from Binance hot wallets in two transfers of 3,996 ETH and 1,250 ETH. The purchase cost him $10.78 million, so about $7.7 million from the Nov. 5 sale is still left in USDC, according to Lookonchain.

Wang then sent 3,996 ETH ($8.23 million) to Lido. Lido is the largest liquid staking protocol on Ethereum. He deposited the wstETH he received into Spark, a lending protocol in the Sky ecosystem, which was previously known as MakerDAO. In Spark, wstETH can be used as collateral, while it continues to earn staking rewards.

He started out arbitraging Bitcoin between overseas exchanges and China's Taobao and later co-founded F2Pool. He launched the pool in 2013 together with Mao Shixing. At that time, F2Pool was one of the first Bitcoin mining pools in China.

In 2015, he bought a condo in Pattaya for 2,900 BTC. He later sold it for 7 BTC, as the price of Bitcoin had risen many times over.

In 2025, Wang also funded and commanded Fram2, the first crewed spaceflight over Earth's polar regions, on a SpaceX Crew Dragon.

F2Pool also mined ether until Ethereum moved to proof-of-stake in September 2022. Before that, Wang launched the non-custodial staking service stakefish. According to Wang, he decided to do it after a conversation with Vitalik Buterin in 2017. Stakefish was also one of the first node operators of Lido.

Ether is down more than 15% since the start of the month amid outflows from spot ETFs, according to SoSoValue. At press time, ETH is trading at $2,020.

In a recent announcement, Binance Wallet stated it will discontinue support for the Blast chain effective November 30, 2026, at 10:00 (UTC). After this date, the crypto exchange said that users will no longer be able to view, send, or receive Blast assets within the Binance Wallet.

Ethereum layer-2 network Blast announced in October 2026 that it was shutting down, a little over two years after its launch, as fading activity left it unable to cover operating costs.

Binance noted that this change only affects the display and management of Blast assets within Binance Wallet, as users can still claim their assets on the official Blast site. In light of this, users are strongly advised to manage or transfer their Blast assets before November 30, 2026, at 10:00 (UTC).

This update only affects the Blast network and does not impact other blockchains supported by Binance Wallet.

#5220. A High-Severity Bug Could Have Stalled Bitcoin Mining—Bitcoin Core Has a Fix

A High-Severity Bug Could Have Stalled Bitcoin Mining—Bitcoin Core Has a Fix

Bitcoin Core developers have disclosed three security vulnerabilities in the software that runs most of the Bitcoin network, including one high-severity bug that could disrupt mining, all of which have since been fixed.

Bitcoin Core, the reference software operating the majority of the network’s nodes, classifies vulnerabilities into four tiers, from “low” to “critical,” and these fell into the middle of that range.

The most serious, tracked as CVE-2025-62861, concerns block template creation, the process miners rely on to assemble new blocks.

According to the advisory, a chain reorganization could trigger an erroneous eviction of certain transactions, corrupting a node’s mempool, its waiting room for unconfirmed transactions, so that an affected node could no longer build block templates until it was restarted. For a miner, that means a halt to the very work that earns block rewards.

A second flaw, CVE-2025-62859, could let a peer stall a node’s block processing by announcing a compact block with a manipulated witness, a form of transaction malleation.

None of the three rises to the “critical” level Bitcoin Core reserves for bugs that threaten coin theft at the protocol level, unauthorized coin creation or permanent network-wide chain splits. The developers did not report any evidence the flaws were exploited.

The disclosures come amid a broader reckoning over how quickly Bitcoin’s defenders can keep pace with a shifting threat landscape.

Bitcoin Core recently rewrote its security policy to account for AI, adding a category for “easily discoverable” vulnerabilities that can be surfaced through a simple AI prompt, which it now patches and discloses immediately rather than holding under a long embargo.

That shift reflects mounting concern that large language models have lowered the bar for finding flaws, a worry underscored by the volunteer “Bitcoin Red Team,” which has been racing to uncover AI-discoverable bugs across the ecosystem before attackers can exploit them.

The fixes themselves shipped earlier, with the advisories following under Bitcoin Core’s disclosure timeline. Node operators are generally urged to run the latest software to stay protected.

#5219. Solana (SOL) to Connect With Wall Street’s $4.7 Quadrillion Clearing Giant

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The Depository Trust & Clearing Corporation (DTCC) will connect its tokenization service to Solana (SOL) in the second half of 2027, the company said on Nov. 12.

The integration will allow "digital twins" of Russell 1000 stocks, major ETFs and U.S. Treasuries to be issued on the Solana blockchain. In December 2025, the SEC issued a no-action letter that allowed DTC to tokenize these assets.

In 2025, DTCC processed $4.7 quadrillion in securities transactions. Its depository, DTC, holds and services $114 trillion in assets from more than 150 countries.

The DTCC Tokenization Service (DTS) will let large market participants convert conventional securities into tokens, move them across the Solana network and redeem them.

"DTC-tokenized assets made available on Solana – and all blockchain networks integrated with the service – will retain the same investor protections, ownership rights and regulatory safeguards that apply to traditionally held securities," said Brian Steele, president of DTCC Clearing & Securities Services.

According to DTCC CEO Frank La Salla, the integration will also free up securities for use as collateral.

DTS went live on Oct. 28, 2026. At launch, it worked with Canton Network and a private ledger. DTCC said that it adds new networks at clients' request.

"DTCC is committed to providing an open and connected platform that provides our users with access to mature distributed ledger technology networks like Solana that have established performance, security and governance features," said Nadine Chakar, global head of DTCC Digital Assets.

Nick Ducoff, general manager for institutional at the Solana Foundation, also commented on the news. "Institutions choose Solana because it already runs real financial activity at scale," he said.

According to Ducoff, the integration "connects one of the most trusted names in post-trade infrastructure with a network built for always-on markets."

As of Nov. 12, distributed real-world assets (RWA) in the Solana ecosystem reached $5.13 billion across more than 1.02 million holders, according to RWA.xyz. Transfers over the past 30 days totaled $16.48 billion.

The stablecoin market cap on Solana stands at $13.85 billion, with monthly turnover of $387.41 billion.

Solana is facing pressure from both sides of the market, with its bearish technical structure and declining futures participation offering little evidence of a strong recovery. The $77 to $78 zone remains the immediate support to watch, while the EMA cluster stands as a major hurdle for buyers.

A daily close below $77 could expose SOL to further losses towards $70.11 and $64.60. On the other hand, holding above $80 and attracting fresh market participation could help stabilise the price. Until buyers regain control, however, the broader outlook remains tilted towards the downside.
