#5189. Aave Proposal Targets $3.5M in Added Annual Revenue Through Reserve-Factor Changes

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: TokenLogic proposes changes to Aave's reserve factors across 54 reserves. Its model estimates $3.5 million in additional annual revenue, using November 9 balances and rates; governance has not yet approved the changes.

Aave's latest revenue proposal focuses on how lending income is divided. A November 12 submission from TokenLogic covers 12 V3 instances and V2 Ethereum, with the modeled blended reserve factor rising from 12.0% to 12.7%.

The practical issue is straightforward: the protocol treasury would retain a larger share of interest, leaving a smaller share for suppliers where a change applies. Whether that improves the economics over time depends partly on how those suppliers respond.

Aave's official FAQ defines the reserve factor as the percentage of borrowing interest that accrues to the treasury. It is set for each asset through governance. The treasury is controlled by Aave Governance, rather than functioning as a conventional company's bank account.

A simplified example makes the mechanism clearer. Suppose borrowers generate $100 of interest and a reserve factor is 20%. Before considering other mechanics, $20 goes to the treasury and $80 remains for suppliers. Raising the factor to 25% changes that split to $25 and $75. The underlying $100 of interest has not grown simply because the allocation changed.

This hypothetical illustrates why a higher reserve factor can improve treasury receipts while reducing the attractiveness of supplying to the same market. It also shows why the percentage should not be read as an annual return paid to either group.

TokenLogic proposes moving Ethereum Core DAI's factor from 25% to 30%, while Avalanche V3 USDC would move from 10% to 25%. The proposal's stated purpose includes changing incentives across legacy markets as Aave's newer infrastructure develops.

The immediate parameter change does not itself alter the borrower interest rate. Over time, however, a supplier response could change available liquidity and utilisation. Those subsequent effects make it important to distinguish a mechanical estimate from a forecast of the eventual outcome.

NewsBTC's report on Aave's externally curated V4 markets offers context on how the protocol is developing different arrangements for managing liquidity and risk.

Aave's official protocol changelog records the launch of V4 on Ethereum in March and on Avalanche in July. The architecture groups liquidity into hubs and lending activity into separate spokes, giving the network a different way to organise markets.

That deployment history helps explain why comparisons between versions now matter commercially. A supplier considering a change in yield can compare not only competing protocols but also different Aave deployments, with their own assets, liquidity conditions and operational characteristics.

Our reading is that governance is making an allocation decision about where activity should be encouraged. That deserves scrutiny at the individual-market level: a portfolio-wide revenue estimate can hide quite different experiences for suppliers of different assets.

The revenue figure annualises a snapshot. Borrowed balances can rise or fall, interest rates can change, and liquidity can move after a parameter update. The estimate therefore gives a basis for comparison, while the realised result remains open.

Governance also has to consider the cost of retaining liquidity and the resilience of each market. An extra dollar of treasury income is less useful if the settings create an offsetting loss of activity or a more fragile funding base.

Interest in AAVE ownership, including Payward's recently disclosed investment, makes these economic decisions relevant to a wider audience. The next steps are community feedback and an onchain proposal, followed by measurable results if the changes are approved and executed.

#5188. XRP Ledger Announces Major Fix to Come December 3

The XRP Ledger (XRPL) will be delivering a major update on Dec. 3, which will address the occasional sluggishness the blockchain network has been experiencing in recent weeks.

As stated in a recent XRP Ledger Operations announcement, the network will be rolling out its new version 3.5.0, which will exclusively include technical improvements and bug fixes, such as a lasting fix for the relatively slow ledger closing times.

"XRPL version 3.5.0 will be on Dec 3. Just improvements/fixes, including a long-lasting fix for the occasional slow ledger close times," XRP Ledger Operations said.

The developers have informed users that the interim fixes to help improve the situation have already been working effectively.

"Interim fixes already helped improve close times, and we are watching the network very closely," it added.

This is a critical advancement in improving the reliability of the XRP Ledger, a network widely used to process cryptocurrency payments, tokenization, and stablecoin transactions.

The latest improvement to the XRPL comes after the blockchain network suffered technical issues in October, when some ledgers were closing much slower than usual.

The XRP Ledger usually closes new ledgers once every three to five seconds. This ensures that transaction confirmations happen fairly quickly.

However, intermittent sluggishness was seen on the network, as XRP Ledger Operations admitted on Oct. 21. The team later confirmed that its developers were already working on the issue.

Afterward, XRP Ledger Operations started sharing a recommended configuration update for xrpld software with network operators such as validators and other infrastructure providers.

The temporary fixes were helpful. On Oct. 22, XRP Ledger Operations shared that the occasional slower ledger closures were no longer happening, thanks to the new recommended configuration.

That said, the developers remain hard at work to deliver a software fix that will correct the issue once and for all, a long-term solution that would not rely solely on temporary changes.

That is why the December release is expected to finally bring that permanent fix.

In the meantime, Vet, one of the notable XRP Ledger validators, has already assured the community that the blockchain network is always being monitored as developers work on more improvements.

In a recent post on X, he stressed that a great amount of work has been put into network monitoring, information security and engineering.

Vet said that their engineers are always on call to address any possible incidents.

#5187. Tether's Tiny AI Agent Beats Models 2.5 Times Its Size at Controlling Your Phone

Tether's Tiny AI Agent Beats Models 2.5 Times Its Size at Controlling Your Phone

Tether, the company behind the world's largest stablecoin, USDT, is getting surprisingly good at making tiny AI models punch above their weight.

The company on Wednesday unveiled QVAC BotPsy, an 800-million-parameter AI agent designed to carry out everyday tasks on personal devices. Parameters are essentially the knobs that determine how much an AI model can do, and more of them generally translate to improved performance. And for reference, frontier AI models today run in the trillions of parameters.

In the company's own testing, Tether’s relatively tiny model outperformed competitors with up to 2.5 times as many parameters, suggesting that AI assistants may not need enormous models to get things done.

Unlike traditional chatbots that simply respond to questions, BotPsy is agentic, meaning it’s designed to take action. It can identify which tools an application needs, execute commands in the right order, and adjust when something goes wrong.

For example, Tether described a benchmark scenario in which an AI assistant needed to send a message but encountered two problems: Wi-Fi was disabled and the inbox was full. BotPsy turned on Wi-Fi, cleared enough storage, and completed the task, while the underlying model repeatedly attempted the same failed operation.

That's a fairly mundane achievement, but it's precisely the kind of problem that makes AI agents useful outside a chatbot window.

BotPsy is built on Alibaba's Qwen3.5-0.8B architecture, a relatively small language model that Tether trained further to specialize in interacting with software tools. Its 800 million parameters represent the numerical values the model learned during training, and a smaller parameter count generally means less memory is needed to run it.

According to Tether's technical documentation, BotPsy scored 44.0% on BFCL v4, a benchmark that evaluates an AI system's ability to select and execute software functions across different tasks and conversations. Scores reflect a weighted percentage of successful performance across tool-use categories.

The underlying Qwen3.5-0.8B model scored 23.4%, while the considerably larger Qwen3.5-2B reached 37.7%.

BotPsy also scored 58.7% on ACEBench, which evaluates the accuracy of function calls, including selecting the appropriate tools and arguments. Its base model managed 30.1%, while the 2-billion-parameter competitor scored 55.4%.

The company tested five models across six tool-use evaluations, with BotPsy posting the highest results on five. Its average score across those tests reached 45.0%, compared with 38.8% for the larger Qwen model.

BotPsy is the latest addition to QVAC, Tether's effort to build an AI ecosystem that runs on devices controlled by users rather than relying entirely on centralized cloud infrastructure.

The company first introduced QVAC in May 2025, positioning the project as an alternative to AI services operated by technology giants. Its longer-term ambitions included autonomous agents capable of interacting with applications and potentially making payments using Bitcoin and USDT.

Tether expanded that initiative in October 2025 with a 41-billion-token dataset for training science and engineering models, alongside QVAC Workbench, an application that lets users run AI models locally.

Earlier this year, Tether released its SDK, providing developers with tools to build offline AI applications across mobile and desktop operating systems.

The company also developed QVAC MedPsy, a medical AI system designed to run on consumer hardware. Its 1.7-billion-parameter version outperformed Google's much larger MedGemma-27B on HealthBench Hard, a test of realistic clinical conversations evaluated using physician-developed criteria.

BotPsy is currently available as an open-weight model under the Apache 2.0 license, meaning developers can download, modify, and integrate it into their own applications. Tether has also released compressed versions intended to reduce memory requirements for local deployment.

The company has not announced a release date for a finished consumer assistant powered by BotPsy. Its published model card also says the training code and training data are not included in the release.

#5186. Ethereum’s store-of-value narrative is gaining traction – From Joseph Lubin, at least

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Ethereum’s “store-of-value” narrative is gaining traction lately.

Looking at the broader picture, there are several factors that are leading to increased risk sentiment in the U.S. market.

Whether it be fears over an AI bubble, high-interest rates, CPI figures, or the aftermath of the mid-term elections, Bitcoin is heading toward its worst annual cycle since the 2022 bear market, with year-to-date losses approaching 17% in 2026.

Keeping this in mind, while Ethereum has suffered nearly twice Bitcoin’s losses and is looking at its worst year since 2022, co-founder Joseph Lubin is bullish over the future potential of Ethereum as an inflationary hedge.

Highlighting several key trends on-chain, he says that Ethereum has long-term potential to outperform both Bitcoin and gold as a store of value.

One of the main reasons for his argument is because of the DeFi ecosystem, stablecoins, and smart contracts on Ethereum. These allow ETH to have a stronger utility base compared to Bitcoin and gold.

In addition, with AI adoption accelerating, Ethereum could also see rising demand for its network infrastructure.

Ethereum’s inflation-hedge narrative faces a key test.

As previously mentioned, the U.S. market is facing a range of risks. This has made this one of the most volatile times for investors. So far, Ethereum has had trouble attracting risk capital, falling almost 30% during Q4 compared to Bitcoin, which has fallen only 17%.

This puts Joseph Lubin’s thesis under pressure, as Bitcoin continues to lead the store-of-value narrative while Ethereum struggles to keep up.

Gold, meanwhile, is also losing steam. The metal is trading in a technical downtrend with losses of more than 5% so far in Q4, as it has failed to move above $4,000. The most recent fall below this level has caused a loss of around $480 billion in market value in just 10 hours.

That said, gold still has a clear “relative-strength” advantage over crypto.

As the chart above shows, the ETH/XAU ratio has lost more than 19% in Q4, demonstrating that Ethereum has failed to make any progress relative to gold. This becomes a key level to monitor. A breakout could result in better technical support for Lubin’s long-term thesis.

For now, Ethereum is struggling to outperform Bitcoin and gold currently. Despite rising staking participation and stronger network security, ETH is yet to translate these fundamentals into stronger capital inflows or relative price performance.

Ethereum is showing growing network participation, supporting Lubin’s store-of-value thesis.

Despite this, ETH continues to lag Bitcoin and gold, with the ETH/XAU ratio down over 19% in Q4.

#5185. Helius Launches Solana Privacy Rings With Confidential Balances and Transfers

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

The feature addresses a practical obstacle for public-ledger payments. A business may want the settlement benefits of a blockchain without publishing every payment amount to competitors or employees. Individual users can have similar concerns about exposing the size and history of their financial activity.

The technical documentation separates confidentiality from anonymity. In the default confidential ring, assets, amounts and optional memos are encrypted during private transfers, while the participating wallet addresses remain public. A visible transaction hash also remains part of the record.

Entering from a public balance has a different footprint: the deposit's asset, amount and addresses are visible, even though the resulting private balance is encrypted. Users should therefore understand each step of a payment, rather than assume that selecting a private balance hides all earlier activity.

This is a meaningful improvement for applications that mainly need to conceal amounts. It also establishes a clear limit: a counterparty relationship can still be observable. Product designers will need to explain that distinction before users rely on it.

Helius says developers can integrate the system through APIs and build private escrows for financial activity. Its planned uses include confidential swaps, staking and yield-related applications. The launch provides a foundation that other teams can incorporate into their products.

The broader usability question also appears in NewsBTC's reporting on Samsung Wallet's live USDC rollout on Solana. Reaching users requires a workable product around the underlying network capability.

The foundation says the enterprise version is expected in the coming weeks. That expectation should be tracked as a delivery milestone. The current permissionless launch and the planned enterprise release have different operating requirements.

For an institution, confidentiality is only one part of the problem. Staff still need appropriate access, auditors need reliable evidence, and a payment's legal and accounting treatment must remain clear. NewsBTC's coverage of Solana's delivery-versus-payment programme examines another piece of that wider settlement workflow.

The foundation reports audits of the core programmes and proof circuits by Certora, OtterSec and ZK Security, while formal verification is still underway. It also identifies applications and service providers working with the infrastructure. These are useful implementation details, with separate questions still open about usage and long-term reliability.

#5184. Satoshi-Era Bitcoin Address From 2010 Suddenly Activates With $3.4 Million

A Bitcoin (BTC) address from 2010 has become active after being dormant for over 16 years. The address has transferred around $3.4 million in BTC.

According to blockchain tracking platform Whale Alert, the address held 50 BTC, which was roughly equivalent to $3,442,939 at the time of activation.

The dormant address had been inactive for 16.2 years. Back in BTC's early days, all 50 BTC were worth just $3.

Notably, this wasn’t the only old Bitcoin address that suddenly became active.

According to Whale Alert, another dormant Bitcoin address holding 50 BTC worth roughly $3,442,129 became active after 16.2 years.

The two transfers took place within minutes of each other on Nov. 13. Hence, the total value of the newly activated Bitcoin comes to around $6.89 million.

There’s no confirmation about who owned these wallets. Also, it’s not confirmed if the same individual or entity owns these addresses.

The reason these addresses are significant is that they date back to 2010, which is considered to be the Satoshi era.

In simple terms, the Satoshi era refers to the first few years of Bitcoin, mainly 2009 and 2010. During those years, the pseudonymous founder of Bitcoin, Satoshi Nakamoto, was pretty active in the network's development.

To be precise, Bitcoin came into existence in January 2009. It initially carried a block reward of 50 BTC. This block reward of 50 BTC continued until Bitcoin had its first halving in November 2012.

This is why these newly activated addresses’ holdings of 50 BTC match the rewards miners could receive during the early days of Bitcoin.

These old addresses always raise a lot of interest as Bitcoin had relatively few miners and users during those times.

However, this does not mean that these wallets are owned by the mysterious founder of Bitcoin.

This recent activation comes after a series of interesting activations in the crypto world. These are long-dormant crypto addresses that have become active over the past month.

On Oct. 22, a Bitcoin address with 170 BTC worth roughly $14.38 million became active after being dormant for 13 years, as reported by Whale Alert.

Prior to this, on Oct. 20, a dormant address with 500 BTC worth around $41.54 million became active after 10.7 years. In 2016, these holdings were worth just $194,955.

Later, crypto analytics platform Arkham linked the 500 BTC transaction to addresses belonging to convicted Irish drug dealer Clifton Collins. Previously, his crypto holdings were deemed inaccessible.

On Oct. 17, Whale Alert tracked activity involving old Bitcoin addresses holding 248 BTC and 167 BTC worth roughly $20.24 million and $13.65 million, respectively.

#5183. Russia Legalizes Crypto Trading While XRP Stands Apart

Home » Bitcoin (BTC) » Russia Legalizes Crypto Trading While XRP Stands Apart

Platforms are able to conduct business operations in crypto, offering retail knowledge-based accessibility.

Russia’s crypto bid is going at a faster pace since the fourth largest economy around the globe introduced crypto trading for retail & businesses. The new set of rules, coming from the Central Bank Of Russia, limits regular investors to a 300,000 rubles yearly cap (roughly $3,600).

This allows buying & holding Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH) & USDT through regulated intermediaries (brokers, trust managers & licensed exchanges). Everyone must pass a knowledge test plus review risk disclosures before starting trading activities, while XRP is considered an asset for advanced users only.

The Moscow Exchange already lists cash-settled perpetual futures (XRP/USDF) and related indices tracking XRP prices. These are available only to qualified investors, settle in rubles (no actual XRP is delivered), and form part of a broader set of crypto derivatives with substantial turnover among Futures traders across the scene.

Russian Central Bank: Exchanges May Conduct Crypto Trading from November 22, Subject to Prior Approval of Trading Rules According to TASS, the Bank of Russia said Russian exchanges may begin offering cryptocurrency trading from November 22, 2026, provided they first agree on… pic.twitter.com/txoYyq5vfL

The fresh legal framework dates back to August. Then, Russia’s President Vladimir Putin signed the inaugural comprehensive cryptocurrency law (Federal Law No. 282-FZ, “On Digital Currencies and Digital Rights”), setting September 1, 2026 activation window: contrastingly to the entry-level investor restrictions, qualified ones have no limits.

Meanwhile, businesses face hard limits, needing to meet a minimum equity requirement (15 million rubles, roughly $187,000) to run crypto services or custody services. The regulatory framework is way more lenient on businesses conducting cross-border sanctions to dodge the 21-chapter European Union sanction packages.

The retail crypto trading gets legalized on November 22, 2026, the related Duma documents state. The approved exchanges & crypto brokers are obliged to report the trading data and results to the central bank in accordance with existing reporting procedures for XRP & others.

For XRP traders in the region, the MOEX XRP Futures index allows up to 5x leverage.

RUSSIA MADE A MASSIVE $XRP MOVE!! pic.twitter.com/dmuUaPnsuP

Paying with XRP or any other popular crypto coin at a local store is still off the table. Goods & services are prohibited as a result of a separate ban, pushing crypto adoption in the perspective of an investment vehicle rather than a means of payment.

Still, setting up state-surveilled crypto brokers is a pretty big move that could inspire other major economies to do the same.

#5182. Chainlink and Moody’s Complete Testnet Pilot for Cross-Chain Credit Ratings

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Chainlink and Moody's Ratings completed a cross-chain distribution pilot using test data. It ran on testnets and did not publish live rating actions or change Moody's existing publication processes.

Tokenized finance needs information to move alongside assets. In a November 10 announcement, Chainlink and Moody's Ratings reported a completed pilot that used the Cross-Chain Interoperability Protocol, or CCIP, to distribute credit information across multiple networks.

The exercise connected Moody's Token Integration Engine, known as TIE, with cross-chain messaging. Its significance lies in a familiar market problem: investors need consistent information about an instrument even when different pieces of financial infrastructure represent or hold it.

The pilot used representative data in a controlled testnet environment, spanning permissioned and permissionless networks. No actual rating actions were published onchain, and live dissemination processes were not involved.

That scope makes the result useful as an interoperability test. It leaves the separate work of operating a production service, setting responsibilities and integrating it with financial institutions.

Consider a hypothetical bond represented across several networks. If its credit assessment changes, a reliable system needs to identify the relevant instrument and ensure each destination receives the appropriate update. Otherwise, applications can act on different versions of information about the same underlying obligation.

Chainlink's documentation describes CCIP as infrastructure for transferring both data and tokens between blockchains. Its developer tools cover standalone messages and transactions that combine value transfers with instructions.

This matters because a rating update is information. Delivering it does not necessarily require moving the asset itself. A recipient application can receive a message and decide how to use it according to its own rules and permissions.

The documentation also includes message-status, execution and finality tools. Those operational details become important when a financial workflow needs to determine whether an update has arrived, whether it has been processed and how to handle an exception.

NewsBTC has examined the configurable controls around this infrastructure in its report on CCIP 2.0's security and compliance features.

The new test sits within a longer programme. In June, Moody's announced an Alphaledger integration for Solana, giving issuers on that platform an option to integrate ratings with tokenized fixed-income assets. That followed TIE's March deployment on Canton.

Moody's described TIE as network-agnostic. The aim is to make its analytical information available within different blockchain environments, with the underlying credit opinion retaining its established role.

This history is useful because connecting a ratings service to one network and distributing information across several networks are related but separate tasks. The latest pilot tests a way to extend that reach; it should not be presented as the first time Moody's explored blockchain delivery.

Our assessment is that the hardest questions extend beyond whether a message can cross a network boundary. A production workflow needs clear instrument identifiers, controls over who can publish updates, a record of changes and rules for handling delayed or disputed information.

It also needs to preserve the meaning of the data. A credit rating is an analytical opinion about credit risk. Putting that opinion onchain does not make an asset risk-free or remove the need to assess its liquidity, price and legal terms.

Similar lifecycle work appears in NewsBTC's coverage of Chainlink's tokenized-equity dividend demonstration. A ledger can carry an asset, but the asset's obligations and information still need maintenance.

The pilot offers evidence that one distribution route can work in a test setting. Named production integrations, defined update policies and operating records would provide the next evidence of how it performs for real portfolios.

written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.

#5181. Everyone’s selling XRP right now, but why?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

XRP [XRP] has been sliding on the charts for weeks, and it looks like everyone’s selling their stash right now.

Whales have been steady though. They’ve been trading big, but we don’t quite know whose side they’re on.

Cryptoquant’s Futures Taker CVD has been sell-dominant since late August. That hasn’t changed yet so, two and a half months straight.

The only time this year Futures traders leaned buy-heavy was in the beginning of 2026. In that time, XRP has gone from $1.55 in late September to $1.05 now.

Spot traders used to turn a blind eye, but not anymore. For the most part, their CVD was neutral this year. There were some buy-heavy stretches in January, February and May.

That changed in October. Now, the Spot Take CVD is also sell-dominant. This is the first time that’s happened all year!

That matters more than you think. Futures selling might just be traders betting short. When Spot starts selling too, that would mean that people holding actual XRP may be getting out.

Usually, when people trade a coin this much, it’s because the price is rising. With XRP though, that’s not been the case.

Futures volume bubble map went red in late August. Through most of September and early October, it was marked as “overheating.” While that’s slowed down a bit since, it’s still been a fair spike.

It moved into “heating” in early September, was “overheating” in the middle of the month, and has stayed hot ever since.

Now, put that next to when Spot volume was “cooling” at $1.40.

Both markets are busy right now, but since it’s all during a dip, it may be indicative of selling.

Finally, CryptoQuant’s Average Order Size has shown “big whale orders” on Futures and Spot since early October.

That’s happened before as well. Whale-sized orders were all the rage through June, July, and most of August. All this, while XRP went between $1 and $1.25.

Towards the end of August, the price went back to $1.50 on the charts.

As with many others, this metric also only shows how big orders are, and not which way they’re going. Whales could be buying the dip, or walking away.

Either way, they’re the ones moving the most money right now. Whatever they decide at $1 is probably where XRP goes next.

#5179. This New AI Music Model Is Already Better Than Suno—We Put ACE to the Test

This New AI Music Model Is Already Better Than Suno—We Put ACE to the Test

Artificial intelligence has gotten pretty good at writing songs. Now, a relatively unfamiliar competitor has managed to beat Suno, arguably the biggest name in AI music, at its own game.

ACE Music v1, developed by ACE Studio, has taken first place on Artificial Analysis' vocal music leaderboard, scoring 1,160 Elo points against Suno v6's 1,144.

The AA-Music-Vocal v1.1 benchmark measures the quality of AI-generated songs containing vocals. Evaluators listen to tracks produced from identical prompts without knowing which model created them, then choose their favorite. Those preferences determine an Elo-style rating, with higher scores indicating stronger listener preference.

The lead is relatively narrow, and the models' statistical confidence intervals overlap. Suno also retains the top position on the separate instrumental music leaderboard.

ACE Studio, founded in 2018, originally focused on synthesizing singing voices from MIDI notes and lyrics. Its latest ACE Music v1 model generates complete songs from written descriptions or user-provided lyrics.

In Decrypt's hands-on tests, ACE Music consistently produced more melodic and coherent songs in mainstream styles.

That said, the vocal performances sounded more natural in general, with fewer of the synthetic artifacts that sometimes make AI singers sound like they're struggling to pronounce their own lyrics, use a lot of autotune or hallucinate words just to fill spaces. (Go ahead and give our song a listen here, which incidentally is about our latest company announcement regarding the Information Exchange.)

ACE also handled song endings better. Where Suno occasionally introduces strange sounds, unnecessary repetitions, hallucinations or abrupt musical changes near the finish, ACE generally maintained its structure and delivered cleaner conclusions.

That matters because making a convincing chorus is relatively easy compared with maintaining a musical idea throughout an entire composition even if the lyrics are already over.

Suno still had an advantage when we pushed the models toward less conventional genres.

In our melodic death metal test, ACE consistently produced tracks featuring a woman singing clearly in a pleasant, clean voice, missing the aggressive vocal delivery expected from the requested style. In general it confuses metal genres all over. Suno offered more creative arrangements in the proper heavy styles, with better stylistic consistency in several similarly demanding prompts.

The results were subjective rather than a controlled benchmark, but they showed why a leaderboard victory doesn't automatically make one model better for every musician.

ACE's most interesting feature may be its songwriting workflow.

Its “Idea” feature can develop a basic concept into lyrics and a structured composition, identifying individual song sections and describing how they should be arranged. Users can then refine the material rather than repeatedly generating completely new tracks.

The v1.1 Preview goes further, creating a musical score before generating audio.

“Each generation is a new performance, so the sound may differ even when the score stays the same,” the developers explain.

Users can edit melodies and chords, regenerate performances, export MIDI files and separate vocals from instruments for further production.

That workflow puts ACE closer to a music production assistant than a simple prompt-to-song application.

The flagship ACE Music v1 model remains closed-source with no public API, so users can only generate via the official site. However, its developers are pursuing a parallel open-source strategy.

ACE Studio and StepFun have already released ACE-Step 1.5, a separate family of models whose code and weights are publicly available. Unlike the proprietary flagship, these can run on users' own hardware—but using a weaker model.

The company also promises another release.

“We plan to open-source ACE Music v1 Flash in late 2026,” the developers state on their website, describing a version designed to balance quality, generation speed and hardware requirements.

ACE Music is available through its browser application, with a limited free tier and paid subscriptions. Commercial usage rights for flagship-model songs require a paid plan, while ACE-Step offers a separate free generation option.

For now, ACE Music v1 holds a 16-point Elo lead over Suno v6 in vocal generation. Its promised Flash release would give local users another model to try before the end of 2026.

#5178. Sygnum Launches ETH Fund Targeting 8–10% Returns in Ether

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Sygnum has announced an ETH-denominated fund targeting 8–10% annual returns after fees. The target includes staking income, and investors remain exposed to ETH's market price.

Sygnum is pitching a different measure of investment performance: how much Ether an investor owns at the end of the year. In its November 12 announcement, the bank introduced the ETH Alpha Fund, which opened to investors on November 2 and is aimed at professional and qualified investors in approved markets.

The fund's product page makes two distinctions explicit. Its 8–10% objective is the total portfolio target, including staking. It is also denominated in ETH, so the value of an investment in dollars or another currency continues to move with Ether's price.

That changes how the headline number should be read. As a hypothetical illustration, an investor starting with 100 ETH and achieving an 8% token return would finish with 108 ETH. If the dollar price of Ether fell 20% over the same period, the position's dollar value would still be 13.6% lower.

That example describes arithmetic, not a forecast for the fund. It shows why increasing a token balance and preserving purchasing power are different objectives. The meaning of a successful year depends on which currency the investor uses to measure it.

Sygnum says the portfolio combines native staking with options premiums, arbitrage and derivatives carry or funding strategies. The trading component aims to earn returns without relying on a directional call on ETH.

Ethereum's staking documentation explains the underlying distinction. Validators receive rewards for their role in maintaining the network. They can also lose ETH through inactivity penalties or slashing, while using a service provider adds another counterparty to the arrangement.

Trading income has a separate economic source. An options premium compensates the seller for accepting an obligation; an arbitrage strategy depends on a pricing difference that can be captured after costs. Neither is simply an extra protocol reward.

Combining these activities may diversify the sources of income. It also makes the resulting product more complex to evaluate than a straightforward staking position.

The launch announcement says trading uses Sygnum Protect, keeping collateral off exchanges at the bank. The product page lists a minimum subscription of $100,000 or its ETH equivalent, monthly subscriptions and monthly redemptions, with advance notice requirements. It also lists an investor-level gate of up to 10%, although the page does not explain its mechanics.

Those terms matter for an institution that must meet cash demands on a different timetable. A quoted asset value does not necessarily mean that capital can be withdrawn immediately. A treasury evaluating such a structure has to match its investment horizon with the fund's dealing arrangements.

Custody deserves the same attention. NewsBTC's coverage of Sygnum's MultiSYG lending arrangement illustrates how the control of collateral can be designed separately from the financial exposure attached to it.

This launch sits within the broader expansion of institutional ETH products, alongside developments such as Amundi's Ethereum-based fund share class. The products serve different purposes, even though both use Ethereum-related infrastructure.

For the new fund, the useful evidence will be realised returns, the consistency of those returns and how its strategies behave when market conditions change. Comparisons also need the same accounting basis: net with net, ETH with ETH and equivalent observation periods.

An annual target gives investors an objective to assess. A live track record will show how closely the fund can follow it.

#5177. Binance Halts Luna Classic Burns: What’s Going On?

Home » Altcoins » Binance Halts Luna Classic Burns: What’s Going On?

Terra Luna Classic’s community got used to the monthly burning ceremonies, but this time it’s postponed.

A few weeks after Binance’s $6.4 billion hack following an employee’s laptop breach, the largest digital asset exchange across the globe is resuming most of their regular services. However, the Terra Luna Classic (LUNC) community has missed the monthly LUNC burn, coming from 50% of fees garnered from Binance’s LUNC-related trading pairs.

Typically done on the first of the month, this time Binance’s LUNC burn didn’t arrive at all, chain records show. That’s the first break in a pattern running since March 2023, when Binance pledged to help restore the downtrodden Terra Luna Classic community’s hope in LUNC’s long-term restoration efforts via continuous token burns.

On-chain records show 50 batches of LUNC burns being carried out by Binance on an earlier note. The last one, which was on October 1, 2026, eliminated 335 million tokens, pushing the exchange’s cumulative Terra Luna Classic burn count towards 91 billion.

That’s roughly 19.6% of all LUNC burns to this date, a considerable contribution in the deflationary campaign.

Still no statement from @Binance regarding the monthly #LUNC burn.. Although we understand the massive hack, I feel as though a statement should at least be made to #LunaClassic investors. Is this a temporary pause or indefinite?.. pic.twitter.com/Od2yH1kFgn

Some LUNC community members are floating the main reason for the delay to be apparent changes in wallet security, but Binance confirmed no such action. Naturally, the internal wallet reshuffling can be a normal practice following the multi-billion dollar hack and the day-by-day withdrawal restoration efforts done with diligence.

Back in August, the community behind Terra Luna Classic revisited the proposal 12223 to raise the on-chain transaction tax to 1.5%. Of that, 1.2 percentage points go towards burning even more LUNC coins. Meanwhile, strong voices in the crypto community have been advocating for more exchanges beyond Binance to burn fixed-fee allocations.

KuCoin & OKX were among the ones named in negotiations. The game-tested altcoin still has a massively over-minted supply of 5.5 trillion tokens in circulation, a figure normally ascribed to meme coins.

$LUNC AND $USTC BURN UPDATE pic.twitter.com/hboLcOFNdN

However, the LUNC chain restoration efforts have torched nearly 500 billion out of the original pile, showcasing what years of strict coordination can do for an embattled community.

Aside from the 19% portion claimed by Binance, the on-chain transaction tax and direct LUNC community burns make up the rest of the historical total. Regardless, the biggest hurdle for a proper Terra Luna Classic (LUNC) restoration point remains the stagnant trading volume.

Since the hard fork in May 2022, the cumulative burn efforts crunched 6.7% of total supply so far.

Token burning is a considerable deflation measure for long-term holders to ensure the asset remains at relatively bearable market value.

#5176. Binance Reveals Final Numbers After $6.34 Billion Hack: 100.04% of Shiba Inu (SHIB) Reserves Confirmed

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Binance has published the final results of an unscheduled Proof of Reserves (PoR) report, two weeks after the cryptocurrency exchange lost $6.34 billion in an October hack.

According to Binance, the snapshot was taken on Nov. 12 at 00:00 UTC, and it shows that the exchange fully backs the balances of its clients. Shiba Inu (SHIB) is backed at 100.04%, and the other major assets on the exchange are fully covered as well.

The latest report comes after the Oct. 29 attack, during which hackers stole funds from Binance across ten blockchains in 20 minutes. Among the stolen funds were nearly 500 billion SHIB. After the attack, the exchange took an additional reserve snapshot at Bitcoin block height 975493.

Binance data shows that the net account balances of users stand at 50.67 trillion SHIB, and the exchange holds 50.69 trillion SHIB in its wallets. Of that amount, 50.68 trillion SHIB is held at the addresses of Binance itself, and the remaining 7.46 billion SHIB is held by third-party custodians.

Besides SHIB, the report also shows that the other major cryptocurrencies are fully backed. Compared with the previous check on Nov. 4, the BTC balances of users went up by 0.35% to 687,912 BTC, and the coverage ratio for Bitcoin stands at 100.05%. The ETH balances went up by 1.04% to 3.48 million ETH, and the BNB balances increased by 1.03% to 42.95 million BNB.

Still, the USDT balances of users went down by 3.68% to 24.3 billion USDT. At the same time, the stablecoin has the highest coverage ratio in the report at 104.34%.

Arkham Intelligence data shows that the known wallets of Binance currently hold 58.82 trillion SHIB. That is 5.88% of the total supply of the meme coin, and these tokens are worth $251.16 million. In this context, Binance wallets hold 8.13 trillion SHIB (about $34.7 million) more than the amount shown in the PoR report.

According to Binance, the PoR report only covers the deposits of clients and the coins that the exchange keeps to back them, so the company's own funds are not included in the report. Based on the Arkham data, Binance holds about 16% more SHIB than it owes to its clients.

According to Arkham, Binance is still the third-largest exchange by SHIB holdings. Only the burn address with 410.44 trillion SHIB (or 41.04% of supply), the U.S.-based exchange Coinbase with 74.53 trillion SHIB, and South Korea's Upbit with 64.14 trillion SHIB hold more of the meme coin. Robinhood, Crypto.com and Bithumb come after Binance with 57.12 trillion, 34.95 trillion and 16.74 trillion SHIB, respectively.

Finally, Binance said that the PoR system is still computing the final Merkle Root Hash using zk-SNARKs. After that, users will be able to log into their profiles and check that their balances are included in the overall ledger.

At press time, the price of SHIB stands at $0.00000426.

#5175. Here’s why Zcash could remove 50,000 ZEC from its grants treasury

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The Zcash [ZEC] community is gearing up to vote on a proposal that could significantly change how ZEC funds its development and distributes newly created tokens. The plan involves removing 50,000 ZEC from the Zcash Community Grants (ZCG) treasury and redirecting its value toward future mining rewards.

That said, Zcash currently allocates 8% of newly issued ZEC to ZCG, which uses the funds to support developers, wallet providers, and other ecosystem projects.

These grants help maintain the infrastructure and software needed to use ZEC. However, rising ZEC prices has increased the value of ZCG’s existing treasury, which has raised concerns that the committee was accumulating more funds than necessary while continuing to receive new token allocations.

However, now, under the new proposal, approximately 55% of ZCG’s treasury (50,000 ZEC) would be removed from circulation within 30 days of activation. Going ahead, its value would be credited to the Network Sustainability Mechanism, introduced through the NU7 upgrade. This, in turn, would gradually return ZEC to the network through future mining rewards.

At the same time, the proposal would also end ZCG’s existing 8% allocation of newly created ZEC. Instead, this share would accumulate in a new Future Dev and Legal Defense Fund until the next halving takes place.

As this happens, the fund would remain inaccessible until a separate proposal establishes withdrawal rules. Lastly, ZCG would retain its remaining treasury to continue funding development, while the separate 12% Coinholder-Controlled Fund would remain unchanged.

For those unaware, the revised plan follows ZCG’s 3rd November decision to abandon an earlier proposal, which faced criticism for seeking to redirect approximately 98,000 ZEC from existing assets and future income toward mining rewards.

So, now, the next step is community voting, which is scheduled from 16th November to 30th November. Coinholders and community panels will assess the proposal’s support.

If it receives rough consensus, developers, and miners can work toward implementing it through a future network upgrade called NU7.1. However, there is no confirmed activation date yet.

This coincided with Zcash’s monthly active developer count having nearly doubled over the past two years.

According to Glassnode’s report, around 70% of its active developers have more than one year of experience in crypto development, compared with 58% across the wider industry.

Still, ZEC’s price action was under bearish pressure as it was trading at $986.40 at press time after a drop of 1.4% in the past 24 hours.

This followed a recent report by AMBCrypto wherein ZEC fell by 15.25% and Open Interest also declined 13.4%, indicating weakening speculative demand.

#5174. Chainstack Traces Customer Data Breach to Exposed Admin Tools

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Chainstack’s expanded incident report focuses on access-control failures. Its recovery guidance makes completed credential rotation a priority.

Chainstack has added technical detail to the security incident it first disclosed on November 3. Its updated report, with a technical section dated November 10, describes an October intrusion built around configuration and access-control failures. The company says it found no evidence that a software vulnerability was exploited.

According to Chainstack, the attacker entered through a development Grafana instance with unchanged default administrator credentials. An unauthenticated tool server with broad Kubernetes permissions then formed part of the access path. Machine credentials also connected the development and production environments.

The operational lesson is about the reach of an account after the first login. A low-profile testing service can become consequential if it can contact sensitive systems or holds privileges that extend beyond its immediate job.

NewsBTC's coverage of the NEAR AI infrastructure incident explored a related boundary between an application and the services supporting it. The mechanisms differ, but both make the surrounding infrastructure relevant to a crypto platform's security.

The report identifies account names, organisation details, email addresses and password hashes as potentially exposed. It treats node credentials as exposed despite finding no evidence they were read, and asks customers to change passwords, rotate node credentials and regenerate platform API keys where used.

A password hash and a working access token create different problems. Hashes are representations of passwords, while an active token may directly authorise requests. Replacing a login password therefore cannot be assumed to invalidate every other credential associated with an application.

This is why incident guidance needs to identify the affected credential types clearly. A single instruction to change a password can leave developers uncertain about application endpoints, automated jobs and service integrations.

Chainstack's node-management documentation describes a staged rotation process. New credentials are generated while the existing ones remain active. Applications can then move to the replacements before the old credentials are deactivated.

The documentation says both sets continue working until that final step, with no automatic time limit. Completing the rotation permanently rejects requests using the previous credentials. It also says a node rotation does not change a multichain authentication token.

For an operator, the overlap reduces the risk of interrupting a working service while updating applications. The corresponding responsibility is to finish the migration. Generating a replacement alone leaves the previous means of access available.

A useful completion record would therefore track which applications have moved and when the old credentials were disabled. That is a practical control a team can verify after the initial emergency.

Grafana's security guidance warns that services on its local network may be reachable through its data-source proxy. It describes controls such as limiting permitted destinations and using firewall rules to restrict access to sensitive internal services.

Those precautions address what a tool can reach, as well as who can log into it. Authentication and network boundaries perform different jobs; permission limits provide another layer if either boundary fails.

NewsBTC's report on Chainalysis restoring screening services shows why operational recovery and data-exposure analysis can follow different timelines.

Chainstack's expanded account gives customers more detail to assess. The remaining test is whether access changes are completed and whether the revised controls can prevent one compromised service from reaching far beyond its intended role.

written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.

#5173. Why Social Security Cuts Could Cost $500 a Month

Home » Regulations » Why Social Security Cuts Could Cost $500 a Month

A $10.8 trillion payroll tax gap is accelerating Social Security’s 2032 shortfall—and reshaping retirement and investment strategies.

For years, the impending Social Security funding crunch has been framed as a simple demographic math problem: Baby Boomers are retiring faster than younger workers can replace them.

While an aging population plays a role, a new economic analysis reveals a far bigger, largely overlooked culprit—a structural shift in how U.S. income is earned and taxed that has quietly starved the program of $10.8 trillion and shaved a quarter-century off its solvency timeline.

According to a November 2026 study from the AARP Public Policy Institute, the program’s trust funds could have paid 100% of scheduled benefits until 2059 if the share of the U.S. economy subject to payroll taxes had simply held steady at 1983 levels.

Instead, the retirement fund is projected to deplete its reserves in late 2032, putting millions of Americans on track for automatic Social Security cuts exceeding $500 a month.

Social Security relies on payroll taxes for roughly 91% of its annual funding, according to the 2026 Social Security Trustees Report. However, a shrinking slice of U.S. economic output comes from traditional wages covered by that tax.

AARP’s analysis found that wages subject to Social Security payroll taxes accounted for 41% of U.S. gross domestic product (GDP) in 1983. By 2025, that share had fallen to 34%. That 7% represents an estimated $10.8 trillion in lost tax revenue and interest between 1984 and 2034.

The report attributes this erosion to several modern economic shifts:

Under current projections from the Social Security Administration, the Old-Age and Survivors Insurance (OASI) trust fund, which pays retirement and survivor benefits, will run out of reserves in the fourth quarter of 2032.

If Congress were to merge it with the separate Disability Insurance trust fund, combined reserves would last until 2034.

Depletion does not mean Social Security payments will stop entirely, because active workers will continue paying payroll taxes into the system. However, without legislative action, incoming tax revenue will only cover 78% of scheduled retirement benefits in 2032 (or 83% on a combined basis in 2034).

That translates to an automatic 22% across-the-board cut in 2032—reducing the average retiree’s monthly check by more than $500 in over half of U.S. states.

With less than six years before the 2032 deadline, lawmakers face a narrow window to either broaden how the program captures revenue from a shifting economy or let an automatic benefit reduction take effect by default.

A 22% reduction in Social Security payouts would directly compress disposable income for tens of millions of U.S. households, reshaping consumer spending, personal savings rates, and long-term fiscal deficit projections.

First, concerns about the long-term future of Social Security could lead some younger Americans to take more responsibility for their own retirement savings. For some investors, that may mean looking beyond traditional retirement accounts to alternative assets, including Bitcoin and crypto ETFs.

Second, proposals to strengthen Social Security by taxing a broader range of income could have implications for financial markets. If lawmakers eventually target investment income or adjust capital gains taxes, the changes could affect investor behavior and market liquidity across both traditional and digital asset markets.

#5172. Important Ethereum (ETH) Bug Might Be Finally Fixed

One of Ethereum's consensus clients has merged a fix for a validator pubkey cache poisoning attack, according to a pull request on GitHub. The patch addresses a vulnerability around the Gloas fork, which could result in some nodes rejecting valid signatures due to a bug in the global pubkey cache.

Gloas is the consensus layer part of the upcoming Glamsterdam upgrade.

The issue lies in a mechanism where the client stores validator public keys and their indexes in a global cache.

Technically, deposits that are already finalized should give the same validators on every chain, though it is a different story at the fork boundary.

The attacker needs 3 pending deposits just before the fork. The first one is a large top-up for an existing validator. The other two are new keys: A with builder credentials and B with regular validator credentials.

On the canonical chain, the top-up consumes the balance churn, so A and B are processed after the fork. A becomes a builder, and B takes the next validator index.

On the attacker's side chain, the topped-up validator gets slashed before the fork, and its deposit is skipped. This leads to A being processed earlier, essentially taking B's index.

The buggy cache assumed both chains would produce the same validators and imported A's key globally.

At the fork, affected nodes would reject all valid signatures from B on the canonical chain and fork off.

Realistically, the attack is somewhat complicated, as it needs a slashing at the right moment and a side chain, so it's unlikely to happen by accident.

According to the PR, the fix is to only add keys to the global pubkey cache once their activation eligibility epoch is finalized.

A new HeadPubkeys structure also combines keys from the head state and the global cache, so nodes don't miss keys if the chain finalizes without new blocks.

Whether other consensus clients had a similar issue is yet to be confirmed. In other words, the closer Glamsterdam gets, the more edge cases around the fork boundary developers have to catch.

#5171. How Ethereum staking hit a record 36.3% despite ETH’s price drop

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Ethereum’s [ETH] staking ratio has reportedly reached an all-time high (ATH)  of 36.3%, accounting for more than one-third of ETH’s total circulating supply. Well, the rising staking ratio suggests that an increasing number of ETH holders are choosing to lock up their tokens rather than sell or actively trade them.

This comes as Ethereum’s validator queues show that at press time, 911,938 ETH was waiting to be staked with a 16-day, 7-hour wait. Meanwhile, 264,211 ETH was queued to exit with a 5-day, 16-hour wait, plus a 7-day sweep delay.

Both queues are limited by the same churn rate of 256 per epoch, meaning only a fixed amount of ETH can join or leave each epoch, which is why a big backlog translates into longer waits.

In other words, more people want to stake than unstake right now, but the entry line is slowly shrinking, and exiting takes roughly 12-13 days in total once the sweep delay is included.

This comes as the total ETH staked rose from about 42.1 million ETH (roughly 34.5% of supply) in mid-August to about 44.5 million ETH (around 36.5%) at press time. Since late October the climb has resumed steadily.

Of this, BitMine earned $121.4 million from staking and validation activities, accounting for approximately 97% of its total revenue. By the 8th of November, the largest Ethereum DAT held 6.07 million ETH, of which 5.22 million ETH was staked.

That said, the company also estimates that these holdings could generate around $309 million in annual staking rewards, based on an estimated 2.65% yield and an ETH price of $2,234.

On the other hand, SharpLink has accumulated 31,363 ETH in staking rewards as of 9th November. Its strategy also includes liquid staking through Lido, allowing it to receive wstETH in return for its deposited ETH.

All of this happened while the active validators moved in the opposite direction. As per charts, they held near 900K through mid-September, peaking around 912K. But again, they dropped sharply from mid-September to mid-October, falling to about 815K, and have since drifted down to roughly 807K, with a small additional drop on the last day or two.

However, the price action of ETH trading at $2,042.94 at press time after a modest drop in the past 24 hours but a 15.5% drop in the past week sparks concern. This comes as Ethereum faces renewed selling pressure after losing its $2,250 support level. However, Vitalik Buterin’s recent push to position ETH as a store of value suggests hope.

#5170. Bitcoin Short-Term Holders Send $3.6 Billion in BTC to Exchanges at a Loss

Bitcoin Short-Term Holders Send $3.6 Billion in BTC to Exchanges at a Loss

Bitcoin investors who bought recently are moving tens of thousands of coins to cryptocurrency exchanges at a loss, adding to concerns about selling pressure as the cryptocurrency falls toward the low-$70,000 range.

CryptoQuant’s latest short-term holder profit-and-loss chart shows roughly 47,300 BTC transferred to exchanges below their acquisition price over 24 hours, compared with only about 1,100 BTC arriving at a profit.

Combined inflows stood near 48,500 BTC, or around $3.6 billion at Bitcoin’s current price of approximately $74,000.

The research company's chart below shows transfers, not completed sales. Sending Bitcoin to an exchange can be the first step toward selling, but some deposits are made for trading for other tokens, collateral, or other services. The data also does not establish exactly how much of the transferred Bitcoin was subsequently sold.

The distinction is important because the chart measures the behavior of short-term holders, a group that CryptoQuant generally defines as investors holding Bitcoin for less than 155 days.

These are often the buyers most exposed to a sudden price reversal, particularly when their holdings are worth less than they paid.

In a November 11 CryptoQuant analysis, contributor Amr Taha identified two large waves of underwater exchange deposits over the previous week: 50,790 BTC on November 3 and 46,060 BTC on November 10.

“On November 10, profitable transfers stood near 1,700 BTC, meaning approximately 27 BTC arrived at a loss for every 1 BTC transferred at a profit,” Taha wrote.

That is an unusually one-sided flow. Investors moving coins into exchanges are overwhelmingly doing so at prices below those at which they acquired them, rather than cashing in gains from the earlier rally.

A similar pattern has occurred over several months, with recurring spikes in loss-making exchange transfers as Bitcoin’s price swung higher and lower. The latest red spike appears near the deepest readings visible since July.

A separate CryptoQuant chart breaks down deposits to Binance by the time since each Bitcoin last moved on-chain. It shows 3,290 BTC arriving from coins last moved one day to one week earlier on November 1, followed by a much larger 8,114 BTC from the same category on November 3.

On November 9, the platform also received 1,925 BTC from coins last moved one to three months earlier, according to Taha’s analysis. Those figures help identify which age groups are active, but they do not necessarily reveal when the owners originally purchased their Bitcoin.

The Binance chart measures one exchange and groups coins by their last on-chain movement. The profit-and-loss charts track short-term holders sending coins to exchanges more broadly.

The timing matters because Bitcoin has been sliding from its late-September highs above $85,000 into the $73,000-to-$74,000 area visible at the end of the screenshots. A newer buyer who paid $80,000 would be sitting on a paper loss of roughly 7.5% with Bitcoin at $74,000; selling at that price would lock it in.

The recent weakness is not limited to short-term holders. Glassnode identified about 4 million BTC held by longer-term investors that had been acquired between $85,000 and $118,000, potentially leaving another pocket of underwater supply above current prices.

#5169. 15 Years Since David Schwartz First Coded XRP Ledger’s Foundation, XRPL’s First Step?

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The first line of code for the core of what would become the XRP Ledger (XRPL) was written about 15 years ago, on November 11, 2011, by David Schwartz.

According to the official XRP account on X, the date 11/11/11 marked the beginning of Schwartz's work on what would later support one of the cryptocurrency industry's established payment-focused blockchain networks.

Schwartz, now Ripple CTO Emeritus, was one of three engineers, alongside Jed McCaleb and Arthur Britto, who began developing the XRP Ledger in 2011. The three were inspired by Bitcoin to build an alternative that would address some of its problems, especially in the areas of sustainability and payments.

In June 2012, the XRP Ledger went live. Shortly after Chris Larsen joined the group in September 2012, a company called NewCoin was formed; it soon changed its name to OpenCoin before becoming Ripple. The founders of the XRPL donated 80 billion XRP, the network’s native digital asset, to the company.

Ripple has locked up most of its XRP in escrow, about 55 billion XRP, and releases 1 billion XRP on the first day of every month.

The anniversary on November 11 marks the start of the XRP Ledger’s development, as what began with Schwartz's first code in 2011 eventually morphed into a blockchain network designed to enable value transfers.

November is also a big month for XRP as U.S. spot XRP ETFs celebrate the first anniversary of their landmark launch.Canary Capital’s XRP exchange-traded fund, listed under the ticker XRPC, began trading on November 13, 2025, and is the first spot XRP ETF on a U.S. exchange.

In upgrade news, XRPL version 3.5.0 is scheduled for December 3, 2026. This release includes only improvements and fixes, including a permanent fix for occasional slow ledger close times.

#5168. Bitcoin Drops Below $68K as Buyers Quietly Vanish

Home » Bitcoin (BTC) » Bitcoin Drops Below $68K as Buyers Quietly Vanish

On-chain metrics show a massive 362,000 BTC demand gap opening up across major markets.

Bitcoin crashed below the key $70,000 support level on Friday, hitting a three-month low of $67,650 as a sudden leadership crisis at Nvidia, new MSCI index rules, and hot U.S. inflation rattled global markets.

Bitcoin fell 8.6% in 24 hours to $67,650 on early Friday, its lowest mark since August 19, extending a 10.2% weekly decline.

While yesterday’s pullback below $73,000 was driven largely by macroeconomic jitters, Friday’s breakdown reflects a rare convergence of tech-sector turmoil, institutional index risks, and evaporating spot liquidity.

Adding structural pressure to crypto equities, index provider MSCI’s November Index Review has placed Bitcoin treasury companies, including Michael Saylor’s Strategy (MSTR) and Tokyo-listed Metaplanet, at risk of losing their place in major stock market indexes.

Under MSCI’s updated eligibility rules companies that hold too much of their assets in investments rather than running an operating business could lose their eligibility for indexes such as the MSCI USA and MSCI World.

When a company is dropped from a major index, funds that track it often have to sell their shares, which can drive down demand and put pressure on the stock price.

Strategy’s shares dropped 6.49% in extended trading on Thursday. The company has previously warned that eventual removal from MSCI indexes could force passive funds to sell around $2.8 billion worth of its shares.

For Bitcoin, the bigger concern is that Strategy could slow its Bitcoin purchases if it becomes harder or more expensive to raise money. That would remove a major source of buying demand just as U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs have recorded five consecutive days of outflows totaling $1.43 billion, according to SoSoValue.

Market sentiment weakened further on November 12 after Nvidia announced that CEO and co-founder Jensen Huang was stepping down with immediate effect. CFO Colette Kress was named interim CEO.

Huang’s abrupt departure follows an internal board investigation and media reports detailing alleged extramarital affairs, including one with a alleged Chinese spy as well as relationships with Nvidia employees.

Nvidia shares fell as much as 4% on Thursday, extending a month-long decline that has wiped out 27.28% of the stock’s value. The Nvidia news added to pressure on risk assets, alongside declines in the Nasdaq Composite (-0.39%) and S&P 500 (-0.35%).

Rising inflation is adding pressure to both stock and crypto markets. The U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics reported that consumer prices rose 0.4% in October, above the 0.3% forecast. Core inflation, which excludes food and energy, also came in higher than expected at 0.3%, against a forecast of 0.2%.

The higher-than-expected figures have reduced hopes of near-term interest rate cuts and raised expectations of a rate hike.

According to CME Group’s FedWatch tool, markets are now pricing in an 88.5% chance of a 0.25-percentage-point rate increase in December. Higher interest rates could put further pressure on Bitcoin by making safer investments, such as U.S. Treasury bonds, more attractive

Blockchain data suggests Bitcoin lacked enough buying demand to defend the $70,000 level.

According to a CryptoQuant report, demand across spot and perpetual futures markets fell by 362,000 BTC over 30 days, with spot demand declining by 280,000 BTC and futures demand by 82,000 BTC.

Meanwhile, Bitcoin miner transfers to exchanges surged to 2,077 BTC on November 10, roughly 16 times the 30-day daily average of 127 BTC. About 97% of the transfers came from Antpool.

At the same time, stablecoin supply contracted by $14.2 billion over 30 days, pointing to declining liquidity across the crypto market.

According to CryptoQuant analysts, the broader picture remains cautious. Leverage is being unwound, but fresh spot buying has yet to return convincingly. Until demand strengthens, ETF flows turn positive and market liquidity stabilizes, Bitcoin’s recovery is likely to remain fragile, with further downside risks still in play.

#5167. Can TRX/ETH extend its 23% rally after Tron takes the lead with USDT?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

A key shift is taking shape in the market, and it is one traders should keep a close eye on.

On a macro level, the stablecoin market is currently at one of its lowest levels since the 2022 bear market. The total supply of Tether (USDT) has fallen by almost $15 billion from its peak of $310 billion in mid-May. This indicated that liquidity has been flowing out of the crypto market, which is why demand has been pretty low even though Bitcoin is now trading almost 30% off its $126K high.

As the largest stablecoin hub, Ethereum has taken a significant hit too.

According to DeFiLlama, the total amount of stablecoins on Ethereum fell by over $20 billion. This was compounded by Tether as in the last 30 days alone, the total supply of USDT on Ethereum fell by nearly 9% to just over $66 billion. The key takeaway, however, may be Tron’s USDT supply.

As shown in the chart above, USDT supply on the Tron blockchain was down by only just over 1% at press time. This was much lower than the 9% decrease seen on Ethereum. This hinted at a clear divergence in stablecoin liquidity between the two networks.

With Ethereum seeing heavier USDT outflows, Tron’s share of total USDT supply climbed above 52%, reinforcing its position as the dominant network for USDT liquidity.

Interestingly, Tron founder Justin Sun also pointed out this trend in a recent X post, highlighting data from Token Terminal. In the past 12 months, TRON recorded a $16.1 billion hike in stablecoin supply, the highest among 45 tracked blockchains. HyperEVM came second. Arc also had an increase of $1.3 billion while Arbitrum decreased by $4.3 billion. On the contrary, Ethereum saw a more significant $30.6 billion decline.

In short, Tron’s [TRX] stablecoin dominance may be taking down Ethereum. It is also making inroads into other major Layer 1 networks. According to AMBCrypto, with increasing liquidity advantages, TRX is an altcoin to watch when the market moves back into a risk-on environment.

The real impact of this divergence becomes clearer when looking at on-chain activity.

Interestingly, data from the Tron network also seemed to confirm the stablecoins trend. Within a 24-hour period, Tron processed 12,990,075 transactions. This is the highest daily total for the past 7 days, averaging at around 150 transactions per second (TPS). This suggested that there may be consistent demand on the network, with the increase in stablecoins on Tron being due to real demand.

The result? The TRX/ETH ratio has risen by over 23% so far in Q4 and was only 15% away from making up all of its Q3 losses. On top of that, with stablecoin liquidity and on-chain activity between the two projects increasing, this trend may be accelerating.

If this continues, a break above the 0.002-resistance level could become more likely, opening the door for further TRX outperformance against ETH.

Taken together, with increasing stablecoin dominance, high volume, and the 23% rally in the TRX/ETH ratio, 0.002 is a key level. If it breaks through it could be a testement to TRX’s strength, while a failure to clear resistance may leave the pair trading within its current range.

As it stands, the combination of stablecoin inflows and growing network activity means that TRON is well-positioned to outperform Ethereum [ETH] this quarter.

Tron gained significant stablecoin share as Ethereum saw heavier outflows and weaker on-chain activity.

At press time, TRX/ETH was up 23% in Q4, with 0.002 as the next key resistance level.

#5166. ‘AI and Bitcoin Are on One Big Trade Now,’ Says Anthony Pompliano

As Bitcoin’s price continues to fall further, sparking a fresh wave of panic across the broader crypto market, renowned Bitcoin advocate Anthony Pompliano has issued a statement to investors.

In a recent X post published by the pro-Bitcoin advocate on November 13, Pompliano urged investors to remain calm even as Bitcoin revisited $67,000 for the first time since August.

While Pompliano acknowledged the current market weakness, pointing out that Bitcoin was falling to price levels last seen just three months ago, he urged Bitcoin investors to remain calm.

#5165. Aave user loses $305,000 after Across Protocol website hijack

Bitquery's November 12 update estimates $401,900 across nine wallets, including about $305,000 from one Aave deposit token.

A depositor in Aave lost about $305,000 in a deposit token during the Nov. 10 hijack of the cross-chain bridge Across Protocol's website, according to a Bitquery investigation updated Nov. 12.

Bitquery links the loss to spending permissions requested through the compromised interface, exposing a lending position held outside the bridge.

Bitquery's revised trace estimates $401,900 taken from nine wallets across five blockchains, using prices at the time the assets were stolen, and the Aave depositor accounts for about 76% of the traced losses. The update adds a previously missed ether sweep.

Across Protocol said its domain was taken over on Nov. 10 through social engineering involving its registrar, Namecheap.

The asset taken from the largest wallet was aWETH, an interest-bearing token representing an Aave deposit. Aave's documentation explains that its deposit tokens can be transferred and that a signed permit can authorize another account to spend them.

In Bitquery's reconstruction, the fake site requested a permit, which the attacker then used to move the depositor's tokens, and that spending permission put the separate lending position within reach.

The first transfer transaction on Nov. 10 recorded an unlimited approval followed by about 115.23 aWETH sent to one wallet and 28.81 aWETH to another. Bitquery identifies those recipients as the attacker's wallet and the drainer's fee wallet, respectively, matching an 80/20 split.

The depositor's transfer history shows four pulls ending three minutes after the first. Bitquery says the owner revoked the approval a minute after the last pull, but the transfers had already happened by that point.

On Nov. 11, Across Protocol said the domain was back under its control and pledged to make affected users whole and publish a postmortem.

#5164. Stablecoin Drain, ETF Outflows Leave Bitcoin 'Running on Empty'

Stablecoin Drain, ETF Outflows Leave Bitcoin 'Running on Empty'

The pool of dollars that fuels crypto trading is draining.

Total stablecoin supply fell by $14.2 billion over the 30 days through Nov. 9, the fastest contraction since June 2022, according to a report published Wednesday by onchain analytics firm Glassnode.

The total stablecoin supply now sits just below $280 billion, and the research company suggests that's a big reason why Bitcoin is currently "running on empty."

Stablecoins, tokens typically pegged to the U.S. dollar, serve as crypto’s cash. Traders park money in them on exchanges and onchain before buying other assets.

When the stablecoin supply shrinks, it means holders are redeeming tokens for dollars and pulling that money out of crypto entirely, which Glassnode said leaves “less fuel for a rally.” The firm said a return to supply growth would be the first sign that fresh money is coming in.

The stablecoin drain isn’t the only sign of money leaving. U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs saw about $1.6 billion in net redemptions over 20 trading sessions, the report found, and Bitcoin has traded at a discount on Coinbase, a gauge of U.S. demand.

Bitcoin also slipped below the average cost basis of short-term holders, about $75,600, leaving most recent buyers underwater, and traded near $73,000 on Wednesday. Over $3.6 billion in Bitcoin was recently moved over to exchanges, potentially to be sold, at a loss, according to a separate report from CryptoQuant today.

Glassnode did point to some silver linings. The slide has flushed out most leveraged long positions near the current price, buy orders outweigh sell orders in the order book, and wallets holding between 100 and 10,000 BTC have started accumulating.

The firm flagged $73,000 as key support and said the broader trend change that began in August remains intact as long as Bitcoin holds above its June low.

The contraction comes as stablecoins face growing competition from traditional finance.

Global banks including Goldman Sachs, Citi, and Deutsche Bank are backing a $270 million challenge to crypto’s dominant stablecoin issuers, and the Federal Reserve recently unveiled stablecoin rules on reserves and capital.
