#5163. Bitcoin dominance hits 61%, but THIS will decide when is altcoin season!

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Altcoins have been waiting for their moment all year. Bitcoin [BTC] keeps taking it from them. However, it would seem now that the latter’s market share could decide who wins the next few weeks.

Coinglass’s Altcoin Season Index had a reading of 42 at press time.

Anything above 75 ordinarily counts as altcoin season. This means that the press time reading still leaned towards Bitcoin.

There’s numbers to back this up too. According to CryptoRank, for instance, Bitcoin was up 16.6% over the last 90 days.

Bitway [BTW] has risen by about 220%, Quant [QNT] went up 184%, Midnight Network [NIGHT] by 172% and Raydium [RAY] by 152%. Stacks and NEAR also doubled on the price charts.

A lot of the big names didn’t even keep up with BTC. Solana [SOL], for instance, went up a measly 10% over the same stretch. Binance Coin [BNB] also rose by 10.6%, and Chainlink [LINK] outdid them… with 12%.

So yes, a few altcoins are having a great run. Most of the rest are just watching Bitcoin ride off into the sunset.

At the time of writing, BTC.D was at 60.9%.

On the weekly chart, that’s a range that’s been narrowing for more than a year. The top of that has been sliding since late 2025. The bottom has been going up from around 57%.

Right now, it’s pushing right against the top.

If dominance breaks above 61.5%, Bitcoin will maintain its lead and altcoins will stay stuck in the mud. If it diverges near 61%, that would mean that the latter group will get a little breathing room.

A drop below 58% is where things could really swing towards Ethereum [ETH] and the rest of the market though.

For context, its dominance peaked at about 66% back in mid-2025. We are nowhere near that yet.

Until then, the market should lie in wait. Altcoin holders will really want that rejection at the 61%-level though.

Only 31 altcoins surpassed Bitcoin’s 16.6% gains over the last 90 days.

#5162. Coinbase to Delist 12 Crypto Trading Pairs, ADA-ETH and XLM-BTC Among Them

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. Major crypto exchange Coinbase is set to suspend trading in 12 crypto trading pairs as part of an effort to improve overall market health and consolidate liquidity on the platform. This decision affects two trading pairs involving Cardano (ADA) and Stellar (XLM): ADA/ETH and XLM/BTC.

Coinbase said it regularly monitors the markets on its exchange and, in line with this, trading will be suspended on the named markets on November 25, 2026. In an effort to improve overall market health and consolidate liquidity, Coinbase will be removing a number of non-USD trading pairs for supported assets.

According to an announcement, Coinbase will suspend trading on the following markets on November 25, 2026, at or around 12 PM ET: APE-USDT, ATOM-USDT, ALGO-BTC, CRV-BTC, ETC-BTC, ICP-BTC, XLM-BTC, APE-EUR, ADA-ETH, 1INCH-GBP, CHZ-GBP, SNX-GBP.

However, Coinbase Advanced Trade users in eligible regions can still trade these assets in the USD order books.

In light of this, APE-USDT, ATOM-USDT, ALGO-BTC, CRV-BTC, ETC-BTC, ICP-BTC, XLM-BTC, APE-EUR, ADA-ETH, 1INCH-GBP, CHZ-GBP have been moved to limit-only mode on Coinbase Exchange and Coinbase Advanced.

In recent delistings, Coinbase has disabled trading for Balancer (BAL) and Tensor (TNSR), but users will continue to have the ability to withdraw their funds at any time.

The suspension of trading for Stargate Finance (STG) and Vulcan Forged (PYR) has also been postponed to November 20, 2026, at or around 2 PM ET. Based on recent reviews, Coinbase will suspend trading for Ampleforth Governance Token (FORTH) on November 23, 2026, at or around 2 PM ET. Trading for Neon EVM (NEON) and Syndicate (SYND) will be suspended on November 16, 2026, at or around 2 PM ET.

Spot trading for Pearl (PEARL) will go live on November 13, 2026. The opening of the PEARL-USD trading pair will begin at or after 9 AM PT, if liquidity conditions are met, in regions where trading is supported.

Perpetual futures trading for GRAM, GRASS, QNT and ZRO will go live on November 13, 2026, at or after 12:00 PM UTC.

#5161. NYSE Taps Avalanche to Power Tokenized Stock Trading as Wall Street Embraces Blockchain Infrastructure

Home » Crypto Market News » NYSE Taps Avalanche to Power Tokenized Stock Trading as Wall Street Embraces Blockchain Infrastructure

NYSE has selected Avalanche technology to support settlement for its planned tokenized securities platform, bringing round-the-clock stock trading and faster settlement closer to reality, pending regulatory approval.

The New York Stock Exchange is mixing together blockchain technology and traditional finance. It selected Avalanche to power the settlement infrastructure of its upcoming tokenized securities platform. The decision is part of a growing effort to bring crypto trading and its benefits to traditional finance.

Announced on November 9, 2026, the partnership will see Avalanche provide the core ledger technology for the clearing firm supporting the NYSE-affiliated digital trading venue. One of the goals is to enable round-the-clock trading, as well as clearing and settlement of tokenized securities, all of which still require regulatory changes.

NYSE has organized a yearlong test, and afterwards Avalanche emerged as a favorite provider and one that could meet its needs and provide a seamless inclusion of blockchain traders into the fold of the stock exchange account holders.

The platform is designed to support equities issued across multiple blockchain networks, meaning Avalanche will not necessarily host every tokenized stock traded through the venue. The technology will therefore mostly be used to record and settle transactions.

NYSE Head of Digital Issuance Dennis Lee said that the focus was on speed, reliability, and predictable costs. Ava Labs CEO Emin Gün Sirer also highlighted the importance of dedicated infrastructure.

The crypto infrastructure industry is huge, and there’s a growing list of cryptocurrency exchanges, including those reviewed by CryptoManiaks, to choose from. Platforms made for transfers and smart contracts automating payments have also grown and now include thousands of providers. NYSE chose Avalanche due to some of its unique characteristics.

Avalanche’s architecture allows businesses to create dedicated blockchain networks with rules tailored to their operational and regulatory requirements. It creates a flexibility that traditional financial institutions are looking for.

Stock exchanges handle countless transactions, and stability is the most important feature they are looking for. Another important feature is atomic settlement. It allows the transfer of securities and corresponding payment to occur as part of the same transaction. Therefore, there’s no chance one side of a trade is completed while the other remains outstanding.

NYSE also plans to operate a custom blockchain built using Avalanche technology rather than relying directly on Avalanche’s public network.

The decision reflects a broader effort to tokenize traditional markets and get the best of both worlds. That way, crypto companies won’t dominate the space, but can combine their features with those of the stock market.

Nasdaq is among the NYSE’s most prominent competitors. It, too, has developed tokenized stock infrastructure and is pursuing partnerships that could help distribute blockchain-based securities. This is done in cooperation with Kraken and in a way that NYSE is responding to the demands of a competitive market.

The Depository Trust & Clearing Corporation (DTCC), a central institution in US securities settlements, also has plans to introduce tokenization. It plans to use blockchain to support securities custody and transfers.

NYSE has also expanded its list of partners. It now includes Broadridge Financial Solutions, Computershare, Equiniti, and Superstate. These developments show that financial institutions are fighting to establish the underlying infrastructure that will determine how digital securities are traded, transferred, and settled.

What Tokenized Stocks Could Mean for Investors

Tokenized stocks could change how investors are accessing traditional financial markets. The possibility of trading outside of business hours is the first thing investors have been looking for, alongside faster settlements. The NYSE’s broader platform plans include stablecoin-based funding, fractional share trading, and immediate settlement.

Ownership will also change. The NYSE has indicated that its platform will support tokenized shares that are fungible with traditionally issued securities, as well as securities originally issued in digital form. Shareholders will continue to have the same rights as traditional owners, meaning they’ll receive dividends and take part in corporate governance.

All of these benefits aren’t regulated, and before investors can take advantage of them, traditional regulatory bodies need to act and establish rules and guidelines for the new features.

Despite the institutional interest, the NYSE’s Avalanche-powered project still faces several challenges before it can become a functioning alternative to conventional securities infrastructure. The platform needs to demonstrate that it can maintain the safeguards expected of a major US stock exchange.

There’s also a distinction between Avalanche’s institutional adoption and the potential benefits for its native AVAX token. It’s not clear if the cooperation with NYSE will generate demand for AVAX. The project will depend on its ability to attract issuers, brokers, and investors while delivering reliable settlement.

#5160. Circle hands USDC reserves to EU regulators risking higher fees

Circle’s EU-issued USDC moves under EBA supervision, potentially raising capital, liquidity and compliance requirements.

Nov. 12 marks the transfer of supervision of EU-issued USDC’s financial safeguards to the European Banking Authority, according to Circle’s quarterly filing.

Under the disclosed arrangements, the European regulator oversees reserves and redemption planning, while France’s financial regulator keeps governance responsibilities.

For Circle’s European issuer, the change could mean more capital or liquidity held in the EU and higher compliance costs. Circle says it is evaluating the impact, without putting a figure on those potential obligations in the disclosure.

The transfer follows the EBA’s Oct. 14 classification of EU-issued USDC as a significant e-money token under the Markets in Crypto-Assets Regulation (MiCA). Circle describes USDC as the first e-money token to receive that designation.

The handover was planned since October, establishing the token’s classification. The November handover sets the effective date for the stated division of supervisory work.

The allocation applies to USDC issued in the EU by Circle Internet Financial Europe SAS, known as Circle France.

Circle says the EBA’s direct supervisory remit covers the issuer’s own funds (regulatory capital), reserve assets, and liquidity requirements. It also oversees recovery and redemption plans.

France’s Autorité de Contrôle Prudentiel et de Résolution (ACPR) remains responsible for governance, qualifying holdings, fit-and-proper assessments, outsourcing, and conflicts of interest. Circle continues to face national oversight in those areas alongside the EBA’s financial-safeguard remit.

The filing also reports an EBA memorandum of understanding with the New York State Department of Financial Services to facilitate cooperation and information exchange on cross-border stablecoin activities.

The memorandum covers cooperation and information exchange, as well as the allocation of supervisory responsibilities for the EU issuer.

Circle says the significant-token classification may bring increased regulatory capital, reserve, liquidity and other prudential requirements under MiCA. These could require additional capital or liquidity in the EU, raise compliance costs or adversely affect its business and financial results.

The disclosure provides no estimate of possible additional capital, liquidity, or compliance spending, and identifies no quantified financial charge arising on Nov. 12. Whether additional commitments will be needed, and their potential size, remain unresolved in that assessment.

Supervision of redemption plans concerns the issuer’s safeguards, and the note announces no revised cash-out terms or new holder fees. The disclosure identifies the responsible supervisors but does not specify any change to holders’ redemption arrangements.

#5159. SUI crypto’s 26% drop sparks bottom calls – Is a ‘short‑term bounce’ next?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Sui [SUI] crypto token was trading at $0.7799 as of writing, after a drop of 4.6% in the past 24 hours. In fact, in just one month the altcoin has fallen by over 26%, which has raised eyebrows across the community.

However, the SUI Community has taken this drop from an optimistic lens and suggested that SUI’s price may be nearing its bottom. They argue that sell-side liquidity, such as stop-loss orders, is piling up just below the low range.

Their theory, based on Wyckoff-style analysis, is that price may briefly dip below the range to trigger those orders, then reverse sharply upward and break out.

If the candles break lower, we could see a short‑term bounce back soon.

For context, this analysis was done when SUI was trading around $0.81, and since then the altcoin has fallen even further.

Echoing similar sentiments, another X account added,

However, not everyone shared the same sentiment, as another analyst shared his opinion and said,

So, if looked at carefully, SUI has already experienced its major price decline and is now in a consolidation phase. During this period, impatient investors may sell their holdings out of frustration, while patient investors continue accumulating.

Many believe that this phase could eventually lead to an expansion phase, potentially triggering another upward price move.

This comes as Suiet, a self-custodial wallet for the Sui blockchain, has announced plans to shut down development and maintenance, with its API services ending on the 14th of November.

All of this also resulted in Sui’s total value locked (TVL) falling more than 23% in a month, from $510.9 million to $394.27 million, raising more questions.

This somewhat fits the Sui Community’s “shake-out” idea, since the dip below the range likely cleared out leveraged longs before the bounce.

Remarking on the same, known crypto analyst, Ted Pillows added,

Late longs have been wiped out, and now shorts are getting aggressive. Only a matter of time before these shorts will get liquidated.

#5158. Nvidia Futures Top XRP in Crypto Liquidations as Jensen Huang’s Exit Sparks $2.14 Billion Surge

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The daily trading volume of the NVDA-USDC perpetual contract on the decentralized exchange Hyperliquid has reached a record $2.14 billion, according to CoinGlass. The contract is provided by the Trade.xyz protocol, and only Bitcoin had higher trading volume on Hyperliquid over the same period.

Nvidia futures also surpassed Ethereum, Solana and XRP in trading volume, and the combined volume of Nvidia derivatives across all tracked crypto exchanges reached $9.51 billion, according to CoinGlass.

The latest surge in trading comes amid the resignation of Jensen Huang, the co-founder of Nvidia and the only CEO in the history of the company. Huang stepped down on Nov. 12 amid an internal investigation, and Nvidia CFO Colette Kress has taken over as interim CEO.

On Nasdaq, the price of NVDA went down by as much as 14% during the day, but the stock later recovered most of the loss and closed at $166.19, down 4.34%.

Sharp price moves cost leveraged traders on crypto exchanges $40.86 million, according to CoinGlass. Of that amount, $26.23 million came from the on-chain ticker XYZ:NVDA, and $14.63 million came from the base NVDA contract. The largest single liquidation on Hyperliquid was a $1.54 million long position.

In the base NVDA contract, $8.87 million in long positions and $5.89 million in short positions were liquidated. During the same period, the price of Nvidia futures on crypto exchanges went up by 5.6% to $171.17.

Liquidations in the NVDA contracts were almost twice as large as those in XRP, where traders lost $20.75 million over the same period. The price of XRP went down by 2.5% to $1.0192, and long positions accounted for 94.5% of XRP liquidations, or $19.6 million.

The liquidations came amid a sell-off across major cryptocurrencies. The price of Bitcoin dropped below $70,000 to $69,450.50, down 5.3% over 24 hours, and Ethereum fell 4.1% to $1,958.71.

Over the past 24 hours, crypto exchanges liquidated 187,389 traders for a total of $1.65 billion, and 88% of that amount came from long positions, according to CoinGlass. Bitcoin traders lost the most at $919.56 million, followed by Ethereum traders at $297.24 million.

Finally, Nvidia is expected to report its quarterly earnings on Nov. 17.

#5157. Nvidia-Backed CoreWeave Is Surging: Here’s What It Means for the AI Boom

Nvidia-Backed CoreWeave Is Surging: Here’s What It Means for the AI Boom

Nvidia-backed CoreWeave soared 24.7% Wednesday, helping revive the AI trade after weeks of selling, as the company reported surging demand for the expensive computing hardware powering the artificial intelligence boom.

The cloud provider posted $3.66 billion in third-quarter revenue, up 168% from a year ago, and raised its 2026 sales forecast to $13 billion to $13.3 billion. The results also helped lift Nvidia, along with AI infrastructure stocks including Nebius, IREN, Oracle and Applied Digital.

For investors watching Nvidia, CoreWeave offers a useful look at what happens after the chipmaker sells its processors.

CoreWeave buys Nvidia GPUs, installs them in data centers, and rents that computing power to companies developing or running AI models, including OpenAI and other major customers.

That makes its revenue growth evidence that businesses are still willing to pay for AI computing capacity. It does not, by itself, establish that the enormous spending required to supply that capacity will produce sustainable profits.

Nvidia's connection goes well beyond a typical supplier relationship. In January, Nvidia invested another $2 billion in CoreWeave shares at $87.20 each, expanding a business arrangement aimed at building more than five gigawatts of AI data-center capacity by 2030.

The companies also agreed to introduce new Nvidia technology across CoreWeave's infrastructure, including its Rubin platform and Vera CPUs. That makes CoreWeave an important showcase and customer for future Nvidia hardware, not just the chips already installed.

“Together, we’re racing to meet extraordinary demand for NVIDIA AI factories,” Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang said when the expanded deal was announced in January.

Nvidia also has a $6.3 billion initial agreement to buy CoreWeave's unused computing capacity through April 2032, subject to the contract's conditions. In practice, the arrangement gives CoreWeave a buyer for capacity it cannot sell elsewhere, while Nvidia gets access to cloud computing built around its own chips.

The relationship explains both the excitement and some of the criticism surrounding AI investment. Nvidia sells the hardware, invests in a company buying that hardware, and has also committed to purchasing some of the computing services it helps create. Inventors call it “circular finance.” AI bros call it a meme. Skeptics call it a warning sign.

Nvidia gives money to OpenAI who gives money to Coreweave who gives money to Nvidia who gives money to OpenAi who gives money to Coreweave who gives money to OpenAi who gives money to Nvidia pic.twitter.com/3UFfK7KwJv

—  Q-Cap  (@qcapital2020) September 22, 2025

Those are real commercial agreements, but the overlapping financial relationships complicate attempts to measure independent demand.

CoreWeave reported $132.5 billion in revenue backlog, meaning contracted business that it expects to deliver over time, not money already in the bank. The company says it is working with customers including OpenAI, Cohere, and Perplexity, and had more than 1.7 gigawatts of active power capacity at the end of September.

“This was a breakout quarter for CoreWeave, with revenue increasing by more than $1 billion sequentially,” CEO Michael Intrator said in the company's earnings release.

The company also expects about $9 billion in customer prepayments and deposits over the next three months. Getting paid before delivering all the contracted services could reduce its immediate need to borrow at a time when investors have become increasingly uncomfortable with the debt financing AI data centers.

The market reaction extended beyond CoreWeave. Wednesday's rebound touched Nvidia shares and the broader technology market, with the Nasdaq gaining 1.6%. CoreWeave's report arrived as investors were trying to decide whether recent AI-stock losses reflected weakening demand or anxiety about the cost of meeting it.

These specialized GPU-rental businesses are known as neoclouds. Some—including CoreWeave, which began as an Ethereum miner—have roots in crypto infrastructure, where operators were already accustomed to running large amounts of power-hungry hardware.

Bernstein analysts described the quarter as “about the best quarter we could have asked for,” according to Reuters, but warned CoreWeave would be among the companies hit hardest if the shortage of available AI computing capacity begins to ease.

The Nvidia partnership does not remove that risk. Newer processors can make older GPUs less competitive, while electricity availability, construction delays, and higher borrowing costs can all squeeze returns. An oversupply of AI computing would also put pressure on the rental prices CoreWeave earns.

#5156. Ethereum unveils quantum-safe accounts, with a costly catch

Ethereum researchers propose quantum-resistant accounts by 2027, with higher transaction costs until scaling upgrades arrive.

Ethereum research group Ethlabs has proposed giving users a route to quantum-resistant accounts before Ethereum can process those transactions at full scale.

Its Nov. 11 plan would let users adopt the protection earlier, but their quantum-enabled transactions would cost more than ordinary transactions because they take up more space.

The proposal pqai27 targets readiness for quantum-resistant accounts and consensus in 2027. Full-capacity support is a stretch goal for that year, and the target remains a proposal.

At “pqai-ready,” Ethereum would have post-quantum consensus and every user would have a post-quantum route for their account. At “pqai-scaled,” it would support full-capacity quantum-enabled transactions and blobs, the data objects used by rollups.

The first stage would allow users to migrate to post-quantum accounts and transact with a larger footprint. Signature aggregation and post-quantum data availability would provide the later path to full scale.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Ethlabs proposes readiness in 2027, with full-scale post-quantum transactions and blobs as a stretch goal. Users would still need wallet support and migration.

At readiness, wallet support enables account migration and higher-cost post-quantum transactions.

Rollups could choose PQ calldata at readiness. PQ blobs belong to the proposed full-scale stage.

Early protection would carry a transaction cost premium; full scale needs additional protocol work.

The 2027 goal is a proposal; full scale is a stretch goal. Wallet migration is separate work. The proposal supplies no numerical fee premium, and full-capacity support does not establish fee parity.

Conceptual dependency diagram using Ethlabs’ proposed stages and account/rollup routes. Arrows express dependencies and sequencing, not measured capacity, fee amounts or fixed activation dates. No quantitative calculations.

The 2027 goal is a proposal; full scale is a stretch goal. Wallet migration is separate work. The proposal supplies no numerical fee premium, and full-capacity support does not establish fee parity.

Ethlabs proposes an optional fallback to calldata from quantum-enabled transactions, carrying their data in regular blocks before post-quantum blobs are available.

Ethlabs places an orderly user migration, including tooling and wallet support, outside the network upgrade schedule. Protocol readiness would leave practical work for wallets and users before adoption is complete.

Draft EIP-8141, known as Frame Transactions, provides part of the proposed foundation. It would let account code define how transactions are validated and how gas is paid, creating a native route away from today's elliptic-curve signatures and supporting key rotation.

Ethlabs’ plan pairs Frames with the SPHINCS post-quantum signature scheme and other account proposals. Upgrading consensus remains a separate, substantial task, with the choice of design unresolved.

Ethereum.org’s Hegotá roadmap, last updated Oct. 16, lists Frames as scheduled for inclusion in that upgrade, which remains in planning with an unconfirmed date. That earlier listing is separate from Ethlabs’ new proposal for broader quantum readiness in 2027.

Ethlabs says it will withdraw its Quick Slots proposal for faster slots from Hegotá and redirect its resources toward pqai27.

For users considering the proposed account route, the practical milestones are wallet migration support and the cost of quantum-enabled transactions, followed by the aggregation and data upgrades needed to use them at full scale.

#5155. Bitcoin volatility falls to 45% – Here’s what it does NOT mean for BTC

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Crypto evaluation, especially for Bitcoin [BTC], tends to be shifting from highly volatile to a mature asset. Bitcoin volatility has fallen to 45% over its 18 years of existence, within the range of common large-cap U.S. stocks.

For instance, JPMorgan and Amazon are at 80% and 90%, respectively, meaning they are a bit more volatile than BTC. Oracle, Coinbase, Micron, and Sandisk are more stable, with their volatilities well below 20%.

This current reading of Bitcoin volatility reflects an asset that has matured with greater institutional participation. However, this does not mean that BTC is now a low-risk asset.

Therefore, the asset deserves another risk framework different from how it was viewed in the past 10 years. During its initial stages, the volatility was exceeding 100% against a capitalization of single-digit billions.

Interestingly, during periods of strong positive momentum, Bitcoin’s gains were achieved faster than the downside deviation as per the Sortino ratio. Still, that does not negate sudden and more aggressive nosedives.

Therefore, maturity has been driven by clearer regulations, more secure investment vehicles like ETFs, and institutional involvement. BlackRock, Grayscale, Strategy, and Morgan Stanley are a few of the deeply involved institutions in BTC adoption.

The maturity of Bitcoin has created opportunities for a more diversified crypto portfolio. Other crypto sectors are becoming less tied to Bitcoin’s price performance, an edge ahead of traditional assets.

Most traditional assets are related to one another.

For instance, the annualized volatility of Smart Contracts Platforms is at 58%, with BTC contributing 38% while sector-specific drive was 20%.

However, Artificial Intelligence (AI) was at 85%, probably because it was somehow new in the financial markets. For this, Bitcoin-driven volatility was 35%, while sector-specific was 50%.

Events like the resignation of the CEO of Nvidia, Jensen Huang, after having an affair with a Chinese spy, have greatly impacted the AI market sector.

Other sectors in crypto, like financials, are at 59%, with 30% being Bitcoin-driven. This phenomenon is quite evident with seasonality in the crypto market.

But Bitcoin seasons are characterized by selective altcoin performance. On the other hand, altcoin seasons have BTC being performed by more than 75 alts in the top 100 by capitalization. Currently, only 45 altcoins have performed better than Bitcoin in the past 90 days.

All in all, crypto maturity is not only on Bitcoin but also in the whole sector. Therefore, a crypto risk framework should be done across more than one dimension to reflect its evolution.

XRP added almost 20% in open interest on MEXC, which could be a double-edged sword for the price of the asset. Across all exchanges, open interest went up by only 0.86%, reaching $2.25 billion.

XRP is not benefiting directly from an increase in open interest; it could reflect an increase in futures positions.

Futures by themselves are a source of strong volatility and liquidity for assets. Considering the state of the market, XRP's increase in open interest is not unexpected.

Over the past 24 hours, $18.83 million in XRP positions were liquidated, with longs making up 94%.

XRP already dipped to $0.9961 earlier today before going back above the threshold. A cluster of long liquidations sits right below that level.

XRP is teetering on the edge of the $1 threshold, which, if broken, essentially enables a rapid decrease.

In addition, US spot XRP ETFs recorded zero net flows on November 12.

Unfortunately, volume shows an uptick, a direct sign of increasing pressure on the asset. Spot volume went up by 46.7%, with sellers outweighing buyers by $48.2 million.

It is not a guarantee of a bearish market strengthening, but it shows that sellers are there and ready to push the price lower. Whether buyers can defend the $1 level or not is the main test for the asset right now.

#5153. Pressure Mounts on Nvidia's Jensen Huang Amid Reports of Second Affair

Pressure Mounts on Nvidia's Jensen Huang Amid Reports of Second Affair

Nvidia's board is preparing to remove CEO Jensen Huang after reports that he had two extramarital affairs, one with a woman U.S. intelligence believes is a Chinese spy and another with a company executive, The Wall Street Journal reported on Wednesday.

The board was planning to meet as soon as Wednesday to determine Huang's future, according to people familiar with the matter cited by the Journal. It was not immediately clear who would succeed him, the paper said.

The New York Times reported on Wednesday that Huang had an extramarital relationship with Kimberly Powell, Nvidia's vice president of health care, who reports to him, citing five people who worked at the company. The Journal also reported the relationship, citing a person familiar with the matter.

Nvidia's code of conduct tells employees to avoid situations that create conflicts, including personal relationships, according to the Times. When the newspaper reached him by phone, Huang said, "I don't think you should be talking to me directly," before hanging up. Nvidia declined to comment, and Powell did not respond to requests for comment from either paper.

It is the second affair involving Huang to come to light. The New York Times reported in October that U.S. officials had concluded Alice Huang, an executive at Singapore-based Nvidia customer Megaspeed, was a Chinese intelligence asset who had developed a close relationship with Jensen Huang. The Financial Times reported last week that a U.S. government memo said the two had been romantically involved. China has denied the allegations.

On Sunday, Huang acknowledged in a letter to staff that he had been unfaithful to his wife, without naming the person involved. He had told Nvidia's board that he had been intimate with Alice Huang, the Times reported, prompting the board to open an independent investigation, led by law firm Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz.

The Justice Department is now leading multiple investigations involving Alice Huang, the Journal reported. Huang also faces a proposed shareholder class action and the threat of a congressional subpoena.

Nvidia shares were down 5.9% at $163.43 in premarket trading early on Thursday, according to Yahoo! Finance data, after closing up 2.9% at $173.73 on Wednesday, before the reports emerged. The stock had already lost about $430 billion in market value on Monday and Tuesday.

Traders expect further losses. On Decrypt Predict, users put an 89% chance on Nvidia trading at or below $160 during regular market hours at some point in November, according to the market. By contrast, they gave just a 6% chance that the stock would climb back to $216 this month.

Huang co-founded Nvidia in 1993 and is its largest shareholder, owning about 3.6% of the company. He has said he does not believe in succession planning, according to the Journal, and the company's latest proxy statement warns that it is "highly dependent" on its longstanding executive team, raising questions over who could replace him.

Potential successors are reportedly under discussion already. One person inside the company told the Financial Times that the simplest option would be to appoint chief financial officer Colette Kress as interim CEO, while two sources pointed to Ian Buck, who runs Nvidia's data center business. Analysts cited Arm CEO Rene Haas, a former Nvidia executive, as a possible outside candidate. "We always knew key man risk was an issue for Nvidia," Bernstein analyst Stacy Rasgon told the paper.

At least one high-profile figure has offered Huang a way forward. Responding to a tweet urging him to hire Huang, Tesla CEO Elon Musk wrote: "That would be an honor."

#5152. Trezor slashes reseller network by 80% following hardware implant discovery – Details

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Trezor confirmed on the 12th of November that three Safe 5 hardware wallets sold by CryptoBilis contained malicious implants. Fortunately, no customer had reported losing funds.

Still, Trezor urged affected buyers to move their crypto after releasing a firmware fix.

Customers bought those 3 devices between the 20th of August and the 23rd of September through CryptoBilis directly or its Tokopedia and Shopee stores. Trezor examined one device in its lab and identified two more from customer photos.

48 buyers had contacted the company, and at press time, checks were ongoing. Trezor reported that there were no confirmed cases involving other models or resellers.

Apparently, the implants could take recovery phrases and passphrases that appeared on the wallet screen.

On X, a user asked how a software update could stop a physical implant from recording the display. Trezor explained that firmware could “listen to the communication between the MCU and the display,” and the firm has now changed the order of commands needed for the trigger.

Regardless, Trezor still urged buyers to use a fresh recovery phrase because “the update can’t protect a wallet backup that was captured before it.” This advice applied to customers who bought a Trezor from CryptoBilis from March 2026 onward.

The hardware wallet company also urged those using its devices to move funds to a new wallet with a fresh recovery phrase. It told buyers with unused devices to leave them unconfigured. Eligible buyers could contact Trezor Support for a replacement or a refund where shipping limited their options.

Trezor dropped CryptoBilis on the 9th of October after reports of tampered Ledger devices sold through them. A customer also flagged a suspected Safe 5 implant on the 28th of October.

Trezor issued a warning two days later, saying that it cut its reseller network by about 80% and tightened rules on sourcing, storage, and access.

This brought the debate back to what self-custody protects, and as Ledger CEO Pascal Gauthier said,

Everybody will be hacked at some point. Perfect security doesn’t exist.

#5151. Czech central bank tests DLT bonds, boxing out stablecoins

Czech central bank tests tokenized bonds as its governor backs public-money settlement that could reduce stablecoin demand.

Czech National Bank Governor Aleš Michl wants central bank money to anchor tokenized securities settlement, a preference that could limit the institutional payment balances available to stablecoin issuers.

He set out that position on Nov. 10, as the bank announced it had completed small bond and repo tests.

If eligible institutions can pay through usable central-bank-money infrastructure, those trades need not generate demand for stablecoins. Yet, the CNB has not identified the pilot’s exact cash instrument, so the competitive implication remains forward-looking.

The opportunity for private issuers depends on who supplies the payment asset and whether bonds move onto distributed ledgers.

The CNB reported completed transfers, purchases and sales against payment, and repo transactions during October and November.

According to the bank, settlement was almost instantaneous, but transaction volume was only in the tens of thousands of koruna.

In his Nov. 10 speech, Michl named commercial bank Česká spořitelna as the counterparty, put each bond’s nominal value at CZK 10,000 and said interest payments had also been tested.

The bonds ran on Prague’s Central Securities Depository infrastructure using distributed-ledger technology (DLT). The CNB says only approved financial institutions can access the network, with transaction records visible only to the counterparties.

Michl’s goal is a much larger market for koruna-denominated assets that are easier to buy and sell than German Bunds. That is an ambition for the market, beyond what a small test establishes.

His settlement preference also sits alongside a broader willingness to experiment. A year earlier, CNB Lab had started testing Bitcoin, a dollar stablecoin and a tokenized deposit.

The depository’s general transfer guidance describes a buyer paying its cash account. Receipt of the money triggers the securities transfer and generation of a payment order to the seller.

Crediting the seller then depends on the receiving bank, its support for instant payments, and the transfer timing.

A Nov. 5 presentation by CNB payments-regulation director Michal Vodrážka said settlement in CNB money on DLT was not yet available.

That was five days before the bond-test announcement, which did not specify whether the cash arrangement had changed. The Nov. 5 presentation also said the CNB Board had made no decision to issue a Czech retail central bank digital currency.

Putting a bond on a shared ledger does not determine which institution supplies the money used to buy it.

The Eurosystem launched Pontes on Sept. 21 to enable wholesale tokenized asset transactions to settle in central bank money. An initial group of market participants and DLT operators had completed onboarding, with more institutions expected to connect.

Completed October-November bond and repo tests were announced on Nov. 10, 2026. Select another state to compare the cash-settlement model.

Michl wants central-bank money to anchor securities settlement. This is an objective; the pilot does not establish its delivery on DLT.

The Eurosystem’s separate service offers central-bank-money settlement. Wider availability is being introduced in stages.

A usable central-bank cash option could let tokenized securities grow without creating matching stablecoin demand.

The pilot’s exact cash instrument is undisclosed. Pontes is separate from the Czech tests. Hours expansion is planned; no stablecoin displacement is measured.

Schematic trade flows and three reported states. Undirected connectors pair the securities and payment legs, not asset conversions. Dashed pairs mark test scope or a goal; the solid Pontes pair marks operational settlement. No quantitative chart adds value because test scale and future operating dates are not comparable measurements.

The pilot’s exact cash instrument is undisclosed. Pontes is separate from the Czech tests. Hours expansion is planned; no stablecoin displacement is measured.

Pontes connects market DLT platforms with the Eurosystem’s TARGET Services, and availability is still expanding. In an Oct. 29 speech, the ECB described plans for significantly longer operating hours by mid-2027 and 24/7/365 availability in 2028.

Where an eligible buyer can use connected central bank settlement infrastructure, a tokenized securities purchase need not require a private settlement token.

If that infrastructure becomes more accessible and usable, it could narrow the prospective market for stablecoin balances used to pay for securities.

The Czech network has restricted institutional access, while Pontes is expanding its services in stages.

The next meaningful test of Michl’s vision is how central bank money becomes available for those trades, to which institutions and during which hours.

If a larger tokenized koruna market develops around public settlement money, more securities activity could arrive without a corresponding need for private settlement tokens.

#5150. Bitcoin could face a cash squeeze without another Fed rate hike

The November 12 update uses Q2 2026 data, pointing to a potential financing channel that could matter before another Fed hike.

A New York Fed study published Nov. 12 puts modeled US bank liquidity stress at a level last seen in 2014, highlighting a funding vulnerability that could curb Bitcoin risk-taking even without another Fed hike.

The research update uses bank holding company data through the second quarter of 2026. Its liquidity stress ratio remained moderate by historical standards, at slightly above half its third-quarter 2007 peak.

The ratio compares liabilities weighted for their likelihood of withdrawal, plus off-balance-sheet exposures, with assets weighted for liquidity. Its rise since early 2022 reflects a decline in liquid assets alongside more unstable funding and off-balance-sheet exposures. That leaves banks more exposed if funding becomes harder to retain.

A separate capital measure tests a repeat of the 2022 rate shock. The modeled capital gap was $151 billion in Q2 2026, down from $161 billion a year earlier. It measures capital needed after the hypothetical shock to bring each bank's Tier 1 common equity to at least 10% of risk-weighted assets, fully reflecting unrealized securities gains and losses. That is a model benchmark, not evidence of current realized losses or a regulatory breach.

The potential transmission runs through financing and risk budgets. Banks facing more expensive or less dependable funding could become more selective about lending. Financial intermediaries dependent on that credit could then have less capacity or willingness to hold risky assets, including Bitcoin.

The Q2 2026 model shows a funding mismatch. Whether it becomes a Bitcoin squeeze depends on financing conditions.

Illustrative condition: wider funding and credit spreads alongside weaker Bitcoin financing would support the transmission case.

Illustrative condition: stable funding and financing would weaken the case that modeled bank vulnerability is squeezing Bitcoin.

Bank vulnerability can matter before another Fed hike, but Bitcoin transmission still needs evidence.

Bank-model data end in Q2 2026. The ratio is historically moderate. The Bitcoin path is analytical inference; neither study demonstrates a current Bitcoin financing shock.

Schematic fraction shows the reported liquidity-ratio components and direction of change; no lengths encode amounts. Selectable states illustrate source-backed monitoring criteria, not forecasts or current observations.

Bank-model data end in Q2 2026. The ratio is historically moderate. The Bitcoin path is analytical inference; neither study demonstrates a current Bitcoin financing shock.

A separate Fed funding-shock study published in February explains how weaker capital measured at market values and reliance on uninsured deposits can raise banks' funding costs. Its results depend on the scenario: the added shock hurts profitability and capital under stagflation, but has minimal effects in a severe recession with sharply falling rates.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

There is already evidence of selective credit restraint. The Fed's October lending survey, covering conditions generally in the third quarter, reported tighter approval standards on net across every queried nonbank financial borrower category. Large and middle-market business-loan standards were basically unchanged. CryptoSlate previously examined the possible implications for Bitcoin.

The risk-appetite link has historical support. An August 2023 IMF working paper found that US monetary tightening reduced a common crypto price factor through a risk-taking channel similar to equities. Applying that channel to the new bank-vulnerability findings remains an inference.

The Nov. 12 H.15 release lists effective federal funds at 3.88% for Nov. 11 and the 10-year Treasury yield at 4.98% for Nov. 10. Treasury markets were closed Nov. 11. Those benchmark rates are only part of financing conditions: banks can also restrict access to credit.

For Bitcoin, the next test is whether the modeled vulnerability becomes tighter financing. Widening funding and credit spreads alongside weaker Bitcoin financing would strengthen that case; stable funding conditions would weaken it.

#5148. Simon Dixon: What It Would Take for Me to Sell My Bitcoin for Gold

Simon Dixon, CEO and co-founder of BnkToTheFuture, tells BMTV there are only three things that would make him sell all of his Bitcoin and buy gold.

Simon Dixon, CEO and co-founder of BnkToTheFuture, tells BMTV there are only three things that would make him sell all of his Bitcoin and buy gold. Since 2011, he says Bitcoin has done three things incredibly well: you can earn and hold it in self-custody, spend it peer-to-peer without a government in the middle, and rely on a fixed supply of 21 million as a hedge against long-term debasement. If any of those three is ever called into question, he would sell immediately. The real battle, in his view, is not whether Bitcoin gets adopted — he says institutional adoption is on a trajectory we have never seen — but whether custodians, Wall Street-owned miners, and treasury companies end up centralizing the network.

#5147. Google’s new AI can make 100 decisions in under a second, without writing an answer

The Decisions API returns fixed choices at $0.10 per million input tokens, while confidence and consequential actions still need checks.

Google introduced its Gemini Decisions API on Nov. 11, 2026, letting software ask an AI model for a bounded judgment without generating a written answer. The endpoint returns probabilities and fixed choices that code can use directly to route requests, check policies or score content against a rubric.

Powered by Gemini Decision 1, the service supports yes/no questions, choices from a supplied list and scores against predefined rating scales. It is available through the Gemini API in Google AI Studio, according to Google's launch announcement.

The practical change is in the response software receives: a value it can branch on, accompanied by confidence, without having to parse a chatbot's prose. Generated explanations, tool calls with arguments and multi-step reasoning remain uses for Google's Interactions API, its documentation says.

Google lists a price of $0.10 per million input tokens and no output-token charge. Shared input is billed once within each request, with each question adding its own instruction and criteria tokens. OpenRouter's listing shows the same input and output pricing.

A request can combine text, images, audio, video and PDFs with up to 1,000 independent questions. The total context limit is 262,144 tokens across shared input and all questions. The questions cannot see one another's answers, so code must combine their results.

Gemini Decisions API returns typed judgments and confidence without generating answer text. Software combines the results and decides what to do next.

Illustrative code path after validation on relevant examples. A passed confidence threshold is not a safety guarantee.

Illustrative code path for an ambiguous result: use a fallback or human review. Handle out-of-scope input explicitly.

The model supplies judgments; software owns the action and escalation rules.

Confidence must be validated on relevant labeled data. Choice can select an option even when none fits; scope checks or an other option remain necessary. Passing a threshold does not guarantee safety; Google recommends human or second checks for high-stakes actions. Lines do not encode latency or volume.

Qualitative schematic of shared input, independent question types and typed responses. The selectable panel illustrates confidence-threshold handling in application code, with no numerical threshold or observed traffic assumed.

Confidence must be validated on relevant labeled data. Choice can select an option even when none fits; scope checks or an other option remain necessary. Passing a threshold does not guarantee safety; Google recommends human or second checks for high-stakes actions. Lines do not encode latency or volume.

Google reports end-to-end latency of 150–250 milliseconds for contexts up to 16K tokens, with latency nearly flat across 1–100 questions per request. Those reported measurements cover a smaller workload than the maximum context and question limits.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Google says Gemini Notebook already uses the service to choose model tiers and thinking levels, with up to 40% lower latency for everyday queries while reserving deeper reasoning for complex requests. It also says Search uses the API to detect adversarial behavior and content as a safety layer for its tools.

Each choice or rating comes with a separate confidence estimate of whether the top answer is correct. Google's documentation recommends setting thresholds with labeled examples from the developer's own traffic, weighing the costs of false positives against false negatives.

A fixed list also creates a specific failure case: the model distributes probability among the supplied options even when none fits. Google recommends a confidence threshold, a separate check that the input belongs within the question's scope, or an explicitly described catch-all option.

User-supplied text can argue for its own classification, the documentation warns. Google recommends explicit criteria, adversarial tests, and a human or second check for high-stakes actions such as payouts.

#5145. Bitcoin ETFs Bleed $1.4B in Five Days as Investors Cut Risk

Bitcoin ETFs Bleed $1.4B in Five Days as Investors Cut Risk

U.S. spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds have recorded five consecutive days of net outflows, as investors retreat from risk assets after a turbulent week for markets.

The funds lost some $1.44 billion between November 5 and November 11, including $299.9 million on Wednesday, according to SoSoValue data. That brings net outflows for November so far to $1.88 billion, after $520 million left the funds in October.

The outflows have coincided with a sell-off in AI and technology stocks. On November 5, when the Nasdaq Composite fell 5.5% in a single day, Bitcoin ETFs saw net outflows of about $430 million.

"This wave of outflows is the result of institutional investors actively or passively reducing their risk exposure following a decline in macro risk appetite, which is not yet sufficient to prove that Bitcoin's long-term allocation thesis has reversed," Tim Sun, senior researcher at HashKey, told Decrypt.

The five-day outflows amount to about 2.6% of the funds' $55.1 billion in cumulative net inflows, "far from a full-scale retreat," Sun said. But the funds' total net assets have fallen to $93.7 billion, down about 45% from a peak of $169.5 billion in October 2025, as both outflows and falling prices take their toll.

Bitcoin is trading at $73,358, down 0.87% over the past 24 hours, according to Decrypt data. On Decrypt Predict, users put a 40% chance on Bitcoin trading above $74,000 on Sunday afternoon, and a 94% chance that it will hold above $70,000. Sun estimated that the average holding cost for spot Bitcoin ETFs is around $81,000, meaning some ETF holders are now sitting on unrealized losses, and that some investors may cut their exposure further as they reassess their allocations at year-end.

Analysts say the bigger concern is a shortage of new buyers. In a note shared with Decrypt, Greg Cipolaro, head of research at BitGo, said the post-election bounce brought only $75 million into spot Bitcoin ETFs, while the supply of dollar-pegged stablecoins contracted 1.9% to $286.3 billion, "suggesting little fresh capital is waiting on the sidelines." Onchain analytics firm Glassnode found that total stablecoin supply fell by $14.2 billion in the 30 days through November 9, the fastest contraction since June 2022, Decrypt reported on Wednesday.

The basis trade, in which hedge funds bought spot ETF shares while shorting futures to capture the price gap, has also become less attractive because the basis has fallen below borrowing costs, prompting many funds to exit, Cipolaro said. That means ETF flows may now reflect directional demand for Bitcoin more directly than before, according to Sun.

Corporate buyers have also slowed. The premium of Strategy's stock over the value of its Bitcoin holdings has narrowed, making it less attractive for the company to raise capital to buy more, while MSCI's decision to exclude it from its indexes could cut off passive investment, Sun said.

"The most critical factor to watch right now is the persistent lack of buying demand itself, the impact of which on prices may be more significant than the outflow figures alone," he said.

Cipolaro said Bitcoin had fallen with risk assets but lagged their recoveries. "Unless it begins to participate in rallies and ETF demand strengthens, digital assets could remain exposed to further downside without benefiting from a broader risk asset rebound," he wrote.

Many people forget just how long the 'winter' was for $GLD. It had an initial burst, even becaming the biggest ETF in world for one day in 2011 ,and then the bad times came and and it (saw 40% of investors bail) and took nearly a DECADE to get back to its that aum high again.… pic.twitter.com/Ny9gyyo1Am

— Eric Balchunas (@EricBalchunas) November 11, 2026

Not everyone sees the outflows as a long-term warning sign. Bloomberg ETF analyst Eric Balchunas compared Bitcoin ETFs with gold ETF GLD, which saw around 40% of its investors leave after its 2011 peak and took nearly a decade to recover its assets, but now holds nearly three times that early high. "Assets without cash flows run purely on sentiment and it changes," he wrote.

#5144. AI helped find 66 security flaws, with no fixes planned for some older devices

The research paired commercially available models with expert validation, while earlier Siemens advisories show uneven remediation for affected devices.

Researchers at cybersecurity company Forescout used commercially available AI to find 66 previously unknown flaws in embedded networking software, exposing weaknesses that can disrupt connected devices, according to the company's Nov. 12 announcement.

The team validated 99 vulnerabilities across 10 TCP/IP stacks, the software components that let devices communicate over networks. That total included 33 rediscovered issues. Forescout reported 54 of the new findings to vendors or maintainers because it believed they still affected current versions.

The researchers built a specialized system combining conventional code analysis with Anthropic's Claude models. It examined both open-source code and proprietary binaries, then used dynamic testing to validate potential flaws. Researchers could intervene and guide that testing.

Forescout's research reports roughly 10 hours of automated analysis and 80 hours of dynamic validation. It compares that with about nine months of manual code analysis during its earlier Project Memoria work.

The comparison involves different target sets, rather than a controlled benchmark. It also excludes building that system, refining analysis rules and prompts, and developing the infrastructure needed to emulate embedded software.

Static analysis cost about $935 in model spending; Forescout estimates total model spending at roughly $1,200 to $2,300. Those figures describe the cost of applying an already-built system, rather than the full expense of expert research and setup.

The approach also had limits: it rediscovered 33 of 82 known issues, or 40%, in the previously studied target set and was less effective at finding implementation-specific logic and design flaws.

The study includes flaws affecting building-automation products covered by Siemens' Oct. 13 advisory. The advisory identifies vulnerabilities in specified APOGEE, TALON and Desigo PXC/PXM products. Depending on the issue and product, an unauthenticated remote attacker could read device memory or cause a denial of service.

Siemens lists updates for affected APOGEE PXC and TALON variants, but no planned fix for listed APOGEE MBC/MEC and Desigo products. It recommends issue-specific mitigations, including disabling affected FTP/web services or placing controllers behind network segmentation and properly configured gateways or firewalls. The advisory notes that FTP/web services are disabled by default on the named product lines.

Another finding involves lwIP, an open-source networking stack. An Oct. 26 maintainer fix addresses an MQTT parsing loop under certain buffer configurations. Forescout says the flaw could let a malicious messaging broker freeze a device's network services with a crafted message. Both that fix and Siemens' advisory predate the November research announcement.

Disclosure remains unfinished. Forescout's report lists three unnamed proprietary stacks as under investigation and Ethernut as having provided no response. Forescout also says open-source CVE identifiers are still awaiting assignment.

For operators, Forescout recommends reducing unnecessary service exposure, isolating devices that cannot be patched and applying available fixes. Faster discovery still leaves protection dependent on whether a vendor supports a device and whether a fix reaches it.

#5142. Peter Schiff on the Bitcoin Crash: “Get the Hell Out”

Challenged on why two or three down years would matter for Bitcoin when gold and the Nasdaq have had similar streaks before recoveries, Peter Schiff says the difference is the backdrop.

Challenged on why two or three down years would matter for Bitcoin when gold and the Nasdaq have had similar streaks before recoveries, Peter Schiff says the difference is the backdrop. When a market can’t go up on the best news it has ever had, he argues, that normally indicates that it’s going to go down. He calls Bitcoin “all hype,” with no intrinsic value, income, or dividends, and says much of the supply now sits with ETF investors and Bitcoin treasury companies rather than people who want to use it as money. All of that hot money, he warns, is going to exit, the market will go down faster than it went up, and he expects a massive washout. Even long-term bulls, he says, will have cheaper opportunities after leveraged speculators are cleared out, and he adds that this is not the time to be hodling.

#5140. India's Financial Crimes Agency Searches CoinDCX, Delta Exchange Over Offshore Trades

India's Financial Crimes Agency Searches CoinDCX, Delta Exchange Over Offshore Trades

India's Enforcement Directorate has searched three premises in Bengaluru linked to crypto exchanges CoinDCX and Delta Exchange, alleging the platforms carried out unauthorized cross-border transactions worth hundreds of millions of dollars through crypto derivatives and tokenized assets.

The searches were carried out on November 4 and 5 under India's Foreign Exchange Management Act (FEMA), the agency, India's main financial crimes investigator, said in a statement dated November 6, which it shared on X on Thursday.

ईडी, बेंगलुरु ने क्रिप्टो टोकन डेरिवेटिव एवं वास्तविक विश्व परिसंपत्ति (आरडब्ल्यूए) टोकन से जुड़े बड़े पैमाने पर अनधिकृत सीमा-पार वित्तीय लेन-देन के मामले में फेमा, 1999 के अंतर्गत मेसर्स नेब्लियो टेक्नोलॉजीज प्राइवेट लिमिटेड (“कॉइनडीसीएक्स”) तथा मेसर्स एक्सेलियम टेक्नोलॉजीज… pic.twitter.com/vXwMU5uCTj

The investigation targets Neblio Technologies, which operates CoinDCX, and Excelium Technologies, which runs Delta Exchange. Both platforms offered futures and options on crypto tokens and openly advertised trading in tokenized shares of foreign companies, despite not being authorized to do so, according to the agency.

The Enforcement Directorate alleged that CoinDCX used stablecoins such as USDT to carry out trades for its users on foreign crypto exchanges, including Binance, KuCoin, Bybit and Gate.io, bypassing India's framework for sending money abroad.

When users deposited rupees and placed spot or derivatives orders, CoinDCX converted the funds into USDT and opened matching positions on the foreign exchanges, the agency said. The trades ran through accounts held in the name of a Costa Rica-based group entity, which it described as a shell entity, and were carried out directly from CoinDCX through APIs.

Trades in tokenized shares of companies such as Apple, Google and Tesla were in fact derivatives positions opened on decentralized exchange Hyperliquid, according to the agency.

The Enforcement Directorate alleged that the cross-border transactions in each of these areas ran to hundreds of millions of dollars. It also said CoinDCX holds crypto assets in enterprise accounts on foreign exchanges, and has paid tens of millions of dollars in fees to those platforms in USDT, in breach of FEMA.

The agency said a large share of Delta Exchange's derivatives trading was executed through CoinDCX, and that Delta Exchange had transferred tens of millions of dollars to its overseas group entities, which is under investigation. Their fintech products also appear to contravene India's securities and central banking laws, it said, adding that its investigation is ongoing.

CoinDCX said in a tweet on November 6 that Enforcement Directorate officials had visited its Bengaluru office "as part of a routine enquiry" on November 4, and that it had fully cooperated. The exchange said it operates "in strict compliance with all applicable Indian laws, including FEMA," and adheres to its KYC, anti-money laundering and reporting obligations as an entity registered with India's Financial Intelligence Unit. Trading, deposits and withdrawals on the platform remain fully operational, it added.

Officials from the Enforcement Directorate visited our Bengaluru office as part of a routine enquiry on 4th November. We fully cooperated with the authorities and provided all necessary information and assistance.

As an entity registered with FIU-IND, we regularly…

Delta Exchange said in its own tweet on November 6 that it had cooperated fully with officials, and that its business "does not involve any cross-border transfer of VDAs in any form or manner," using India's term for crypto assets. It said it runs a dedicated rupee-settled exchange for Indian users only, has been registered with India's Financial Intelligence Unit since 2023, and that trading, deposits and withdrawals were functioning normally.

The investigation adds to a difficult stretch for CoinDCX, one of India's largest crypto exchanges. Last month, it confirmed a $16 million loss from retired wallets, days after co-founder and CEO Sumit Gupta called reports of a breach a "false alarm." That followed a roughly $44 million exploit in July 2025.

Coinbase, which has invested in CoinDCX since 2020, won approval from India's competition regulator to increase its minority stake in the exchange's parent company.

#5139. Bitcoin falls below $70,000 as $870 million in bullish bets are liquidated

Traders betting on higher Bitcoin prices faced nearly $870 million in forced closures over 24 hours as rate fears and NVIDIA uncertainty weighed on markets.

Bitcoin traded below $70,000 on Nov. 13 as heavy liquidations hit traders betting on higher prices, extending the crypto selloff against a backdrop of rate pressure and uncertainty at NVIDIA.

CoinGlass recorded $869.89 million in Bitcoin long liquidations over the last 24 hours, compared with $31 million in short liquidations. Over four hours, it recorded $559 million in long liquidations and $16 million in shorts.

By late Friday morning, UTC, Bitcoin was down to $69,224, with a rolling 24-hour low of $67,921.

The liquidation figures measure forced closures of Bitcoin positions tracked by the provider, a different measure from the change in Bitcoin's market capitalization.

Long positions benefit when prices rise. Their concentration in the liquidation figures is consistent with forced exits amplifying the decline as bullish bets come under pressure. The initial trigger remains unresolved, and position closures can involve different markets rather than a spot sale in every case.

The liquidation imbalance points to pressure on bullish positioning alongside the broader concerns about technology stocks and interest rates. Forced exits offer a plausible mechanism for amplifying the decline.

Bitcoin traded below $70,000 as long positions dominated forced closures. Select a rolling window to compare the imbalance.

Rolling four hours to about 10:52 UTC, Nov. 13: $559.87M in long liquidations and $16.22M in shorts. This window is inside the 24-hour window.

The previous US stock session ended lower, though the broad indexes' closing losses were modest. The S&P 500 fell 0.35% to 7,592 on Nov. 12, according to LSEG data carried by the Financial Times. eOption's closing recap put the Nasdaq's decline at 0.39% and the Russell 2000's at 1.66%.

The broker also described a stronger dollar and rising Treasury yields as investors anticipated tighter Federal Reserve policy. That places Bitcoin's weakness within a wider retreat from risk.

NVIDIA's leadership change remained part of that backdrop. The company announced on Nov. 12 that Jensen Huang had resigned as president, CEO and director, with Colette Kress becoming interim CEO while retaining her CFO role. Its SEC filing dates the board's acceptance of his resignation to Nov. 11, effective immediately.

Rate concerns also predated Friday's crypto decline. He said a core CPI reading around 0.3% or higher could warrant considering 50 basis points. That was his individual policy view, rather than an FOMC commitment.

Microsoft was also still working to restore affected services on Friday. Its 09:57 UTC update, reproduced in Penn's Microsoft incident feed, said recovery remained variable. There is no direct link between the outage and Bitcoin's decline, however, the risk-off mentality that comes with global technological uncertainty is clearly affecting the broader market.

#5133. The catch in crypto’s Anthropic IPO rush: lock up your money and you could still miss out

Anthropic IPO token buyers face uncertain share allocations, locked funds and fees of up to 12% ahead of its Nasdaq debut.

Crypto investors seeking Anthropic IPO tokens can commit money and still get a refund instead of an allocation.

Kraken, Bitget, and BigONE disclose subscription terms that make delivery dependent on available shares, while successful fills carry additional costs.

The issue became more immediate on Nov. 12 when BigONE opened indications of interest and warned users they could receive a full allocation, a partial allocation, or none. Kraken's Anthropic IPO access terms carry the same three possible outcomes, while Bitget says its commitment caps do not represent the shares it has secured.

Those warnings come ahead of Anthropic's expected Nov. 18 US-time pricing and Nov. 19 Nasdaq debut. Bloomberg News reported on Nov. 11, citing people familiar with the matter, that orders already exceeded the available shares multiple times.

The venues' terms make clear that money can become unavailable while an allocation is determined, and receiving tokens can mean paying above the offering price without acquiring direct shareholder rights.

Allocation decides what investors receive. Select a venue to see how money control, charges and expected delivery differ.

Kraken reserves funds. You can cancel free before the communicated cutoff; after it, the request becomes binding.

Bitget public commitments cannot be canceled. Funds stay locked; token delivery is expected before the regular U.S. session on IPO day.

The published VIP cutoff has passed. Commitments cannot be canceled; delivery is expected after the IPO and unallocated funds are expected back within hours of allocation confirmation, with delays possible.

BigONE uses cumulative subscription tiers and charges only on allocated shares. Full, partial or zero allocation remains possible.

Submitting money secures a place in the allocation process; the received tokens and refund depend on the result.

Terms captured Nov. 12, 2026. Anthropic allocation and final price remain unresolved. Delivery and refunds may be delayed or changed. The graphic compares subscription mechanics, not investment returns or legal equivalence of tokens.

Qualitative branching from the venues' full/partial/zero-allocation terms. No probabilities or share quantities inferred. Venue states use published cancellation, fee, refund and expected delivery terms. Geometry is a process diagram, not a duration or value scale.

In a June 12 notice, Bybit said xStocks could not deliver the underlying SpaceX assets, leaving the exchange with no allocation.

Anthropic's Nov. 10 preliminary prospectus offers 825 million Class A shares at an estimated $70 to $78 each. About 801.4 million are company shares and 23.6 million are being sold by existing holders, whose sale proceeds do not go to Anthropic.

Bloomberg News reported that the public offering could total up to $64 billion. A separate $26 billion Class N private placement would bring the combined amount to as much as $90 billion.

The published charges apply only to the amount allocated:

Kraken reserves money when a user submits interest and charges it only if an allocation is received, and reserved funds remain unavailable until allocation or release. Its cancellation right ends at a communicated cutoff, after which the submission becomes binding.

Supported stablecoin submissions are converted to dollars, and refunds arrive in dollars.

Bitget's restriction says users cannot cancel submitted commitments or trade, transfer, or withdraw locked funds. Its fee is charged by the partner fund, broker, or placement channel and is included in the commitment.

The venue expects remaining funds to be refunded within hours of allocation confirmation, while warning that arrival can be delayed and processing extended if issuance or allocation arrangements fail.

Bitget's published VIP window was scheduled to end Nov. 11, while its public window is scheduled to end at 08:00 UTC on the day before the IPO.

The higher VIP fee does not remove allocation uncertainty, and the expected three-to-five-working-day delivery means those tokens may arrive after the stock's first trading day.

BigONE's window runs through Nov. 18 at 12:00 UTC. Its notice promises refunds if the platform receives no allocation, plus 6% annualized compensation for the time each subscription's funds were committed.

Payments below 1 USDT per subscription are excluded, and the payment schedule will be announced separately.

Using the preliminary range's $78 upper endpoint as an illustration, a 5% surcharge yields an acquisition cost of $81.90 per unit, while a 12% surcharge yields $87.36.

Assuming a token tracks one share without price divergence, the underlying price would need to rise 5% or 12%, respectively, from $78 just to match those acquisition costs, before any later trading charges.

Kraken says unallocated funds return on listing day, BigONE expects ANTx delivery on Nov. 19, and Bitget targets rANT trading about an hour after Anthropic's Nasdaq IPO Cross, while its VIP delivery expectation extends several working days beyond the IPO.
