#5128. Bitcoin’s futures pile has a problem: less cash to fuel a rebound

Bitcoin’s futures exposure meets weaker Treasury demand and thinning spot buying, leaving a rebound dependent on fresh demand.

DWF Labs' Martin Lee noted that Bitcoin futures open interest stood at 704,000 BTC this week, the highest since Sept. 24, while spot market liquidity continued to thin.

Meanwhile, the Treasury's $25 billion 30-year bond auction on Nov. 12 drew a 2.36 bid-to-cover ratio, down from 2.54 in October, and cleared at 5.441% against a 5.437% when-issued yield, a 0.4-basis-point tail.

The 10-year sale two days earlier showed a sharper slide, with bid-to-cover at 2.46 against 2.77 in October.

The 10-year cleared at 5.02%, with indirect bidders taking about 61.2% of competitive awards versus 80.3% in October, and dealers taking 9.8% versus 2.5%. The 3-year sale on Nov. 9 drew 2.53 against 2.62.

The 30-year result sits within the range of recent sales, since August's new issue drew 2.39. The Treasury said on Nov. 4 that demand for short-term bills remained strong, so the auctions show weaker appetite for duration, and a retreat from US debt would be a larger claim.

Treasury expects $618 billion of privately held net borrowing in October through December and $800 billion in January through March. The $125 billion in 3-, 10-, and 30-year refunding sales raises about $28 billion in new cash.

Two-year yields near 4.835% and 10-year yields near 5.068% on Nov. 12 make cash and government debt more competitive with Bitcoin.

The 10-year fell 0.31, compared with 0.18 for the 30-year and 0.09 for the 3-year. October’s 10- and 30-year sales reopened existing securities, while November’s sales were new issues.

Source: TreasuryDirect - Oct. 6, 3-year note · TreasuryDirect - Oct. 7, 10-year note reopening · TreasuryDirect - Oct. 8, 30-year bond reopening · TreasuryDirect - Nov. 9, 3-year note · TreasuryDirect - Nov. 10, 10-year note · TreasuryDirect - Nov. 12, 30-year bond

Lee describes dollar-debasement demand and broader risk-on flows as two supports for Bitcoin in past rallies. Both helped in the 2020 to 2022 cycle, and in August the debasement narrative let Bitcoin hold up while AI stocks corrected.

“The limitation is that debasement alone rarely sustains a rally when the broader market is risk-off.”

He sees little chance of liquidity rotating into crypto until uncertainty from the midterms and a near-certain December hike clears. Adoption continues, with Airwallex launching its own chain and the NYSE advancing a tokenized stock platform.

Lee said Bitcoin options open interest recovered to 447,000 BTC from a dip between Nov. 6 and Nov. 9, though it remains 5% to 10% below October. Bitcoin's futures open interest is about 12% below its 2026 high, while Ethereum's is down about 30% since May and Solana's about 23% since June.

At an illustrative $72,000, 704,000 BTC equals about $51 billion of gross notional. Open interest counts outstanding contracts with a buyer and a seller on each, so it mixes long bets, short bets and hedges, and it describes positioning, a measure separate from fresh capital.

Glassnode noted on Nov. 11 that open interest had rebuilt toward September levels while Bitcoin sat about 15% below its September high, a pattern consistent with traders adding shorts during the decline.

Futures can amplify a rebound through short covering or a decline through forced closures, and both routes depend on spot liquidity.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Glassnode’s Nov. 11 analysis found resting bids within 5% of price outweighing asks on Binance and Coinbase since Nov. 5, with the largest concentration between $68,000 and $72,500.

Wallets holding 100 to 1,000 BTC and 1,000 to 10,000 BTC are accumulating, and other cohorts are distributing. A resting bid shows willingness to buy, and whether purchases follow depends on the bid surviving, since bids can be pulled or overwhelmed.

Bitcoin fell below $72,000 on Nov. 12 with an intraday low near $71,255, entering the top of that band.

About 35% of short-term holder supply is in profit, and Bitcoin trades below the $75,600 short-term holder cost basis. CryptoQuant reported profitable supply falling from more than 15 million BTC to about 11 million.

The 30-day average Coinbase premium is negative, with the deepest discount since February. Stablecoin supply contracted $14 billion in 30 days, the fastest since June 2022.

Farside Investors showed about $2 billion of US spot ETF outflows from Nov. 2 through Nov. 11, including $300 million on Nov. 11.

Daily net flows across the US spot Bitcoin ETFs , showing seven of eight sessions with net outflows.

Source: Farside Investors — Bitcoin ETF Flow, daily fund totals

“We've seen outflows accelerate at the bottoms and inflows accelerate at the tops.”

DWF Labs' Oct. 22 study of 694 trading days found ETF flows correlated 0.54 with Bitcoin's prior five-day return and 0.03 with its next five-day return. Flows turned negative on the low day in seven of eight qualifying selloffs and positive on the peak day in seven of eight tops.

About 20% of ETF buying was historically tied to CME short hedges.

Glassnode puts the True Market Mean near $77,500, a modeled six-month liquidation concentration at $59,500 to $62,600, a June low near $58,500 and a short-liquidation cluster near $87,500.

A recovery would have stronger backing if buyers absorb selling in the $68,000 to $72,500 band, Bitcoin reclaims $75,600 and $77,500, the Coinbase premium turns positive and stablecoin supply resumes growing. That combination could make futures positioning fuel for a rebound.

If bids give way while holder profitability erodes and stablecoin supply keeps contracting, leveraged positions could amplify a decline. Glassnode’s modeled $59,500 to $62,600 zone marks a possible area of liquidation pressure, not a price target.

#5125. Morning Minute: Tokenized Stock Venues Are Launching Without Big Tech’s Approval

Morning Minute: Tokenized Stock Venues Are Launching Without Big Tech’s Approval

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

🏛️ OKX’s Tokenized Stock Venue Launches Without Big Tech

In September, after the CLARITY Act died in the Senate, the SEC created an innovation exemption letting venues trade tokenized versions of US stocks without waiting for new legislation. It came with one condition. A company that doesn’t want a third party minting tokens of its shares can object in writing within 30 days, and that’s the end of it.

Companies have been objecting ever since. The count at the OKXICE venue went from 22 in October to 50, then 56, and as of its November 5 notice, 67. Every member of the Magnificent Seven is on that list.

OKXICE is launching anyway, on November 23. The venue will list more than 70 assets, though 59 of them are Direxion ETFs. The individual stocks that made it through are Coinbase, Circle, Robinhood, Strategy, BitGo, Securitize and Better.com. Strategy is also tokenizing its preferred shares.

Securitize handles the tokenization, each token is backed one-for-one by a real share and carries dividends and voting rights, and trading runs through Uniswap v4 pools on X Layer, OKX’s Ethereum layer-2.

So a venture between a global exchange and the parent of the New York Stock Exchange built a tokenized equities market, and what it opens with is seven crypto companies and a series of leveraged ETFs.

Notably, this news comes just a day after the NYSE picked Avalanche to handle clearing and settlement for its own 24/7 tokenized securities platform, with a fourth-quarter target for launch after about a year of testing. The NYSE has signed agreements with Broadridge, Computershare, Equiniti and Superstate to support issuer-sponsored tokenized securities and transfer-agent functions. When the company’s own share registry issues the token, there is nothing to veto.

DTCC is doing a version of this too, with a tokenization service that went live October 28 on the Canton Network carrying 20 participants including Bank of America, Citi, JPMorgan and Citadel Securities. Morgan Stanley has already run equities and Treasuries through it.

Progress is being made, slowly but surely. It just gets built slowly, through transfer agents and clearing houses, by the incumbents. Now we wait to see how the market reacts to these first launches…

#5120. DOJ’s Operation TorNado Arrests 354 and Seizes $234 Million in Cash and Crypto

The Justice Department and Europol said Thursday that Operation TorNado has produced 354 arrests and the seizure of more than $234 million in cash and virtual currencies, in what the FBI called the largest takedown yet of dark web drug vendors and buyers.

The announcement came from the department’s Joint Criminal Opioid and Darknet Enforcement team, or JCODE, an FBI-led group created in 2018. Officials said Operation TorNado is the sixth, and most extensive, internationally coordinated action since then, and that it builds on last year’s Operation RapTor. The sweep ran from May 2025 to October 2026.

Europol, which coordinated work with agencies in 14 countries, published a parallel statement the same day. The operation targeted vendors and buyers who had been active on five dark web markets already seized by law enforcement: Archetyp, Bohemia, Nemesis, Kingdom and Tor2Door. Officials also identified accounts on DrugHub and on forums, single-vendor shops and encrypted messaging services.

The seizures included about 5.7 metric tons of illicit drugs worldwide, among them fentanyl and other opioids, methamphetamine, heroin, cocaine, ecstasy and MDMA, plus 38 pill presses and 209 firearms. Neither the Justice Department nor Europol split the $234 million between cash and cryptocurrency, and neither named bitcoin or any other coin in the official totals.

Europol put the European share at more than €60 million in cash and cryptocurrencies, alongside 950 kilograms of drugs and 57 firearms, and the U.S. share at more than €150 million, or $166 million, in cash and cryptocurrencies, plus more than 4.8 metric tons of drugs and 152 firearms. Those regional money figures still combine cash and crypto. Europol said its forensic and blockchain analysis teams traced illicit transactions as part of the work.

The closest official window into how the vendors were paid comes from the cases the Justice Department highlighted and from court papers posted with the announcement.

Alexander Green, 32, of Jacksonville, North Carolina, was arrested in June and charged in the Southern District of Ohio with conspiracy to distribute controlled substances, including fentanyl. The department said he allegedly operated a vendor called STARLABS on DrugHub, which it described as a Monero-only dark web market, and that the group has been linked to two overdose deaths. A criminal complaint says undercover purchases from STARLABS were made “in exchange for cryptocurrency.” If convicted, he faces up to 20 years in prison.

In a separate Florida arrest, brothers Stanislav Chernyshov, 30, and Vladislav Chernyshov, 35, are charged in the Eastern District of Virginia with conspiracy to distribute Schedule I synthetic opioids. Prosecutors say they allegedly ran the vendor “BarbaraWhite,” selling counterfeit oxycodone pills laced with nitazenes and shipping more than 1,000 parcels across 49 states. A search-warrant affidavit describes bitcoin transfers tied to that account and the Nemesis market. They each face up to 20 years if convicted.

Operation TorNado led to 132 arrests in the United States, 59 in the United Kingdom, 45 in Hungary and 41 in Germany, along with arrests in France, New Zealand, the Netherlands, Slovenia, Austria, Ireland, Czechia, Australia and Finland. A number of investigations are still ongoing to identify the people behind remaining dark web accounts, the department said.

FBI Director Kash Patel called Operation TorNado “the largest interagency takedown of Dark Web drug vendors and buyers to date.” IRS Criminal Investigation Chief Jarod Koopman, whose agency was part of the JCODE group, put the financial work at the center: “Criminal networks may operate across borders and in the shadows of the Dark Web, but they cannot operate without money.”

Federal prosecutions are underway in more than 20 districts. An indictment or complaint only alleges that crimes were committed, and the defendants are presumed innocent until proven guilty.

What the announcement does not settle is how much of the $234 million was cryptocurrency, and how much of that was bitcoin. Officials have not said. The record on Operation TorNado shows a combined cash-and-crypto seizure, a Monero-only market in at least one featured case, and bitcoin transfers in court papers, without a breakdown of the total.

#5115. DTCC to Bring Tokenized Stocks and Treasuries to Solana in 2027

DTCC to Bring Tokenized Stocks and Treasuries to Solana in 2027

The Depository Trust & Clearing Corporation (DTCC), the backbone of U.S. securities settlement, plans to bring its tokenized securities to Solana, it announced on Thursday.

The firm will connect the DTCC Tokenization Service (DTS) to the Solana network, allowing eligible assets held by its Depository Trust Company (DTC) subsidiary to be converted into tokens on Solana. The integration is planned for the second half of 2027.

BREAKING: @The_DTCC to tokenize custodied assets on Solana. announced plans to connect the DTCC Tokenization Service to Solana, enabling the tokenization of eligible DTC-custodied assets on the network.

The integration is planned for the second half of 2027, further advancing… pic.twitter.com/gXszWFgUOX

"DTCC is bringing regulated, DTC-tokenized securities to another major blockchain ecosystem, giving market participants greater choice in where they access and utilize tokenized assets," DTCC CEO Frank La Salla said.

DTS went live on October 28 on the Canton Network and DTCC's own private blockchain, built on the Besu framework, with more networks due to be added in 2027, according to DTCC's launch announcement. The service creates tokenized "digital twins" of securities held at DTC, which custodies more than $114 trillion in assets. Solana joins Stellar and Circle's Arc blockchain, which are expected to be added in 2027, according to a DTCC fact sheet.

Under a no-action letter from the SEC, the service is limited to a defined set of highly liquid assets, including Russell 1000 stocks, ETFs tracking major indexes, and U.S. Treasuries. Morgan Stanley said last week it had tokenized U.S. stocks and Treasuries through the service.

Tokens issued on Solana "will retain the same investor protections, ownership rights and regulatory safeguards that apply to traditionally held securities," said Brian Steele, president of DTCC Clearing & Securities Services.

Solana has been actively courting traditional finance in recent months, with Solana Foundation head of institutional Nick Ducoff arguing that, "Institutions choose Solana because it already runs real financial activity at scale." He added that institutions are "already issuing, settling and deploying capital" on the network, in a tweet shared by Solana's official account.

Last month, the Solana Foundation unveiled an open standard for institutional settlement developed with input from J.P. Morgan, and the network completed its Alpenglow upgrade, cutting the time it takes for transactions to become final to roughly 150 milliseconds.

Solana is also lining up more venues for tokenized stocks. Trading platform Formation has notified the SEC of plans to trade tokenized U.S. stocks on Solana, starting with Galaxy and Forward Industries.

#5112. Jane Street Lifts Its Strategy (MSTR) Stake 54% to 4.1 Million Shares

A 13F filed Thursday shows Jane Street held 4,119,097 Strategy shares as of Sept. 30, up 53.8%.

Jane Street Group raised its Strategy (MSTR) stake 53.8% in the third quarter, to 4,119,097 shares, a Form 13F filed Thursday shows, a position dated to Sept. 30 that does not capture any trades the firm may have made since then.

Those caveats matter as much as the increase. Jane Street is a trading firm, and one of the largest market makers in stocks, ETFs, and options, and the same filing lists call options covering 13,573,000 Strategy shares and put options covering 13,991,800 shares, each more than three times the size of the common stake. Some of that stock is likely a hedge for the options and market-making book rather than a straight bet on the company. The 13F does not say how much.

The shares were valued at $630.6 million in the filing, using the Sept. 30 price of $153.09. Strategy has fallen about 25% since then. At Thursday’s close of $114.41, the same 4,119,097 shares were worth about $471 million.

The Class A position is up 1,441,475 shares from the 2,677,622 Jane Street reported at June 30, when the same holding was valued at $232.8 million. The firm filed about four days ahead of the Nov. 16 deadline; the SEC accepted the report at 4:04 p.m. Eastern on Thursday.

The options lines were already large in the second quarter and grew again. Calls covering 13,573,000 shares were valued at $2.08 billion in the new filing, and puts covering 13,991,800 shares, listed in two rows, were valued at $2.14 billion. Those figures use the same Sept. 30 share price as the common-stock line; a 13F reports the underlying shares the options cover, not the cash premium paid for the contracts.

The report is a holdings filing that folds in positions across seven other included managers, among them Jane Street Capital, Jane Street Options and Jane Street Global Trading, so the Strategy lines can include inventory across the group rather than a single proprietary bet. Authorized Signatory Jeremy Kahn signed the report in New York on Nov. 12. The table lists 14,764 entries valued at $1.19 trillion.

Jane Street’s 53.8% increase was the sharpest percentage increase among the large Strategy stakes in Thursday’s early 13F wave. Capital International Investors, a Capital Group unit, raised its stake 4.5% to 36,467,109 shares, worth about $4.17 billion at Thursday’s close. Invesco added 10.9%, to 10,362,698 shares, worth about $1.19 billion. Renaissance Technologies cut its position 41%, to 1,515,560 shares, worth about $173 million, and left the spot bitcoin ETF holdings it had reported at midyear, including BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust.

JPMorgan Chase, in a filing already reported, lifted its Strategy stake 36% to 848,932 shares and held 11.4 million IBIT shares, worth about $460 million at Thursday’s close. Bank of New York Mellon, in another early 13F, raised its stake in Strive (ASST). Those share counts, like Jane Street’s, are as of Sept. 30.

Still to file by the Nov. 16 deadline are the main quarterly 13Fs from Goldman Sachs, Morgan Stanley, Millennium and Mubadala. Institutional managers that exercise investment discretion over $100 million or more in 13F securities have 45 days after quarter-end to disclose those holdings. That window ran to Nov. 14, a Saturday, so the deadline is Monday.

The Sept. 30 snapshot also precedes this week’s decision by MSCI to remove Strategy from its USA index, a step that will force index funds to sell by Nov. 30, and it precedes the stock’s slide since quarter-end. Monday’s remaining filings will show where other large holders stood on the same date, not where they stand now.

#5106. Tether Gold issuer follows USDT to complete Big Four audit

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Tether said KPMG U.S. inspected every gold bar backing XAUT, following the USDT issuer's first full financial audit in August.

Tether said Thursday that KPMG U.S. completed an independent audit of the financial statements of TG Commodities, the issuer of its gold-backed XAUT token.

A spokesperson for the Big Four firm subsequently confirmed to The Block that it issued an unqualified, or "clean," opinion on TG Commodities's financial statements in accordance with AICPA standards for the year ending Dec. 31, 2025.

In a blog post, Tether said KPMG physically inspected and counted every individual gold bar backing XAUT Tether Gold (XAUT), checking the identifying information on each one. As of Dec. 31, 2025, TG Commodities held 520,089.350 fine troy ounces of gold worth $2.246 billion, against 520,089.300 XAUT in circulation, according to the firm.

The gold belongs to token holders and is stored in Switzerland. Each XAUT represents "an undivided ownership interest in gold on specified bars," Tether said.

KPMG had already audited the 2025 accounts of USDT Tether (USDT) stablecoin issuer Tether International. Tether announced that audit in August, and a spokesperson for the accounting firm confirmed to The Block that it had issued an unqualified opinion. Before then, Tether's regular reserve reporting consisted of attestations covering its holdings and liabilities on a given date.

"Our strategy was deliberate: start with the largest and most complex businesses, USDT and XAUT, and set the standard for the industry," Tether Chief Financial Officer Simon McWilliams said on Thursday.

The company is working toward audits of the rest of its businesses, and the audited figures matched Tether's quarterly reserve reports, he said.

#5105. SoFi Brings Its Bank-Issued Stablecoin to Polygon’s Payments Stack

SoFi Brings Its Bank-Issued Stablecoin to Polygon’s Payments Stack

SoFi is widening the reach of its digital dollar, bringing SoFiUSD, the first stablecoin issued by a U.S. national bank, onto Polygon’s payments infrastructure as banks and fintechs push stablecoins deeper into everyday money movement.

Polygon Labs said Thursday that SoFiUSD is now live on Polygon Open Money Stack, a payments layer that bundles fiat on- and off-ramps, embedded wallets and cross-chain settlement into a single integration.

The move adds Polygon alongside Ethereum and Solana, the networks SoFiUSD already runs on, and lets SoFi’s more than 17 million members buy, sell, hold, and convert the token on Polygon directly in the SoFi app, without opening a separate crypto wallet or manually bridging assets between chains.

“SoFi is well positioned to bring stablecoins into mainstream finance because it combines the regulatory foundation of a national bank with the reach of a major consumer and enterprise technology platform,” said Marc Boiron, CEO of Polygon Labs.

The expansion lands as stablecoins move beyond crypto trading into the plumbing used to settle card payments, pay businesses, and send money across borders.

On Polygon Open Money Stack, SoFiUSD joins USDC, USDT and PayPal’s PYUSD, and can connect to the wallets, banking rails, and local currencies businesses need for payouts, settlement and cross-border transfers, opening up uses well beyond consumer trading.

The token is redeemable one-to-one for U.S. dollars and issued by SoFi Bank, which is regulated by the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency.

SoFi launched SoFiUSD late last year across Ethereum and Solana as the first stablecoin from a U.S. national bank, and it already settles the company’s entire card program over Mastercard’s network. The firm has been building out a broader digital-money suite, recently launching SoFi Earn, a tokenized deposit that pays 4.40% yield and converts one-to-one into SoFiUSD.

For Polygon, the addition deepens a payments business that has become one of the network’s calling cards.

Polygon recently extended Open Money Stack to Solana, and the chain has processed more than $3 trillion in lifetime stablecoin transfer volume, according to the company. Visa and Mastercard support stablecoin settlement on Polygon, while Stripe uses it to route USDC payments for merchants in more than 150 countries.

SoFiUSD is a payment stablecoin, not a bank deposit, and is not FDIC- or SIPC-insured, bank-guaranteed or legal tender.

#5104. Bitcoin Price Trades Near $71,200 After a Fresh Session Low as the 10-Year Yield Holds Above 5%

Bitcoin price traded near $71,200 on Thursday evening, after slipping to a fresh session low near $71,000, according to Bitcoin Magazine Pro data. U.S. stocks closed modestly lower and the 10-year Treasury yield held above 5% after Nvidia said CEO Jensen Huang had resigned.

The decline started in the morning. Bitcoin price fell below $72,000 before the opening bell and dropped to about $71,150 shortly after 10 a.m. Eastern. A bounce later faded, and the bitcoin price printed a new session low near $71,000 around 6:23 p.m., its weakest level since Aug. 20. It now stands roughly $30,400 below where it closed one year ago.

The Nasdaq Composite slipped 0.39% to 25,366.09, the S&P 500 lost 0.35% to 7,592.40 and the Dow Jones Industrial Average fell 0.36% to 52,297.09, Reuters reported. Nvidia shares finished 4.3% lower at $166.19, off the day’s lows, after the company said Huang had resigned as president, chief executive and a board member, effective immediately, and named Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress interim CEO.

Bitcoin-linked stocks had a harder session. Strategy (MSTR), which MSCI is removing from its USA Index at the Nov. 30 close, finished at $114.41, down 6.5%. Shares of BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT), which had bled $2 billion over nine straight days of outflows through Wednesday, closed at $40.36, down 4.5%.

The bond market did much of the afternoon work. The 10-year Treasury yield rose about 12 basis points to 5.105% and reached 5.12%, its highest since Oct. 21, after President Donald Trump said the United States does not need a deal with Iran, Reuters reported. The two-year yield, which tracks Federal Reserve expectations most closely, climbed about 14 basis points to 4.867%. Brent crude settled at $95.82 a barrel, up 3.3%, after attacks on Saudi energy infrastructure escalated.

Higher yields reflect a market that still expects the Fed to tighten again next month. Fed funds futures are pricing in 29 basis points of hikes in December, a quarter-point move as the base case with only slim odds of a half-point step, Reuters reported. Rising rates raise the cost of holding an asset that pays no interest, and that has weighed on the bitcoin price all week.

The Treasury added a fiscal reminder after the close. The government ran a $404 billion deficit in October, the first month of fiscal 2027, the Treasury Department said, up 42% from $284 billion a year earlier, as receipts fell to $369 billion and outlays rose to $773 billion. Some benefit payments that would normally have been made on Nov. 1, a Sunday, were shifted into October.

The evening also brought a cluster of crypto headlines. The Justice Department said it has court authority to seize or restrain more than $1.2 billion of cryptocurrency stolen from Binance in October’s North Korea-linked heist, part of a freeze that officials said halted about half the breach. Binance, in a separate announcement, said it has fully covered the $6.34 billion in user losses with company funds. Jane Street’s quarterly 13F showed it had raised its Strategy stake 54% as of Sept. 30, though large options positions point to hedging rather than a straight bet. After the close, Gemini reported third-quarter revenue of $40.9 million, down 19%, as platform trading volume fell 73%; its net loss narrowed 63% to $58.6 million.

Those announcements did not lift the bitcoin price off the session lows. Friday’s Producer Price Index is the next inflation test, and Nvidia is still scheduled to report quarterly results on Nov. 17 under its interim leadership.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $71,200.

#5101. Unocoin ‘login suspension’ continues as exchange flags outgoing BitGo ETH transactions

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Unocoin says it lacks full visibility into its frozen BitGo wallets and estimates a server rebuild will take up to five working days.

Indian cryptocurrency exchange Unocoin has identified "outgoing transactions" from an ETH address in its BitGo wallet infrastructure, adding a new development to a security incident that has left customers unable to access their accounts since Nov. 9.

In its latest statement, the exchange said it discovered the activity on Thursday and immediately escalated the matter to BitGo, which has opened an investigation.

BitGo said its systems were not breached in connection with the transaction. "Our review found that the transaction was signed using the user and backup keys held by Unocoin and broadcast externally on-chain, without BitGo's participation," a BitGo spokesperson told The Block.

The custodian also said it froze Unocoin's enterprise accounts days before the transaction and that the freeze "functioned as intended," adding that the enterprise freeze "restricts activity through BitGo, but does not prevent transactions independently signed and broadcast using the user and backup keys."

Unocoin says more than 90% of customer cryptocurrency is held in cold storage and that no funds were lost in the incident. However, when asked by a user on X whether those could be recovered, the exchange replied that it would not "give you an assurance based on assumptions" until transaction-level details are verified.

The exchange also said it cannot log in to the frozen accounts and that BitGo's use of change addresses limits what it can check on-chain.

"We currently do not have complete visibility into all potentially unintended transactions across our BitGo wallets," it wrote in the incident report.

Unocoin said rebuilding its servers from the ground up would take an estimated four to five working days. The investigation into the breach, potential data exposure, and status of user holdings remains ongoing.

Meanwhile, Onchain Lens reported that Arkham shows $0 across 81,000 tracked Unocoin addresses and that the exchange's website returned a 504 error. The exchange has not confirmed that its tracked wallets were drained.

The article has been updated with BitGo's comments.

#5099. Jensen Huang Resigns as Nvidia CEO, With CFO Colette Kress Named Interim Chief

Jensen Huang Resigns as Nvidia CEO, With CFO Colette Kress Named Interim Chief

Jensen Huang has resigned as CEO of Nvidia, ending a 33-year tenure at the helm of the chipmaker he co-founded, after reports of affairs with a suspected Chinese spy and a senior company executive.

Huang has stepped down as president, CEO and a member of the board, effective immediately, Nvidia said on Thursday. Colette Kress, Nvidia's chief financial officer since 2013, will serve as interim CEO while remaining CFO, as the board searches for a permanent successor from both inside and outside the company. Nvidia did not give a reason for Huang's resignation, but thanked him for his "immeasurable contributions to the company and the industry."

NVIDIA announces CEO transition. Read the release: https://t.co/MTTUvFHCJA

— NVIDIA Newsroom (@nvidianewsroom) November 12, 2026

The board also named Harvey Jones, a former CEO and executive chairman of chip design software company Synopsys, as its first-ever chair.

"The Board has full confidence in Colette and the leadership team, and we expect the company's operations, customer commitments and product roadmap to continue without interruption," Jones said.

Kress said she was "committed to building on the extraordinary foundation Jensen created over the last 33 years." Nvidia will report its third-quarter results as scheduled on Tuesday, the company said.

Nvidia shares were down 7.7% at $160.28 shortly after the market opened on Thursday, having fallen as low as $148.80, according to Yahoo! Finance data.

Huang's departure comes four days after he acknowledged in a letter to staff that he had been unfaithful to his wife, prompting Nvidia's board to open an independent investigation. He had told the board that he had been intimate with Alice Huang, an executive at Singapore-based Nvidia customer Megaspeed, The New York Times reported.

The Times had reported in October that U.S. officials had concluded Alice Huang was a Chinese intelligence asset, while the Financial Times later reported that a U.S. government memo said the two had been romantically involved. China has denied the allegations.

On Wednesday, the Times reported that Huang had also had a relationship with Kimberly Powell, Nvidia's vice president of health care, who reports to him. The Wall Street Journal later reported that the board was preparing to oust him, adding to the pressure on the CEO.

The scandal has wiped hundreds of billions of dollars off Nvidia's market value, with the company losing about $430 billion in two days earlier this week. Nvidia and Huang also face a proposed shareholder class action, and lawmakers had threatened to subpoena him.

Huang ran Nvidia with about 60 executives reporting directly to him and has said he did not believe in succession planning, leaving the company without an obvious successor.

Prior to the announcement of Huang's resignation, one person inside the company had told the Financial Times that naming Kress as interim CEO would be the simplest option. Other potential candidates cited by the paper's sources include Ian Buck, who runs Nvidia's data center business, and Arm CEO Rene Haas, a former Nvidia executive.

Huang remains Nvidia's largest shareholder, with a stake of about 3.6%, according to the Journal.

#5098. Meet the Athlete-Turned-Founder Rethinking the Sports Bra

Sara Falkson turned her own experience with ill-fitting sports bras into a brand focused on better support.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

When an athlete suits up for competition, every piece of equipment matters. A shin guard that doesn’t fit or football pads that aren’t secured properly can affect performance. But one essential piece of gear is often overlooked: the sports bra.

When shopping at top athletic retailers, female athletes are usually presented with around three options: small, medium, and large. The cup size grows with the band size, and there is little to no room for customization.

Anyone with breasts can relate to struggling to find a comfortable bra, but it's an unfortunate reality many female athletes have come to accept. What may seem like a trivial aspect of an athlete’s uniform holds a much greater significance. According to a 2021 survey of 70 elite athletes conducted by the English Institute of Sport, 75 percent of athletes have never been fitted for a sports bra, and 26 percent reported that breast pain affects their athletic performance. This speaks to the bigger picture that 46 percent of school-aged women said their breasts were a barrier to exercise.

Trying to find the solution for an ill-fitting sports bra often falls on the consumer—but Robyn founder Sara Falkson wants to change that.

Falkson grew up in a bustling home filled with love for sports and love for one another. In the middle with three older brothers and one younger sister, she tried almost every sport, but eventually settled on field hockey.

Playing sports while going through puberty is something all adolescents have to deal with, and Falkson was no exception. Navigating a changing body and mind can be tricky, especially when you’re relying on that body to remember plays and execute drills.

When it came time for the obligatory mall trip to shop for sports bras, Falkson says she found herself relying on her teammates.

“My teammates were really the best sisters of all,” she tells Women’s Health. “I found such a family and community in sports.”

Her teammates offered emotional support, but Falkson still struggled to find physical support.

“I have a bigger chest, and when it came to finding a sports bra, I struggled so deeply,” she says. “I would either find some that would be over-sexualizing, or I would chafe, and I would get horrible scars and stabs.”

A solution she and her mom came up with was for her to wear two sports bras while playing field hockey. Although this remedy did solve the support issue, Falkson says it came with another set of issues—teammates commented on it, and it wasn’t comfortable.

“Early on, I just assumed that my body’s probably the problem,” she says. “This gear design came from huge companies. They know what they’re doing, and I’m probably the one not fitting the mold.”

This prompted her to look toward other solutions, such as modifying her fueling regime to change her body, as well as the more permanent solution of a breast reduction.

These experiences stayed with Falkson, including through her time on the Dartmouth College field hockey team. She says that even in college, a lot of her teammates were still navigating the same issues of pressure and appearance on the field.

“We tried to do as much as possible to bring in the resources on the mental performance side and the nutrition side at Dartmouth and really switch the language [to] being more performance-focused, rather than appearance,” she says.

After earning her undergraduate degree, Falkson moved to San Francisco and began coaching youth field hockey on the side. She loved coaching, but soon heard familiar concerns from her players.

“The girls were saying the exact same things,” she says. “They were focused more on how they looked in their uniforms than how they were actually performing, and by that point, I’m like, ‘This is ridiculous. Why do so many of us feel this way?’”

Motivated by her frustration, Falkson enrolled at Harvard University to pursue a master’s degree in design and engineering. While there, a single stat stayed on her mind: 50 percent of girls drop out of sport by age 14 due to low body confidence, according to research conducted by Dove.

“It was so heartbreaking, but also in some ways validating knowing that there’s some research grounded in this, and we’re not alone,” she says.

While at Harvard, Falkson started leading body confidence workshops in the Boston area where she sat down with young female athletes to get a better understanding on what could be done to help them feel better about playing sports.

“We put the ownership in their hands, and that’s how we got Robyn,” she says of her company, an athletic brand dedicated to designing sports bras that elevate body confidence on and off the field. “Time after time, girls were so frustrated about sports bras, because they’re an essential piece of equipment, but so overlooked.”

Robyn started as a master’s thesis proposal. Falkson’s advisor, Karen Korellis Reuther, played a key role.

Korellis Reuther is extremely familiar with the world of design. Now a Design Critic in Architecture in Harvard’s Graduate School of Design, she is a designer by training and practice for over 40 years and is a former creative executive at Nike and Reebok.

Korellis Reuther’s passion for helping to redesign the world for women also was fueled by personal experience.

“As designers, we really connect very much to the built world, and that’s where Sara really had a passion,” Korellis Reuther says, “We were trying to understand what around the built world—whether it’s what we put on our bodies, what we use, or what we surround ourselves with—that is causing girls’ body confidence to drop, and what could we do from a design perspective?”

Korellis Reuther’s research in women-focused design goes far beyond sports. She is the author of Man-Made: How We Designed a World That Leaves Women Out, and How We Can Make It Right.

In the book, Korellis Reuther explores how women are still grossly unaccounted for in the world of design, despite inhabiting the same world as men. Her research examines how components of everyday life inconvenience women—and sometimes endanger them.

From poor locker room setups to unfit fire equipment gear for female firefighters and personal protection equipment, women are consistently thought of as an afterthought when it comes to design.

“We think, ‘I can’t make this work,’” she says. “But the truth is, it wasn’t designed for us in the first place.”

While working as a design executive for major brands, Korellis Reuther had a unique perspective being physically present where these decisions were made, but still not feeling like change was taking place. She speaks openly about feeling dismissed and looked over in important meetings, despite her high-ranking position in the company.

“The story had to be told, and as a designer, I knew we could make it right.”

Robyn is set to release a high-support bra, the Game Day, and a medium-support bra, the Everyday. The principal component that makes Robyn bras different from other athletic brands is the adjustability features. Almost all components of the bra will be adjustable, including the shoulder straps and bottom band.

In preliminary testing, subjects ran at 8 MPH pace while hooked up to machines to monitor their breast movement. The Robyn Bra demonstrated approximately 46 percent less vertical breast movement than a leading high-support sports bra.

Robyn encompasses Falkson, including the brand’s name. She and her mother share Robyn as a middle name. It proved to have a double meaning after Korellis Reuther pointed out that the robin is one of the only species of bird with a distinct breast that is a different color then the rest of the body.

“Just like the robin bird, we want our athlete to lead with color, joy, and strength,” Falkson says.

Robyn has certainly been led with strength thus far. With the product set to launch this spring, Robyn has already amassed over 34,000 followers on Instagram as well as more than 100 brand ambassadors comprised of high school and college athletes.

It has already been a part of five major collaborations, and was featured in Yankees Mag—another element that’s personal to Falkson, as her longtime boyfriend, Ben Rice, plays professional baseball for the New York Yankees.

The couple went to high school together and began dating early in college. Rice will make the occasional surprise appearance on Robyn’s social media, like when Falkson handmade a coffee table to celebrate his anniversary with the team. She also filmed a series in which she 3D printed custom Yankees memorabilia for this year’s playoff season.

Both athletes, Falkson says the two are able to push each other to be better, and seeing Rice’s career expand has made her more inspired about her own.

“I think if anything, seeing his career has just inspired me to also push to see my dreams and take risks,” she says.

Robyn isn’t just about apparel—it has its own mission, too. The company has hosted dozens of body confidence workshops, including a recent partnership with South Bronx United, a soccer nonprofit.. Robyn teamed up with South Bronx United as well as mental health organization The Hidden Opponent to create a four week-curriculum focused on empowering young athletes.

Robyn also works with Shooting Touch, a basketball nonprofit out of Boston for body confidence-focused programming. Falkson also coordinated a partnership with a 3D printing company to bring the printers into local schools to encourage more girls to get into STEM.

“For me personally, I’m glad our world has Saras, because we need to keep pushing forward,” Korellis Reuther says. “She pushes forward from her true, authentic self—with a sense of joy, with a sense of optimism, but also a sense of, ‘We haven’t done it right yet, and we need to make this right.’”

Looking toward the future, Falkson says her dream scenario would be to have Robyn gear become part of a female athlete’s uniform. “Once I see Robyn in the real world and girls connecting and bonding over a body confident sport and feeling their best, celebrating lifelong movement, that’s my goal.”

Through it all, Falkson has held on to the perspective she had as a young athlete.

“Growing up and being a woman is hard,” she says. “What you’re going through is okay and normal, and you’re still going to be valued. Giving girls the resources to have that space is really important.”

Alyssa Cruz is the social media editor at Women’s Health, where she drives social strategy and helps shape the brand’s voice across platforms. Prior to WH, she worked at CBS News and palabra. Originally from Toledo, she is a proud alumna of Ohio University, where she received two bachelor’s degrees in journalism and Spanish. When she is not creating social content, you can find her trying different restaurants in New York City or planning her next trip.

#5097. Visa adds Western Union’s USDPT stablecoin to its settlement network in Anchorage tie-up

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Visa and eligible partners can now settle VisaNet obligations using Western Union's USDPT, issued by Anchorage Digital Bank.

Anchorage Digital said it is collaborating with Visa to support select stablecoins issued by its federally chartered bank as native settlement assets on the Visa network, starting with Western Union's USDPT.

Visa and eligible partners can use the token to settle VisaNet obligations, with the companies also creating a framework to consider additional Anchorage-issued stablecoins, according to a statement shared with The Block.

"Stablecoins have made money move faster, but speed alone won't bring institutions onchain," Anchorage Digital CEO Nathan McCauley said. "They need a regulated institution behind the money. With Anchorage Digital select issued stablecoins now being supported on the Visa network, institutions get both: the speed of a stablecoin and the safety of a federally regulated bank behind it."

USDPT debuted in May on Solana, with Anchorage Digital Bank handling issuance and Fireblocks providing the wallet and settlement infrastructure. Western Union initially launched the stablecoin in the Philippines and Bolivia, with plans to use it for 24/7 settlements across its network of more than 200 countries.

Anchorage has also issued stablecoins for Tether (USAT), OSL Group (USDGO), Ethena Labs (USDtb), Falcon Finance (fUSD), and Zelle (ZLUSD).

Anchorage reached a valuation of more than $3 billion in its $350 million Series D funding round in 2021. It has spent this year expanding its institutional services, including onchain lending through Spark and custody-based collateral management.

Anchorage said the partnership with Visa fits its broader push to become a settlement bank for stablecoins, extending its business beyond issuance into custody and settlement services for institutions.

The partnership comes as stablecoin supply contracts. According to The Block's data dashboard, the current total supply has fallen to around $280 billion, down from $300 billion in March this year. Tether's USDT dominates the sector with $177 billion, followed by Circle's USDC at around $70 billion.

For Visa, the Anchorage partnership builds on an expanding stablecoin settlement network. The company said in September that its annualized stablecoin settlement run rate reached $20 billion, more than 15 times the year-earlier figure.

Visa has also added networks including Monad, Canton, Base, and Tempo to its settlement options.

#5095. DOJ Wins Court Authority to Seize $1.2 Billion of North Korea’s Stolen Binance Crypto as Freezes Top $2.7 Billion

The DOJ said Thursday it has court authority to seize or restrain more than $1.2 billion in cryptocurrency stolen from Binance, part of a freeze of more than $2.7 billion that officials said halted about half of North Korea’s October exchange heist.

In a Thursday announcement, the department said that on or about Oct. 29, members of the North Korean military, referred to by the FBI as TraderTraitor, stole more than $6 billion worth of virtual assets from Binance. That is below the $6.34 billion figure Binance has covered since the attack.

Binance said it had completed coverage of the assets hit in the breach, replacing the tokens from company funds rather than by recovering the stolen coins. Those are separate tracks: the DOJ is trying to interdict what was taken, while the exchange has said users were made whole from its Secure Asset Fund for Users, a portion of its futures insurance fund and its treasury.

Assistant Attorney General A. Tysen Duva of the DOJ’s Criminal Division said the department and its partners were able to “immediately halt about half of this historic breach” by freezing stolen assets “before they got beyond law enforcement reach.” The freeze of more than $2.7 billion is a joint figure for U.S. government and private-sector efforts. Of that amount, the DOJ said it has already obtained court authorization to seize or restrain more than $1.2 billion. The underlying court papers were not posted with the announcement.

The $1.2 billion is not newly recovered money but comes out of the roughly $2.85 billion Binance said on Nov. 3 had been frozen or intercepted, and Thursday’s court authorization is the legal step that lets the government move toward taking ownership of those funds through forfeiture, where until now they had simply been frozen at the token issuers.

Even that freeze is not the end of the trail. The DOJ said TraderTraitor actors are still moving the stolen assets through cryptocurrency bridges, mixers and exchanges, and that tracing, seizure and forfeiture work continues. Last week, on Oct. 31, the FBI, with the U.S. Secret Service and IRS Criminal Investigation, issued a Cyber Alert attributing the theft to North Korea and urging virtual asset service providers to block transactions with or derived from addresses the actors are using. The alert said investigators have seen the stolen assets converted primarily into ether.

Separately from the DOJ and Binance case, the U.S. attorney’s office in the Central District of California and the FBI announced the seizure of cloud-based infrastructure used in a social engineering campaign the FBI is calling FaceStealer. The FBI said the scheme has resulted in the theft of more than $400 million in cryptocurrency over the last 18 months and has also been used in attempts to compromise people who maintain open-source software, a step the bureau said appears calculated to enable supply-chain attacks.

The method is a fake video call. The FBI said North Korean hackers, known publicly under names including APT38 and BlueNoroff, lure targets with meeting invitations sent from accounts stolen from earlier victims. Once a target joins, the hackers play a prerecorded webcam video of a person the target knows, or a deepfake of a well-known figure in the cryptocurrency industry, then introduce a fake technical error and send a “fix” that installs malware. In several cases, the theft came months to more than a year after the first compromise, as the hackers waited for wallet balances to grow.

A Thursday FBI FLASH report said the meetings run on imposter versions of Zoom, Microsoft Teams and Google Meet, with invitations often moving through hijacked Telegram, LinkedIn, Slack or Discord accounts. Pursuant to a federal warrant, the FBI seized more than 900 webcam videos of FaceStealer victims. The bureau has posted a hub for the campaign at fbi.gov/facestealer.

Principal Deputy Assistant Attorney General for National Security M. Scott Proctor tied both Thursday’s actions to the same charge: “North Korea’s government bankrolls its rogue regime and illicit weapons program through cryptocurrency theft.”

The Binance recovery is still being run by the FBI’s Los Angeles Field Office, Cyber Division and Virtual Asset Unit, with the Secret Service and IRS-CI. The DOJ did not say how much of the more than $2.7 billion freeze, or of the $1.2 billion under court authority, is bitcoin.

#5090. Tether Gold Gets Clean KPMG Audit Confirming $2.2B in Physical Gold Backing

Tether Gold Gets Clean KPMG Audit Confirming $2.2B in Physical Gold Backing

Tether's gold-backed stablecoin, Tether Gold (XAUT), has completed its first full audit by KPMG, which found the token fully backed by physical gold, the company announced on Thursday.

KPMG issued an unqualified opinion on the financial statements of TG Commodities, which issues XAUT, for the year ended December 31, 2025. An unqualified, or "clean," opinion means the auditor found that the statements fairly present the company's financial position, without reservations.

Tether Gold (XAU₮) Becomes Tether’s Second Stablecoin to Complete a Big Four AuditLearn more: https://t.co/eO0asUwvlI

As of the end of 2025, TG Commodities held 520,089.350 troy ounces of allocated gold, valued at $2.246 billion, against 520,089.300 XAUT tokens in issue, according to Tether. The gold is held in vaults in Switzerland, and each token represents an ownership interest in gold on specific bars.

As part of the audit, KPMG physically counted and inspected every gold bar backing the token, rather than relying only on confirmations from custodians, Tether said.

"KPMG counted every single bar as part of its Tether Gold audit," Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino said, adding that he believes XAUT "is on track to become the first true competitor to the gold ETF market."

The XAUT audit is Tether's second by a Big Four firm. In August, KPMG issued a clean opinion on Tether International, the issuer of its flagship USDT stablecoin, in what the company called the largest inaugural audit in history, Decrypt reported at the time.

For years, Tether faced criticism for relying on quarterly attestations, which offer a snapshot of reserves at a single point in time, rather than a full audit of its finances. The company first said earlier this year that it was being audited by a Big Four firm without naming it, before confirming KPMG.

Tether still publishes quarterly attestations for USDT. Its latest, prepared by accounting firm BDO and covering the period to September 30, showed total reserve assets of $191.5 billion against $183.5 billion in liabilities, giving it an excess reserve buffer of almost $8 billion, according to the report. Tether said it made $1.5 billion in net operating profit in the third quarter.

"These milestones advance the progress towards audits across the whole of Tether," said chief financial officer Simon McWilliams.

Separately from the gold backing XAUT, Tether has also built up its own large gold reserves, and Ardoino has said it will keep adding to its stash, which it stores in a former nuclear bunker in Switzerland.

#5087. Digital Credit: Comparing The Six Bitcoin Preferred Stocks

A simple four-part investor framework for evaluating Digital Credit.

Digital credit is an emerging asset class that transforms corporate Bitcoin balance sheets into income producing securities. Strategy pioneered the market with five perpetual preferred equity instruments: STRK, STRF, STRD, STRC, and STRE (the first four are listed in the U.S.). Strive subsequently introduced SATA and SATS, bringing the category to six U.S. instruments across two issuers.

These securities offer investors preferred equity (read: credit-like) exposure to companies whose balance sheets are dominated by Bitcoin. Their perpetual structure means investors principally rely on recurring dividends and the continued financial strength of the issuer. Unlike term debt, there is no maturation date at which the full principal is ever paid back.

Evaluating digital credit requires examining four characteristics: creditworthiness, investor protections, liquidity, and dividend profile.

This article explains all four dimensions and then offers a ranking for the six digital credit instruments along these lines. None of this should be taken as investment advice—it is only a systematic framework for analyzing digital credit.

Creditworthiness measures an issuer’s ability to support its financial obligations.

Traditional credit analysis focuses on earnings, free cash flow, and refinancing capacity. Companies with strong cash generation can service interest and repay maturing debt. Digital credit issuers today derive financial strength primarily from their Bitcoin reserves, placing greater emphasis on net asset coverage. In the future we could certainly see digital credit issuers that also have significant cash flows on top of Bitcoin holdings, which would add to creditworthiness.

The central question is how much Bitcoin supports each dollar of credit obligations. One useful measure is the BTC Rating, which compares the dollar value of Bitcoin reserves with the outstanding credit, accounting for the seniority of each credit instrument. Cash reserves can further strengthen creditworthiness by reducing net obligations.

For example, a 5x BTC Rating indicates five dollars of Bitcoin supporting every dollar of notional credit claims. Higher ratings provide greater protection against Bitcoin price declines.

Seniority matters because instruments from the same issuer can have different claims on the same asset base. A senior preferred instrument benefits from greater coverage than a junior preferred instrument: all senior claims are included in the BTC Rating calculation of a junior instrument.

Creditworthiness also depends on corporate governance and capital allocation. Management must preserve sufficient Bitcoin coverage while funding recurring dividends. Issuing additional credit increases dividend obligations, while assets accumulation (Bitcoin or cash) strengthen the asset base. Excessive leverage, poor treasury management, or sustained Bitcoin declines can weaken coverage over time.

Investors must therefore evaluate both their assumptions about Bitcoin’s long term performance and management’s ability to maintain a sustainable capital structure.

Investor protections determine what happens when an issuer fails to pay its promised dividends.

While creditworthiness concerns an issuer’s ability to pay, investor protections govern the consequences of missed payments, and more specifically the compensation investors may receive for delayed income.

The first distinction is cumulative versus noncumulative dividends. Cumulative dividends continue accumulating when payments are skipped, preserving the investor’s claim to unpaid distributions. Noncumulative dividends permit the issuer to permanently forgo a payment.

The second distinction is the treatment of unpaid dividends. Some instruments preserve unpaid amounts without additional compensation. Others compound arrears at the regular dividend rate. The strongest protections impose penalty rates, which cause unpaid dividends to compound at progressively higher rates as deferrals continue.

Penalty rates create financial incentives for management to maintain regular payments. They also compensate investors for the additional risk and delay associated with missed distributions.

Other investor protections may include restrictions on payments to junior shareholders, preferred shareholder voting rights or director elections, and dedicated dividend reserves.

These provisions establish the contractual guardrails surrounding dividend payments. Stronger protections increase the cost of deferral and improve the investor’s position to receive income.

Liquidity measures how easily investors can enter and exit positions without materially affecting market prices. This is especially important for digital credit, which does not mature and pay back principal on a fixed date. A liquid market is how an investor would “get his money back” if he wants to exit his position.

Digital credit has developed substantial secondary market liquidity, particularly in its leading instruments. The six U.S.-listed digital credit tickers are among the most actively traded preferred equity securities in the U.S. markets.

Two metrics help investors evaluate liquidity.

Absolute trading volume measures the total dollar value traded over a given period. Greater volume accommodates larger transactions and makes it easier for institutions to establish or liquidate positions.

Relative liquidity, or turnover, measures trading volume as a percentage of an instrument’s market capitalization. It captures how actively a security trades relative to its outstanding size.

For example, an instrument with $50 million in daily volume and a $1 billion market capitalization has 5% daily turnover. Another instrument trading $100 million daily with a $10 billion market capitalization has only 1% turnover. The second instrument offers greater absolute trading capacity, while the first experiences substantially greater trading activity relative to its size.

Absolute volume facilitates large transactions, while relative turnover reveals the level of trading activity relative to the capital outstanding.

Dividend profile describes the characteristics of the income an investor receives. Three main considerations are the dividend rate policy, payment frequency, and dividend yield.

The first distinction is between fixed and variable dividends. Fixed dividends establish a predetermined annual payment per share. Investors know the nominal income they are entitled to receive, allowing them to plan around a predictable cash flow stream. Variable dividends allow issuers to adjust the payout rate over time. These adjustments can respond to market interest rates, investor demand, or the trading price of the instrument. Fixed dividends prioritize income predictability for investors with reduced flexibility for issuers. Variable dividends provide flexibility for issuers but less predictability for investors.

The second consideration is payment frequency. Traditional preferred securities generally pay quarterly. Digital credit has introduced monthly, twice monthly, and daily dividend schedules. More frequent payments improve cash flow availability, accelerate reinvestment opportunities, and reduce the concentration of distributions around individual payment dates. After January 1 2027, all six instruments will be doing daily dividends.

The third consideration, and perhaps the most important out of anything mentioned in this article, is the raw dividend yield. This is the annualized income received divided by the trading price of the credit. A higher yield is generally preferred, but it also must be contextualized against risks and other considerations.

The following framework ranks the six instruments relative to one another. Rank 1 represents the strongest position and rank 6 the weakest, with ties where instruments share identical characteristics.

Creditworthiness and investor protections receive overall rankings. Liquidity is segmented into absolute trading volume and turnover. Dividend profile is segmented into rate certainty and yield. While each category’s segments can be evaluated together as a composite metric based on the investor’s own needs, I am choosing to segment them for more precision. I have also thrown out payment frequency since all six will be daily in less than two months.

For dividend rate certainty, fixed dividends rank ahead of variable dividends because their nominal payments are predetermined. This ranking specifically measures income predictability.

Equal signs indicate tied rankings. Liquidity rankings are based on average daily trading volumes from November 2–11, 2026, and turnover is calculated using the latest reported shares outstanding.

Creditworthiness: Strategy’s preferreds rank ahead of Strive’s because of their greater asset coverage. Within each issuer, seniority determines the ranking. STRF has the highest BTC Rating because it is Strategy’s most senior preferred, while SATS has the lowest coverage because it ranks below SATA in Strive’s capital structure.

Investor protections: SATA and STRF rank first and second because unpaid dividends compound at escalating penalty rates and holders can elect directors following prolonged dividend deferrals. SATA’s higher penalty rate ceiling and dividend reserve give it the edge. STRC and STRK both compound unpaid dividends at the regular dividend rate. I rank STRC ahead of STRK based on its higher current compounding rate, although STRK also gives holders the right to elect directors after prolonged dividend deferrals. SATS has cumulative dividends, but unpaid dividends do not compound. STRD ranks last because its dividends are noncumulative, meaning any skipped dividends are permanently forgone. Investors also have no right to bring on directors.

Of the six, STRC and SATA are variable rate instruments and the remaining four are fixed rate. This makes the dividend rate policy pretty straightforward.

The remaining columns—volume, turnover, and yield—are trackable numbers. For instance, here are turnover and volume numbers from the BFC Digital Credit Dashboard:

Each of the six digital credit instruments has a different set of tradeoffs. Investors who prioritize creditworthiness may prefer STRF, while those who prioritize dividend certainty and yield may prefer SATS.

The relative importance of each of these dimensions depends on the investor’s own needs. As digital credit continues to develop, investors can use this framework to evaluate new instruments and determine which ones are best suited to their objectives.

#5084. Anchorage Digital’s Stablecoins Can Now Settle Payments on Visa’s Network

Anchorage Digital’s Stablecoins Can Now Settle Payments on Visa’s Network

Anchorage Digital is extending its stablecoin reach into one of the world’s largest payment networks, teaming up with Visa to let select Anchorage-issued tokens settle transactions directly on VisaNet, starting with Western Union’s USDPT.

Under the arrangement announced Thursday, Visa and eligible companies can now use USDPT, a dollar-pegged stablecoin issued and custodied by Anchorage Digital Bank, to settle their obligations on the Visa network.

It’s the first Anchorage-issued token to gain that status, and the companies said the deal establishes a framework to add more of its stablecoins later.

The appeal, Anchorage argues, is pairing the speed of a stablecoin with the backing of a regulated bank. Anchorage operates what it bills as America’s first federally chartered crypto bank, overseen by the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency.

“Speed alone won’t bring institutions onchain,” said Nathan McCauley, Anchorage’s co-founder and CEO, arguing that institutions want “a regulated institution behind the money,” now getting both a stablecoin’s speed and a federally regulated bank’s safeguards.

The move is the latest sign of Visa weaving stablecoins into its settlement plumbing.

The payments giant has built out a stablecoin settlement program spanning networks like Base, Polygon and others, launched a platform to help banks and fintech firms issue and move stablecoins, and has moved into onchain lending tied to its cards.

Dan Roesbery, a Visa vice president for crypto partnerships, framed the USDPT support as giving partners “another trusted, institution-grade option” for settling with Visa.

For Anchorage, the deal advances a broader ambition to become the settlement bank for stablecoins at institutional scale, not just issuing them but providing the custody and settlement rails underneath.

The bank already issues a roster of institution-grade stablecoins, including Tether’s U.S.-regulated USAT, Western Union’s USDPT, Ethena’s USDtb and others, each redeemable one-to-one for dollars. Its counterparty settlement network, Atlas, handles off-exchange settlement, collateral management and cross-border transfers.

Anchorage also recently positioned itself as the custody backbone of the U.S.-Mexico stablecoin corridor, custodying tokens on both sides of one of the world’s largest remittance routes. Backing a token on Visa’s network, which reaches merchants and banks worldwide, extends that institutional footprint further into mainstream payments.

The companies said support for additional Anchorage-issued stablecoins could follow the initial USDPT launch.

#5081. Bitcoin Price Briefly Crashes to $67,500, Lowest Since August, as Dollar Holds 19-Month High

Bitcoin price traded near $69,800 on Friday morning after briefly crashing to about $67,555 around midnight Eastern, its lowest level since mid-August, according to Bitcoin Magazine Pro data. The rebound held after October producer prices rose less than economists had expected, though the U.S. dollar remained near a 19-month high and traders still fully priced a December Federal Reserve rate hike.

Bitcoin price was trading near $72,800 at this time Thursday. It slipped toward $71,000 by Thursday evening, then gave way late at night, breaking below $70,000 and sliding to that midnight low. The move also pushed the bitcoin price below its 200-week moving average, a long-term trend line near $68,000. Buyers lifted it back above $69,000 before sunrise, and the rebound extended toward $69,800 after the producer-price report.

The U.S. Dollar Index held above 103 Friday after a three-day rally took it to its highest level since April 2025. The 10-year Treasury yield was near 5.15% ahead of the data, and two-year yields, which track Fed expectations most closely, were on pace for their worst week since 2024, Bloomberg reported.

Traders still see an 88% chance of a quarter-point hike at the Fed’s Dec. 8-9 meeting and a 12% chance of a half-point move, CME FedWatch data show. Fed Governor Christopher Waller has added a 50-basis-point increase to the discussion, and New York Fed President John Williams said Thursday that inflation remains high and policymakers need to take further action.

Higher rates raise the cost of holding an asset that pays no interest, and a stronger dollar tends to drain demand for risk assets. Both have pressured the bitcoin price since this week’s hotter-than-expected consumer price index.

Bitcoin has also broken from tech stocks. It fell 5.9% between Nov. 5 and Nov. 12 while the Nasdaq 100 gained 6.4%, a sign it is trading more on yields and the dollar than on the AI trade. Nasdaq 100 futures were up about 0.6% Friday after the producer-price report, as investors also weighed Nvidia’s announcement that Jensen Huang had resigned as CEO.

The break below $70,000 set off a wave of forced selling. About $1.64 billion in crypto positions were liquidated over 24 hours, with bets on higher prices making up nearly 89% of the total and bitcoin positions accounting for $916 million.

U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs added to the pressure. The funds posted about $47.7 million in net outflows Thursday, their sixth straight day of withdrawals, bringing the run to roughly $1.49 billion. BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) alone bled about $2 billion over nine sessions through Wednesday, and the group has now flipped to about $1.56 billion in net outflows for 2026.

Sentiment has soured with it. A widely watched crypto fear and greed gauge dropped to 20 Friday, its lowest reading since July 8, and bitcoin is now on track for a second straight down year.

Positioning has turned defensive. Average perpetual futures funding rates turned negative for the first time in a month as the price fell toward $68,500, meaning short sellers are now paying to hold their bets. Order books are also thin. Bitcoin order-book depth across major exchanges fell to $72 million on Nov. 9, roughly 33% below normal levels. “Liquidity remains thin while leverage stays elevated, leaving the market vulnerable to sharp moves in either direction,” said Tanay Ved, a senior research associate at Talos.

The Labor Department said Friday that the Producer Price Index for final demand rose 0.3% in October, short of the 0.4% monthly increase economists had expected, after a revised 1.4% advance in September. Core prices, which exclude food and energy, increased 0.3%, matching forecasts. On an unadjusted basis, the headline index was up 6.5% from a year earlier, the largest 12-month rise since November 2022. The slightly cooler report took some pressure off the dollar, which still held above 103, but a December rate hike remains fully priced, leaving limited relief for the bitcoin price heading into the weekend.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $69,800.

#5080. NYDFS sets fresh advertising standards for crypto firms

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The NYDFS issued the guidance, outlining its expectations for "accurate content, clear disclosures, and an established governance and review process."

New York’s top financial regulator issued fresh guidance outlining expectations for cryptocurrency firms to establish policies and procedures governing advertising and marketing, including measures to prevent false or misleading content, by next year.

On Thursday, the New York State Department of Financial Services issued the guidance, outlining its expectations for "accurate content, clear disclosures, and an established governance and review process," with firms expected to implement the measures by May 10, 2027.

"For more than a decade, DFS has set the global standard for virtual currency oversight and consumer protection," said Department of Financial Services Acting Superintendent Kaitlin Asrow in the statement. "Today’s guidance reinforces our commitment to protecting New Yorkers and ensures advertising and marketing practices in the virtual currency space are clear, comprehensive, and accessible."

NYDFS has long been a leading regulator of the crypto industry, launching its landmark BitLicense regime in 2015 and helping shape the broader regulatory approach to digital assets in the U.S. Other regulators globally have also turned their attention to advertisements, including in the UK.

The NY regulator said virtual currency entities' advertising should not be deceptive, should not include any exaggerated claims or say that something has no fee trading while omitting other costs. The entities also need to file disclosures on if people making testimonials were compensated, the regulator said.

Entities also need to have a governance and review structure that includes clear roles and responsibilities for advertising and marketing, the regulator added.

#5079. Tron, Tether and Chainalysis Form a New Crypto-Crime Task Force—Replacing One That Just Collapsed

Tron, Tether and Chainalysis Form a New Crypto-Crime Task Force—Replacing One That Just Collapsed

Tron, Tether, and blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis have launched a joint effort to hunt down and disrupt criminal activity involving the USDT stablecoin, just weeks after a nearly identical alliance, with a different analytics partner, quietly fell apart.

Announced Wednesday, the Digital Crime Task Force aims to identify illicit activity involving Tether’s USDT on the Tron blockchain and surface it for law enforcement before criminals can move their funds.

The pitch is a shift from reactive to proactive: rather than waiting for a victim to report a crime or an investigator to flag tainted funds, the group says it will proactively trace criminal networks and coordinate asset freezes, drawing on Chainalysis’s intelligence and AI-powered investigative tools. It will also accept tips and freeze requests from police.

“Criminals are using AI to move faster than ever, and waiting for a victim to report a crime is no longer good enough,” said Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino, noting the company has frozen more than $7 billion tied to illicit activity while working with over 360 law enforcement agencies across 73 countries.

Tron founder Justin Sun framed it as protecting the millions who rely on USDT, which circulates on Tron to the tune of more than $94 billion.

The notable wrinkle is what this new alliance replaces. Tron and Tether previously ran a similar operation, the T3 Financial Crime Unit, formed in 2024 with rival analytics firm TRM Labs and later joined by Binance. Together, the group froze more than $450 million in tainted assets.

But Bloomberg reported earlier this month that T3 was quietly disbanded in late August, with people familiar citing slow response times and a lack of coordination among its members. Its collapse went public just days before the record $6.4 billion Binance hack.

Swapping TRM for Chainalysis is a pointed move, given the two are fierce competitors. Chainalysis has even sued the U.S. government over an ICE contract awarded to TRM without competitive bidding, underscoring the rivalry now running through crypto’s crime-fighting infrastructure.

The new task force says it will target a broad sweep of threats, from romance and investment scams to stolen funds, terrorist financing, North Korean cyber operations, cartel money laundering and child-exploitation material.

The group also framed the effort around a coming era of autonomous AI agents transacting onchain, which it warned will open fresh avenues for criminal exploitation.

#5075. Avalanche Owns Five Percent of Tokenized Stocks

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Sign up to get 2 days early access to each edition of Tokenization Briefing

Over this past week, three U.S. tokenized stock venues picked three different blockchains to launch on. The WSJ reported Monday that NYSE chose Avalanche as the sole settlement chain for its planned 24/7 platform. On November 4, $CNTN became the first U.S. equity to trade under the SEC's Innovation Exemption. Five North sold tokenized $CNTN shares to DRW Cumberland on TSV, a permissioned venue on the Canton Network. On Monday, Formation notified the SEC that it will run a venue on Solana, targeting early 2027.

Given the strict regulations surrounding tokenized stocks in the U.S., no venue is going multichain at launch. Besides increasing complexity for regulatory compliance, fracturing activity across different networks would make it difficult to build deep liquidity while staying under the SEC's strict 0.25% average daily volume cap per asset.

The leading chains by tokenized stock volume have benefited from large exchanges launching their own tokenized stock products and leveraging their existing user bases.

The chains that successfully gain market share will be the ones that attract the biggest platforms and issuers.

Tokenized stocks have matched the growth of stablecoins closely. 240 days after crossing $1 billion, stablecoins stood at roughly $4 billion and tokenized stocks stood at $3.7 billion, while tokenized Treasuries were at $2.5 billion at the same point and have since fallen well behind. Although tokenized stocks may not continue at this growth rate, the trend is promising.

The count of tokenized stocks and ETFs worth more than $1 million climbed from three in Jan 2025 to 287 as of Nov 8, across roughly 4,400 tokenized assets and a $3.7 billion market.

Distribution keeps broadening too. HTX launched spot trading in bStocks on Monday with 20 names including NVDA, TSLA, QQQ, and CRCL. Strategy and SpaceX are now the most tokenized issuers by asset count. Our take is that breadth is the healthier signal for a young market, because a long tail of products with real size offers a better user experience than one or two large wrappers.
